Author Correction: Cretaceous bird from Brazil informs the evolution of the avian skull and brain

Luis M. Chiappe, Guillermo Navalón, Agustín G. Martinelli et al.. Author Correction: Cretaceous bird from Brazil informs the evolution of the avian skull and brain. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper is an author correction notice addressing an error in a previously published study on a Cretaceous bird from Brazil and avian skull and brain evolution. The original analysis of the enantiornithine bird *Navaornis hestiae* contained misplaced anatomical landmarks on its endocranial model, caused by a sharp corner in the three-dimensional reconstruction that displaced semilandmarks to an incorrect surface. The authors corrected the landmark positions using updated software and reanalyzed the dataset, producing a revised principal components analysis of endocranial shape variation. The core finding that *Navaornis* occupies an intermediate morphological position between stem birds and crown birds along the primary axis of shape variation is unchanged, though the corrected data now place *Navaornis* within crown bird variation along a secondary axis rather than just outside it. Additional shape distance analyses confirm that *Navaornis* shares endocranial similarities with a mix of stem and crown bird taxa, reinforcing the original interpretation of its mosaic of plesiomorphic and crown bird-like brain features.

Why it is getting attention: This work is drawing attention because it concerns a Cretaceous-era bird fossil from Brazil that provides new insights into how the avian skull and brain evolved, representing a significant paleontological find relevant to understanding early bird evolution.

Temporal uncoupling of radial glia lineage progression in cortical organoids

Melissa Stouffer, Osvaldo A. Miranda, Florian M. Pauler et al.. Temporal uncoupling of radial glia lineage progression in cortical organoids. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper investigated how radial glial progenitors progress through their lineage to generate the full diversity of excitatory cortical neurons, comparing this process between living animals and laboratory-grown brain organoids. The researchers adapted a mosaic genetic labeling technique, originally developed for in vivo use, to work in mouse embryonic stem cells, enabling precise clonal tracking of progenitor cells within self-organizing cortical organoids. They found that, unlike the strictly timed and stereotyped lineage progression observed in the developing cortex in vivo, radial glial progenitors in organoids display a high degree of plasticity in their proliferative behavior and show increased lineage restriction, resulting in diminished cell-type diversity within individual clones. Notably, these differences in clonal output occurred despite organoid progenitors displaying transcriptional profiles and lineage trajectories indistinguishable from their in vivo counterparts at the single-cell level. The authors conclude that non-cell-autonomous signals absent from self-organizing systems, likely provided by the native stem cell niche, are essential for the faithful temporal coordination of progenitor lineage progression and the generation of diverse cortical neuron subtypes within a clone.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it reveals that cortical organoids fail to faithfully recapitulate the timed progression of radial glia progenitor lineages, producing reduced clonal neuronal diversity due to the absence of external niche signals that normally regulate this process. The findings highlight a fundamental limitation of organoid models and point to non-cell-autonomous cues as essential regulators of cortical development.

Cascading continental-scale floods across Europe in 1342–1343

Andrea Kiss, Alberto Viglione, Mariano Barriendos et al.. Cascading continental-scale floods across Europe in 1342–1343. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper investigated the characteristics and causes of extreme historical flooding across Europe during the early fourteenth century, a period previously understood only partially. Using a new documentary dataset, the researchers identified sixteen major flood events occurring across much of Europe between late 1341 and 1343, four of which had return periods of five hundred to one thousand years. Rather than the previously recognized single extreme event, the Magdalena Flood of 1342, this work reveals that event was part of a prolonged and cascading sequence that made 1342 the year with the greatest number of extreme floods in the past seven centuries. The flood sequence carried substantial socio-economic consequences, including a paradigm shift in European flood mitigation practices. Volcanic eruptions and multi-annual Arctic sea ice retreat are proposed as plausible drivers of this unusual clustering, and the findings argue that risk management frameworks must account for the possibility of cascading extreme flood events occurring within short timeframes.

Why it is getting attention: This work is drawing attention because it reconstructs a sequence of massive medieval floods that cascaded across an entire continent, revealing that modern flood management systems are not equipped to handle such chained extreme events. The findings underscore an urgent gap in current risk strategies by demonstrating that unusually clustered, large-scale floods are a real historical—and therefore future—possibility.

Rb-driven transcription limits its tumour-suppressive effects in breast cancer

April C. Watt, Antonio Ahn, Catherine Blyth et al.. Rb-driven transcription limits its tumour-suppressive effects in breast cancer. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

The retinoblastoma protein Rb is a well-established tumour suppressor that represses E2F transcription factors to halt cell cycle progression, and CDK4/6 inhibitors exploit this function in hormone receptor-positive breast cancer by keeping Rb in its active, hypophosphorylated state. This study investigated how pharmacologically activated Rb reshapes chromatin and gene expression beyond simply arresting the cell cycle. Using breast cancer cell lines, patient-derived xenografts, and clinical samples, the researchers found that CDK4/6 inhibition drives Rb to redistribute across the genome, where it not only represses cell cycle gene promoters as expected but also integrates into oestrogen receptor transcriptional hubs to unexpectedly enhance oestrogen-responsive gene expression in an Rb-dependent manner, a process partly mediated through interaction with the chromatin regulator KDM5A. Many of the genes activated through this Rb-driven programme are pro-proliferative, and while anti-oestrogen therapy can neutralise this effect in endocrine-sensitive tumours—explaining the known synergy between CDK4/6 inhibitors and endocrine therapy—the programme persists in endocrine-resistant contexts such as those harbouring ESR1 mutations, limiting therapeutic efficacy. These findings recast Rb as a dual-function transcriptional regulator whose tumour-suppressive cell cycle arrest activity is partially counteracted by its own capacity to activate pro-proliferative transcriptional programmes.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it reveals an unexpected self-limiting mechanism in a widely used cancer therapy: when CDK4/6 inhibitors activate Rb, the protein simultaneously drives transcription of oestrogen-responsive genes that undermine the very cell-cycle arrest the treatment is meant to sustain.

Procognitive restoration of PV neuron plasticity in neurodevelopmental disorders

Yu‐Tzu Shih, Jason Bondoc Alipio, Zin‐Juan Klaft et al.. Procognitive restoration of PV neuron plasticity in neurodevelopmental disorders. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper investigated how parvalbumin-expressing inhibitory interneurons (PV INs) in the hippocampus regulate memory formation and how their dysfunction contributes to neurodevelopmental disorders such as autism spectrum disorder, epilepsy, and schizophrenia. The researchers designed an input-specific translatome screen to identify genes that regulate experience-dependent PV IN plasticity in the CA3/CA2 hippocampal subregion, finding that many of these genes are haploinsufficient in neurodevelopmental disorders, suggesting a shared mechanistic vulnerability. As a proof-of-concept, they targeted upregulation of one candidate gene, the homeobox transcription factor Meis2, specifically in CA3/CA2 PV INs of an adult neurodevelopmental disorder mouse model. This targeted intervention was sufficient to restore experience-dependent PV IN plasticity, improve hippocampal network properties including sharp-wave ripple activity, enhance cognition, and suppress seizures. The findings establish experience-dependent PV IN plasticity as a convergent mechanism across neurodevelopmental disorder risk genes and demonstrate that developmental circuit deficits can be reversed through targeted molecular intervention in adulthood.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it identifies a molecular target in a specific interneuron population whose manipulation can simultaneously rescue multiple debilitating features of neurodevelopmental disorders—including memory deficits, disrupted network function, and seizures—in an adult animal model, suggesting a potential therapeutic avenue even after development is complete.

A gas-enshrouded and gas-reddened black hole at cosmic dawn

Rohan P. Naidu, Jorryt Matthee, Harley Katz et al.. A gas-enshrouded and gas-reddened black hole at cosmic dawn. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper investigates the formation mechanisms of supermassive black holes in the very early universe, a longstanding theoretical puzzle. Using observations of a source existing only around 660 million years after the Big Bang, the researchers identified extraordinary spectral features including an exceptionally large hydrogen Balmer break, broad multi-peaked emission, and Balmer line absorption. They model the source as a supermassive black hole surrounded by an extremely dense, turbulent, dust-free gas envelope, which accounts for the unusual spectral signatures. This configuration is consistent with theoretical models proposing that dense gas cocoons enable rapid black hole growth through super-Eddington accretion, potentially explaining how billion-solar-mass black holes emerged so early in cosmic history. Critically, the findings suggest that the redness of such objects, including the recently discovered class known as "little red dots," is caused by gas rather than dust, and that scattering rather than kinematics drives their complex spectral line shapes, implying that black hole mass estimates for these sources may be overestimated by orders of magnitude.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it offers direct observational evidence for how supermassive black holes grew so rapidly in the early universe—by accreting mass while shrouded in gas. The findings also suggest that this enshrouded state may explain the mysterious "little red dots" seen at cosmic dawn.

Towards an equitable future of global photovoltaic waste recycling

Chen Wang, Jian Zuo, Xinyu Chen et al.. Towards an equitable future of global photovoltaic waste recycling. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

The study examined the growing global crisis of photovoltaic waste, assessing how different recycling strategies—local versus outsourced—compare in terms of economic and climate outcomes across diverse regions and technologies over time. The researchers developed a comprehensive analytical framework that accounts for supply-side material constraints, regional heterogeneity, and anticipated technological progress. They found that global photovoltaic waste will accumulate to hundreds of millions of tonnes by mid-century, with middle-income regions becoming dominant contributors after roughly two decades, and that the break-even point for economically viable recycling remains more than a decade away. A combination of region-specific and outsourced recycling strategies was found to maximize global net benefits, substantially reducing greenhouse gas emissions and generating large cumulative economic returns, though outsourced recycling risks deepening inequality for low-income regions. The authors conclude that a declining-subsidy scheme alongside international cooperation focused on technology transfer and recycling capacity building in low-income regions represents the most equitable and scalable path toward photovoltaic waste circularity.

Why it is getting attention: The work draws attention by quantifying the massive scale of photovoltaic waste expected by mid-century and arguing that without regionally tailored recycling approaches and international collaboration, the burden of managing that waste will fall inequitably across the globe.

Luminescent-reaction-enabled super-resolution imaging

Wenxin Zhu, Chi Zhang, Jiahui Gui et al.. Luminescent-reaction-enabled super-resolution imaging. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper developed a new super-resolution microscopy framework that bypasses the need for external laser or light excitation by instead exploiting reaction-excited luminescence, including electrochemiluminescence, chemiluminescence, and bioluminescence. The central challenge addressed was that conventional reaction-based luminescent imaging is constrained to poor spatiotemporal resolution due to limited photon output. The authors created a combined experimental and computational approach called RIED, which uses a spatiotemporal recording strategy paired with entropy-weighted correlation and deconvolution to reconstruct super-resolution images from the sparse photon statistics characteristic of reaction-driven emission. Applying this framework, they achieved approximately one-hundred nanometer resolution imaging of intracellular organelles across all three luminescence modalities, demonstrated highly sensitive detection of surface proteins, and performed ultralong-term continuous live-cell super-resolution imaging of mitochondrial transfer dynamics spanning over forty hours. The work establishes a laser-free class of super-resolution microscopy with expanded potential for biological imaging where light excitation is problematic.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it introduces a chemistry-driven super-resolution imaging approach that simultaneously achieves three-dimensional, laser-free, highly sensitive, and ultralong-term live-cell imaging — capabilities that collectively overcome the spatiotemporal resolution barriers that have constrained existing reaction-based imaging methods.

Degree-of-polarization modulation for high-dimensional optical computing

Claudio Conti, Claudio Conti, Davide Pierangeli. Degree-of-polarization modulation for high-dimensional optical computing. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper investigates the spatial modulation of the degree of polarization as a new controllable degree of freedom for optical information processing. The researchers engineered polarization statistics at the micrometre scale using a phase-only spatial light modulator, enabling fully programmable control over both the state of polarization and the degree of polarization across more than a thousand spatial modes. By mapping the red–green–blue colour space onto the full volume of the Poincaré sphere, they encoded colour images into a single-wavelength laser beam, a concept they term "polarization colours." This expanded dimensionality was applied to demonstrate fully parallel photonic classification of colour images using a high-dimensional photonic neural network and high-security multidimensional optical encryption. The work establishes degree-of-polarization modulation as a practical resource for advancing optical computing, photonic information processing, and cryptography.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it demonstrates that engineering light statistics at the micrometre scale allows both the state and degree of polarization to be spatially programmed simultaneously, opening a new high-dimensional space for encoding and processing optical information.

Neural basis of compositional control

Assia Chericoni, Justin M. Fine, TAHA S. ISMAIL et al.. Neural basis of compositional control. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

Abstract unavailable. Open the link to read more.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it identifies distinct roles for specific brain regions in how humans flexibly combine and switch between goal-directed behavioral strategies. The findings challenge simpler accounts of decision-making by showing that value signals contextualize rather than continuously update control policies, with the hippocampus, anterior cingulate cortex, and orbitofrontal cortex each contributing differently to this compositional process.

Structural mechanism governing the directionality of bridge recombination

Masahiro Hiraizumi, Januka S. Athukoralage, Nicholas T. Perry et al.. Structural mechanism governing the directionality of bridge recombination. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

Bridge recombinases from the IS110 transposon family use a bridge RNA to direct programmable recombination between donor and target DNA sequences, but the mechanism by which these elements excise from host genomes to form the circular intermediates needed for transposition had not been characterized. This study investigated the excision reaction of the IS621 recombinase–bridge RNA complex, finding that bridge RNA is weakly expressed from genomic loci and that excision proceeds less efficiently than insertion. Using cryo-electron microscopy, the researchers solved structures of the recombinase–bridge RNA complex bound to excision DNA substrates and compared these to previously determined insertion complex structures. While the insertion complex bends donor and target DNAs into a U-shaped configuration, the excision substrates adopt linear conformations that bind across both bridge RNA loops to form an X-shaped architecture, a geometry that reduces top-strand exchange efficiency and explains the natural bias toward insertion. The study also identifies specific dinucleotide interactions called handshake guides that similarly modulate both reactions, together providing a structural framework for the full IS110 transposition cycle and informing the rational design of programmable gene-editing tools.

Why it is getting attention: The work is attracting attention because cryo-electron microscopy structures reveal the precise structural basis by which a bridge recombinase controls the directional preference for DNA insertion over excision. This mechanistic insight is considered significant for its potential to guide the rational design of programmable gene-editing tools based on bridge recombination.

Species intraspecific variation drives tropical forest drought resistance

Chris Smith-Martin, Robert Muscarella, Timothy Brodribb et al.. Species intraspecific variation drives tropical forest drought resistance. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

Abstract unavailable. Open the link to read more.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it reveals that variation *within* species—not just the replacement of one species by another—plays a substantial role in shaping how tropical forests resist drought, with drier forests showing coordinated shifts toward greater embolism resistance and wider stomatal safety margins.

Numerous bow shocks in the outer Helix Nebula

Pieter van Dokkum, Roberto Abraham, W. Paul Bowman et al.. Numerous bow shocks in the outer Helix Nebula. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

Abstract unavailable. Open the link to read more.

Why it is getting attention: The work is drawing attention because newly detected bow shocks in the outer regions of the Helix Nebula offer direct observational evidence that fragmented stellar ejecta are being recycled back into the surrounding interstellar gas. This finding sheds light on a poorly understood process by which material shed by dying stars is reintegrated into the broader galactic environment.

Maternal influences on infant gut microbiome and health

Trishla Sinha, Siobhan Brushett, Asier Fernández-Pato et al.. Maternal influences on infant gut microbiome and health. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This study investigated how maternal and early-life factors shape the infant gut microbiome, a topic whose determinants have remained poorly understood. Researchers conducted metagenomic sequencing of a large longitudinal collection of fecal samples from mother-infant pairs in a Dutch birth cohort, integrating these data with hundreds of clinical and exposure variables alongside sequencing of breast milk and vaginal microbiomes. They found that the maternal gut microbiome undergoes only subtle changes during pregnancy and postpartum, yet serves as a primary reservoir for infant gut strains, with transmission being time-dependent and influenced by maternal species abundance. Mode of delivery and feeding mode emerged as the dominant shapers of the infant gut microbiome and its functional profiles, while home versus hospital delivery was only moderately associated with infant gut microbiome composition. Notably, the maternal gut microbiome was identified as a predictor of infant eczema, underscoring the broader role of maternal microbial communities in shaping early infant health outcomes.

Why it is getting attention: This work is drawing attention because it identifies the maternal gut microbiome as a predictor of eczema in infants, suggesting that a mother's microbial composition influences her child's health outcomes beyond direct transmission.

Evidence for the first globular cluster stellar stream beyond the Milky Way

Julie Kiel Holm, Sarah Pearson, Jacob Nibauer et al.. Evidence for the first globular cluster stellar stream beyond the Milky Way. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper investigates the dark matter content of ultra-diffuse galaxies using stellar streams as a diagnostic tool. The researchers identified evidence for the first known extragalactic globular cluster stellar stream in deep Hubble Space Telescope imaging of the ultra-diffuse galaxy UGC 9050-Dw1. The stream's morphology, color, and association with a compact source were used to support a globular cluster progenitor interpretation, and simulated globular cluster stellar populations successfully reproduced the observed surface brightness. Using generative stream modeling that fits dynamical models directly to the stream's morphology, the authors constrained both the mass of the progenitor system and the properties of the host dark matter halo, finding evidence for a massive dark matter halo around this ultra-diffuse galaxy. The work demonstrates that globular cluster stream analysis can be extended beyond the Milky Way, opening a new approach to studying dark matter in external galaxies.

Why it is getting attention: This work is drawing attention because it presents the first evidence of a globular cluster stellar stream discovered outside the Milky Way, extending a phenomenon previously known only in our own galaxy to an extragalactic setting. Beyond this observational milestone, the stream also serves as a probe of the dark matter content of its ultra-diffuse host galaxy.

Biomarkers of nivolumab benefit in resectable non-small cell lung cancer

Tina Cascone, Mark M. Awad, Jonathan Spicer et al.. Biomarkers of nivolumab benefit in resectable non-small cell lung cancer. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This study investigated biomarkers associated with clinical benefit from perioperative nivolumab treatment in patients with resectable non-small cell lung cancer, using data from the CheckMate 77T trial. Researchers analyzed circulating tumor DNA, molecular residual disease, genomic alterations in specific driver genes, and pathologic complete response in a subset of randomized patients receiving either nivolumab or placebo. Circulating tumor DNA clearance before surgery occurred more frequently in the nivolumab group than the placebo group and was associated with higher rates of pathologic complete response, while post-surgical molecular residual disease conversion to positive status predicted disease recurrence in both groups. Patients harboring alterations in genes including KEAP1, STK11, CDKN2A, and SMARCA4 appeared to derive prolonged event-free survival benefit from nivolumab compared with placebo. A machine-learning model further identified pre-surgical circulating tumor DNA clearance, tumor histology, disease stage, pathologic complete response, and nivolumab treatment as top predictors of prolonged event-free survival, collectively offering insight into potential predictive biomarkers for perioperative immunotherapy in resectable lung cancer.

Why it is getting attention: This work is drawing attention because it identifies potential predictive biomarkers that could help determine which non-small cell lung cancer patients are most likely to benefit from perioperative immunotherapy, addressing a critical gap in personalizing treatment decisions. The findings are grounded in data from a major clinical trial, lending them particular clinical relevance.

Bow shocks reveal how a dying star returns matter to interstellar space

Bow shocks reveal how a dying star returns matter to interstellar space. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

Deep imaging of ionized gas in the Helix Nebula was used to study the process by which a dying Sun-like star returns its material to the surrounding interstellar medium. The observations revealed numerous curved shock waves, called bow shocks, formed as ejected shells of stellar material fragment and accelerate outward from the star. The changing morphology of these bow shocks indicates that the shell fragments are progressively stripped, shredded, and mixed into the interstellar gas. This process is estimated to unfold over roughly ten thousand years, providing direct observational insight into how planetary nebulae recycle stellar matter back into space.

Why it is getting attention: This work is drawing attention because it provides direct observational evidence of the detailed physical process by which a dying Sun-like star returns its material to interstellar space, revealed through newly imaged bow shocks in the Helix Nebula. The findings show that ejected shell fragments are progressively stripped and mixed into surrounding interstellar gas on a timescale of roughly ten thousand years, offering a clearer picture of stellar mass-loss and galactic chemical enrichment.

Early Detection and Surveillance of the SARS-CoV-2 Variant BA.3.2 — Worldwide, November 2024–February 2026

Mila Shakya, Kevin C. Ma, Laura J. Hughes et al.. Early Detection and Surveillance of the SARS-CoV-2 Variant BA.3.2 — Worldwide, November 2024–February 2026. MMWR Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report. 2026

The paper examined the emergence and spread of SARS-CoV-2 variant BA.3.2, a strain first identified in South Africa in late 2024 that carries a notably large number of mutations in the spike protein relative to the antigens used in current vaccines. Using a multimodal genomic surveillance strategy that integrated traveler-based screening, clinical specimen testing, and wastewater monitoring, public health authorities tracked the variant's introduction and domestic spread within the United States and internationally. BA.3.2 was first detected in the United States through a traveler arriving from the Netherlands, with subsequent detections in clinical patients, airplane wastewater, and community wastewater samples spanning a substantial number of states. The variant had also been reported across more than two dozen countries by the surveillance period's end. The authors conclude that the extent of spike protein divergence in BA.3.2 raises concern for reduced protection from prior infection or vaccination, underscoring the need for continued genomic surveillance to assess its public health impact.

Wastewater Metagenomics for Antimicrobial Resistance and Pathogen Surveillance: A Bibliometric Analysis.

Zheng Y, Ma N, Zhao B et al.. Wastewater Metagenomics for Antimicrobial Resistance and Pathogen Surveillance: A Bibliometric Analysis.. Microorganisms. 2026

This study examined the global research landscape at the intersection of wastewater metagenomics, antimicrobial resistance, and pathogen surveillance using bibliometric methods. The authors retrieved over a thousand publications from a major scientific database and applied network analysis tools to map publication trends, international collaborations, keyword evolution, and literature co-citation patterns across a fifteen-year period. They found that the field experienced dramatic growth, particularly following the COVID-19 pandemic, with research themes shifting from basic detection of antibiotic resistance genes in activated sludge toward broader One Health and public health surveillance frameworks. Despite high output from certain countries, analysis revealed that integration between environmental monitoring and public health literature remains limited, indicating that translating metagenomic surveillance data into actionable health risk assessments is still nascent. The authors conclude by identifying key priorities for the field, including standardizing analytical methods, incorporating artificial intelligence for resistome analysis, broadening participation from underrepresented regions, and operationalizing One Health surveillance approaches.

Validation of molecular detection of &lt;i&gt;Candida auris&lt;/i&gt; from wastewater.

Rodríguez Stewart RM, Litvintseva AP, Chavez J et al.. Validation of molecular detection of &lt;i&gt;Candida auris&lt;/i&gt; from wastewater.. Microbiology spectrum. 2026

The study investigated whether a quantitative PCR assay originally developed for clinical *Candida auris* colonization screening could be adapted for detecting this multidrug-resistant fungal pathogen in wastewater. Given that *C. auris* skin colonization and shedding contribute to its environmental persistence and transmission in healthcare settings, the researchers validated the qPCR workflow using a wastewater matrix to assess its performance characteristics. The assay demonstrated high amplification efficiency, strong reproducibility, and a low limit of detection, indicating it can sensitively and reliably identify *C. auris* in wastewater samples. The authors conclude that this approach offers a cost-effective alternative or complement to direct patient screening, enabling broader surveillance of *C. auris* prevalence across entire healthcare systems, including facilities where routine clinical screening is not yet in place.

A Systematic Review of Gastrointestinal and Respiratory Pathogen Detection in Wastewater in Africa, with Focus on Rwanda: Implications for Early Warning and Public Health Surveillance.

Bambara S, Isingizwe MC, Adegboyega TT et al.. A Systematic Review of Gastrointestinal and Respiratory Pathogen Detection in Wastewater in Africa, with Focus on Rwanda: Implications for Early Warning and Public Health Surveillance.. Pathogens (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

This systematic review examined the use of wastewater-based epidemiology (WBE) for detecting gastrointestinal and respiratory pathogens in Africa, with particular attention to the context of Rwanda as a rapidly urbanizing, low- and middle-income country with limited traditional surveillance capacity. The authors conducted a narrative systematic review by searching multiple databases for peer-reviewed literature, global health reports, and surveillance studies published over more than two decades, ultimately including a substantial subset of records after screening for pathogen-specific wastewater data and public health relevance. The review found that WBE offers a non-invasive and cost-effective means of capturing both symptomatic and asymptomatic infections at the community level, providing real-time epidemiological intelligence that clinical surveillance systems frequently miss due to underreporting. Key challenges identified include variability in wastewater sampling methods, environmental factors that promote viral decay, and inconsistencies in laboratory workflows across settings. The authors conclude that integrating WBE into existing public health surveillance systems, particularly in resource-limited settings like Rwanda, could substantially strengthen epidemic preparedness, guide resource allocation, and improve outbreak response, provided that investment in laboratory infrastructure, standardized protocols, and multisectoral collaboration is sustained.

Wastewater Surveillance for Early Warning of Infectious Disease Outbreaks: A Systematic Review of Evidence and Implications for One Health Surveillance.

Panigrahi S, Pattnaik M, Pradhan R et al.. Wastewater Surveillance for Early Warning of Infectious Disease Outbreaks: A Systematic Review of Evidence and Implications for One Health Surveillance.. Pathogens (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

This systematic review examined the role of wastewater surveillance as an early warning tool for infectious disease outbreaks, with a focus on its integration within a One Health framework that links human, animal, and environmental health. The authors systematically searched multiple major databases, screened the retrieved studies through a structured review process, and synthesized findings from studies that met their inclusion criteria. The evidence showed a positive association between pathogen detection in wastewater and clinical case counts in communities, with respiratory and gastrointestinal pathogens being most commonly identified and demonstrating season-specific surges in circulation. Sampling frequency and duration in socially vulnerable areas were particularly valuable for providing early outbreak warnings. The review concluded that while wastewater surveillance holds strong promise as a complementary public health tool, standardization of methods remains lacking and explicit application of One Health frameworks is rare, pointing to a need for more integrated surveillance systems across human, animal, and environmental domains.

Multi-Pathogen Wastewater Surveillance enables Real-Time Targeted Public Health Interventions During the 2025 African Nations Championship Football Tournament

Nsawotebba A, Morunyanga I, Nakintu V et al.. Multi-Pathogen Wastewater Surveillance enables Real-Time Targeted Public Health Interventions During the 2025 African Nations Championship Football Tournament. 2026

This study examined whether multi-pathogen wastewater-based surveillance could serve as a practical public health tool during a major mass gathering in a low- and middle-income country. During a football tournament held in Uganda, researchers deployed Moore swabs across dozens of manholes at a national stadium on multiple match days, concentrating pathogens from collected wastewater samples and analyzing them for a broad range of enteric, respiratory, and other pathogens using quantitative PCR. The vast majority of samples tested positive for at least one pathogen, with enteric pathogens such as Shigella, Rotavirus A, and Enterovirus detected most frequently, while mpox virus was also identified at a notable proportion of sites, and respiratory pathogens including SARS-CoV-2 and Influenza B appeared at lower rates. Pathogen diversity fluctuated across sampling days, with multiple pathogens co-detected in most positive samples, and surveillance findings directly informed real-time public health responses including sanitation improvements, hygiene promotion, environmental disinfection, and strengthened on-site clinical triage. The authors conclude that integrating multi-pathogen wastewater surveillance into mass-gathering preparedness frameworks is operationally feasible in low-resource settings and can provide actionable, population-level epidemiological intelligence to support outbreak detection and response.

Revealing the hidden burden: wastewater-based epidemiology for underreported and emerging infectious diseases in communities.

Greaves JC, Rodriguez RA. Revealing the hidden burden: wastewater-based epidemiology for underreported and emerging infectious diseases in communities.. Environmental monitoring and assessment. 2026

Wastewater-based epidemiology (WBE) has emerged as a powerful surveillance approach that captures population-level infectious disease data beyond what traditional clinical reporting systems can detect. This review examines how WBE identifies underreported, novel, and emerging pathogens—including enteric, respiratory, and neglected agents—that are routinely missed by healthcare systems, thereby revealing the true hidden burden of infection in communities. Sequencing-based studies conducted through this approach have uncovered extensive diversity within the human virome, including numerous novel and divergent viruses, while also demonstrating that co-detection of multiple viral taxa may influence viral evolution and disease dynamics. WBE has shown the capacity to detect both known and novel pathogens before they gain clinical recognition, though challenges in quantification and biological validation remain. The review anticipates that advances in long-read sequencing, bioinformatics, and global data integration will further establish WBE as a foundational component of early-warning and One Health surveillance systems worldwide.

Inferring variant-specific effective reproduction numbers from combined case and sequencing data

Marlin D Figgins, Trevor Bedford. Inferring variant-specific effective reproduction numbers from combined case and sequencing data. eLife. 2026

This paper addresses the challenge of accurately estimating the transmission rates of different SARS-CoV-2 variants circulating simultaneously. The authors developed a statistical method that jointly analyzes confirmed case counts and genetic sequencing data to infer variant-specific effective reproduction numbers and variant frequencies, going beyond approaches that rely on sequence proportions alone. The method also allows structured relationships between reproduction numbers to be estimated across time series, enabling the inference of fixed growth advantages for specific variants across different geographic locations. Applying this framework to data from United States states over a period spanning from early 2021 to early 2022, the authors estimated effective reproduction numbers for multiple variants of concern and interest and identified consistent growth advantages of particular variants across locations. The work demonstrates that combining case and sequencing data provides richer epidemiological insight into variant dynamics than sequencing data alone.

Bacterial defense mechanisms against bacteriophages: an evolutionary arms race

Rafwana Ibrahim, Jesil Mathew Aranjani. Bacterial defense mechanisms against bacteriophages: an evolutionary arms race. Archives of Microbiology. 2026

This review paper examined the co-evolutionary arms race between bacteria and bacteriophages, focusing on the diverse defense mechanisms bacteria have developed to resist phage infection and the counter-adaptations phages employ in response. The authors systematically surveyed bacterial defenses operating at multiple stages of the phage infection cycle, including surface-level strategies such as receptor modification, capsule production, and biofilm formation, as well as intracellular mechanisms like restriction-modification systems, abortive infection, and CRISPR-Cas adaptive immunity. Phages were found to counter these defenses through means such as anti-CRISPR proteins, receptor mimicry, and enzymatic degradation of capsules and biofilms. The review concludes that these dynamic interactions shape broader microbial ecosystems and have practical implications for developing novel antimicrobial strategies, including engineered phages and combination therapies. The authors argue that a deeper understanding of bacterial-phage dynamics is essential for advancing phage therapy as a viable tool against multidrug-resistant bacterial infections.

The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development

Emad M. Abdallah, Abdulrahman Mohammed Alhudhaibi, Mahmoud M. Dahab et al.. The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development. Frontiers in Pharmacology. 2026

This narrative review examines the global challenge of antimicrobial resistance with a focus on the bacterial pathogens designated as priorities by the World Health Organization. The authors synthesize current understanding of the molecular mechanisms driving resistance in these pathogens, encompassing reduced membrane permeability, efflux pump overexpression, enzymatic drug inactivation, target modification, biofilm formation, and horizontal gene transfer. The review critically evaluates why conventional antibiotic therapies and traditional drug discovery pipelines have proven insufficient, and assesses a range of emerging therapeutic strategies including artificial intelligence-assisted drug discovery, phage therapy, antimicrobial peptides, CRISPR-based systems, and natural product-derived compounds, while addressing their translational and regulatory challenges. The authors also emphasize the importance of antimicrobial stewardship, global surveillance, and a One Health framework that integrates human, animal, and environmental health sectors to slow resistance emergence. The overall conclusion is that combating priority-resistant pathogens will require coordinated, multidisciplinary approaches spanning molecular biology, pharmacology, clinical practice, and public health policy.

Microbial engineering for pesticide degradation: current insights and future directions for sustainable agriculture

S. Sangeeta Srivastava Srivastava, Rakeeb Ahmad Mir, Sofi Javed Hussain et al.. Microbial engineering for pesticide degradation: current insights and future directions for sustainable agriculture. Frontiers in Microbiology. 2026

This review examines the problem of pesticide persistence in agricultural environments and evaluates microbial strategies for their degradation as sustainable alternatives to conventional physical and chemical remediation. The paper synthesizes current understanding of how pesticides affect crop physiology and metabolism, and surveys the diversity of microorganism-mediated breakdown pathways, with particular emphasis on enzymatic mechanisms and the metabolic versatility of microbes that exploit pesticides as carbon, sulphur, and electron sources. A central focus is placed on microbial consortia, which leverage functional diversity and synergistic interactions to achieve enhanced degradation efficiency, and on microbiome engineering approaches that enable targeted manipulation of microbial communities for improved specificity and performance. The review also covers genetic modification strategies, including protoplast fusion and rhizosphere engineering with plant growth–promoting microorganisms, which have shown potential to simultaneously accelerate pesticide breakdown and improve crop productivity. The authors conclude that integrative, consortia-based, and engineered microbial systems represent a viable and sustainable pathway toward resilient agroecosystems, and they highlight emerging technologies, environmental and economic benefits, and recent patentable innovations in this field.

Evaluation of antimicrobial resistance governance across 193 countries to inform the 2026 Global Action Plan update

Weiye Chen, Yige Zeng, Jinxin Zheng et al.. Evaluation of antimicrobial resistance governance across 193 countries to inform the 2026 Global Action Plan update. Nature Medicine. 2026

This study evaluated the effectiveness of national antimicrobial resistance (AMR) action plans across all United Nations member states over a multi-year period, using a newly developed One Health governance index constructed through expert consultation and integrating hundreds of policy documents alongside multinational survey and surveillance data. The researchers applied difference-in-differences and joinpoint regression methods to assess how governance quality relates to antimicrobial use, AMR prevalence, and AMR-related mortality. Overall governance scores improved meaningfully across the study period, but implementation and monitoring consistently trailed behind policy design, particularly in animal and environmental sectors. A notable finding was that measurable reductions in AMR prevalence did not emerge until approximately five years after countries adopted national action plans, highlighting a substantial delay between policy adoption and observable impact. Multisector engagement and antimicrobial use surveillance were identified as the factors most strongly associated with improved AMR outcomes, leading the authors to call for sustained financing and stronger environmental and agricultural integration as the next Global Action Plan is developed.

Blazar Boosted Dark Matter in IceCube

Alberto M. Gago, Jaime Hoefken Zink, Joel Jones-Pérez et al.. Blazar Boosted Dark Matter in IceCube. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigates whether the IceCube neutrino detector can be used to search for dark matter particles that have been accelerated to relativistic speeds by proton jets from blazars. The researchers modeled a fermionic dark matter scenario with a massive vector mediator coupling to quarks, computing the diffuse dark matter flux from a large sample of blazars whose proton spectra were inferred from multiwavelength observations, while accounting for dark matter spike profiles around supermassive black holes and Earth attenuation effects. The dark matter–nucleon scattering cross section was calculated by including elastic, resonant single pion production, and deep inelastic scattering contributions to smoothly bridge different interaction regimes. Using IceCube data, the authors derived constraints on the model's parameter space and found that this blazar-boosting strategy can exceed the sensitivity of conventional direct-detection experiments for dark matter masses below approximately one GeV. The signal is found to be dominated by deep inelastic scattering, making it most sensitive to heavier mediators, while resonant processes reduce event rates by up to roughly nine percent near the detection threshold.

Cosmic Ray Diffusion and the Origin of Very High Energy Gamma-Ray Emission in Young Massive Stellar Clusters

Lucas Barreto-Mota, Elisabete M. de Gouveia Dal Pino, Gaetano Di Marco et al.. Cosmic Ray Diffusion and the Origin of Very High Energy Gamma-Ray Emission in Young Massive Stellar Clusters. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates young massive stellar clusters as potential Galactic sources capable of accelerating cosmic rays to petaelectronvolt energies, focusing on how diffusion physics shapes the very high energy gamma-ray emission observed around such systems. The authors developed a theoretical framework showing that mirror diffusion combined with pitch-angle scattering in turbulent magnetic fields naturally produces an energy-dependent diffusion coefficient scaling as the cube root of energy, then coupled three-dimensional magnetohydrodynamic simulations of a young massive stellar cluster with Monte Carlo cosmic ray propagation calculations to model the multi-wavelength environment realistically. Using the stellar cluster W43 as a benchmark, they tested two cosmic ray injection geometries—a central point source and a spherical shell at the collective wind shock—and compared predicted gamma-ray spectra against observations from Fermi and LHAASO. They found that the suppressed mirror-plus-scattering diffusion regime, combined with a standard injection spectrum, successfully reproduces the observed gamma-ray data, whereas stronger energy-dependent diffusion requires an unusually hard injection spectrum to match observations. A key conclusion is that the observed gamma-ray emission is governed primarily by the diffusive propagation regime rather than by where exactly within the cluster the cosmic rays are injected.

ATLAS. IV. A JWST+MUSE Demographic Study of Ly$α$ Profiles in Little Red Dots

Yuta Kageura, Masami Ouchi, Hiroto Yanagisawa et al.. ATLAS. IV. A JWST+MUSE Demographic Study of Ly$α$ Profiles in Little Red Dots. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated the properties of Lyman-alpha emission in little red dots, a population of compact high-redshift objects detected by JWST, using spectroscopic observations spanning redshifts from three to nine. The researchers assembled a sample of little red dots observed with both ground-based VLT/MUSE and space-based JWST/NIRSpec instruments, detecting Lyman-alpha emission in roughly half of the objects examined. The study found that broad Lyman-alpha emission, indicative of powerful outflows or active galactic nucleus activity, is present in only two sources, while the majority of Lyman-alpha emitters among little red dots show narrow line widths comparable to those of typical high-redshift star-forming galaxies. Although the fraction of broad Lyman-alpha emitters is elevated compared to ordinary star-forming galaxies, stacking analyses of the larger sample reveal no ubiquitous broad component once the two individually detected cases are excluded. The authors conclude that broad Lyman-alpha emission characterizes only a rare subset of little red dots, possibly representing a distinct evolutionary phase tied to unusually strong outflows or other exceptional physical conditions.

Finding Vulnerabilities via LLM-Augmented Semantics-Aware Type-Checking

Ruizhe Wang, Meng Xu, N. Asokan. Finding Vulnerabilities via LLM-Augmented Semantics-Aware Type-Checking. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of detecting software vulnerabilities through static analysis, arguing that traditional rule-based approaches rely too heavily on syntactic patterns while ignoring the semantic meaning embedded in code. The authors propose SETYPE, a semantics-aware type system that derives type information directly from the natural language meanings of symbols and expressions in source code, using large language models to perform both type inference and type checking. A failed type check in this framework signals a potential vulnerability, eliminating the need for security experts to manually encode insecure coding patterns. The approach was prototyped for Python web applications and evaluated on real-world software, achieving high detection precision and accuracy. The system also discovered numerous previously unknown zero-day vulnerabilities, a substantial fraction of which were subsequently confirmed by developers.

Marionette: Predicting World States, Rendering Geometry, Painting Appearance

Zian Meng, Zhen Li, Chuanhao Li et al.. Marionette: Predicting World States, Rendering Geometry, Painting Appearance. arXiv. 2026 preprint

Marionette is a world model for interactive games featuring articulated characters that addresses the problem of error accumulation in long-horizon video generation by explicitly separating world state prediction, geometric rendering, and appearance synthesis. Rather than autoregressing directly in pixel or latent space, the system first predicts an interpretable high-dimensional three-dimensional world state encoding multi-entity articulated skeletons and trajectories, then converts this state into structured pose-control videos using a fixed zero-parameter renderer that computes geometry and occlusion analytically, and finally synthesizes photorealistic observations using a video diffusion model conditioned on these structured controls. This decomposition makes the world state directly controllable, as demonstrated by experiments showing that injecting mismatched action streams measurably alters joint error. Critically, long-horizon failures such as character drift and ground penetration can be corrected by applying simple geometric rules directly to the explicit state without modifying the appearance model, substantially reducing ground penetration and maintaining plausible character proximity. The approach achieves video fidelity comparable to generation conditioned on recorded pose, confirming that routing appearance through the predicted state incurs no detectable quality cost.

The deepest color-magnitude diagrams for the benchmark open cluster NGC 2437 from Gaia and VVVX

Tali Palma, Matías Gómez, Dante Minniti et al.. The deepest color-magnitude diagrams for the benchmark open cluster NGC 2437 from Gaia and VVVX. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study characterizes the physical properties of NGC 2437, one of the most extended star clusters within the near-infrared footprint of the VVV Extended Survey, using a combination of optical data from Gaia DR3 and near-infrared data from the VVVX survey. Proper motions from Gaia were used to identify cluster members, enabling the construction of optical and near-infrared color-magnitude and color-color diagrams, including the deepest near-infrared color-magnitude diagram yet produced for this cluster. The analysis yielded well-constrained physical parameters including distance, reddening, extinction, structural properties, and a mean cluster age estimated using solar-metallicity isochrones, along with a binary fraction measurement. The improved depth achieved through VVVX deep stack imaging substantially increased the number of detected point sources compared to shallower observations, demonstrating the value of this approach for studying stellar populations in the southern Galactic plane. The revised cluster parameters were also used to reassess the relationship of NGC 2437 to several nearby objects, including an adjacent open cluster, a planetary nebula, and an evolved stellar source in the same field.

Point-of-care molecular diagnostics and drug-resistance mechanisms in neglected infectious diseases: current advances and future therapeutic opportunities

Qi Zhang, Xiajun Zhang, Jie Yang et al.. Point-of-care molecular diagnostics and drug-resistance mechanisms in neglected infectious diseases: current advances and future therapeutic opportunities. Frontiers in Cellular and Infection Microbiology. 2026

This review examines the current state and future prospects of point-of-care molecular diagnostics for neglected infectious diseases, with particular attention to antimicrobial resistance. The authors survey a range of emerging technologies, including isothermal nucleic acid amplification, rapid polymerase chain reaction, CRISPR-based detection, nanomaterial biosensors, and microfluidic platforms capable of delivering near-laboratory accuracy within clinically relevant timeframes without dependence on centralized infrastructure. The integration of artificial intelligence and cloud-based digital health systems is highlighted as a means of automating result interpretation and enabling real-time resistance surveillance and antimicrobial stewardship. Next-generation platforms are shown to offer mechanistic insights into drug resistance in diseases such as tuberculosis, malaria, and bacterial infections, though barriers including clinical validation, cost, scalability, and equitable access remain significant. The authors argue that the convergence of molecular diagnostics, nanotechnology, and AI-driven analytics creates transformative opportunities for precision therapy, resistance monitoring, and infectious disease preparedness in low-resource settings.

Rapid and Sensitive Detection of &lt;i&gt;Schistosoma mansoni&lt;/i&gt; in the Intermediate Snail Hosts Using Loop-Mediated Isothermal Amplification (LAMP) Diagnostics.

Li HM, Qin ZQ, Lv S et al.. Rapid and Sensitive Detection of &lt;i&gt;Schistosoma mansoni&lt;/i&gt; in the Intermediate Snail Hosts Using Loop-Mediated Isothermal Amplification (LAMP) Diagnostics.. Tropical medicine and infectious disease. 2026

The paper studied the detection of *Schistosoma mansoni* infections in freshwater snail intermediate hosts, which is a critical component of schistosomiasis surveillance and control. The researchers developed a loop-mediated isothermal amplification assay targeting the mitochondrial NADH dehydrogenase subunit 1 gene of the parasite, optimizing it for a simple, rapid reaction at a fixed temperature. The assay's performance was evaluated against conventional parasitological methods, standard PCR, and real-time PCR using both laboratory-infected snails and field-collected samples from two endemic African countries. The new assay achieved complete specificity and high sensitivity in laboratory conditions, and in field settings it detected more infected snails than conventional PCR, while microscopy failed to detect any positive cases at all. The authors conclude that this assay represents a sensitive, specific, and practically deployable tool suitable for routine snail surveillance and early warning of shifts in schistosomiasis transmission.

You Only Look Once (YOLO) based machine learning algorithm for real-time detection of loop-mediated isothermal amplification (LAMP) diagnostics.

Mezgebo B, Chaffee R, Castellanos LR et al.. You Only Look Once (YOLO) based machine learning algorithm for real-time detection of loop-mediated isothermal amplification (LAMP) diagnostics.. PloS one. 2026

This paper addressed the problem of subjective and error-prone visual interpretation of loop-mediated isothermal amplification results, which can lead to false positives and negatives in molecular diagnostics. The researchers developed a machine-learning pipeline using the YOLOv8 object detection algorithm to automatically locate reaction tubes in digital images and classify them as positive or negative for DNA amplification. The model was trained and evaluated on a test set, achieving high overall accuracy along with strong precision and recall metrics for both positive and negative classifications. The findings demonstrate that this automated approach is suitable for real-time LAMP diagnostics, offering rapid, objective, and reliable result interpretation with minimal operational complexity.

Broad-Temperature Polymerase in Nucleic Acid Amplification-Based Diagnostics: From Thermal Precision to Dynamic Conditions.

Hamidizadeh M, Bier FF. Broad-Temperature Polymerase in Nucleic Acid Amplification-Based Diagnostics: From Thermal Precision to Dynamic Conditions.. ACS sensors. 2026

This paper investigated nucleic acid amplification across a wide temperature range as an alternative to conventional methods that require precise thermal control. The researchers developed a LAMP-based amplification approach using a Bst polymerase with strong strand displacement activity and broad thermostability, enabling DNA synthesis across a temperature spectrum from below typical LAMP conditions up to temperatures approaching those used in PCR. They demonstrated that amplification could be initiated at both high and low starting temperatures and sustained under dynamically changing temperature profiles, rather than requiring a fixed isothermal condition. The system was validated for detection of SARS-CoV-2 and Mycobacterium tuberculosis genomic targets, confirming its diagnostic applicability. The authors propose this broad-temperature amplification platform as a promising solution for point-of-care and field diagnostics where maintaining strict thermal regulation is impractical.

DISCO-LAMP: a novel discontinuous LAMP assay for isothermal antigen detection.

Thomas BM, Simeon RA, Yan KL et al.. DISCO-LAMP: a novel discontinuous LAMP assay for isothermal antigen detection.. Sensors & diagnostics. 2026

This paper developed a novel protein detection method called DISCO-LAMP, which combines proximity ligation assay technology with loop-mediated isothermal amplification to enable wash-free antigen detection without the need for temperature cycling equipment. The central challenge addressed was achieving strong amplification of ligated full-length DNA products while suppressing background amplification from unligated individual DNA fragments, which was overcome through extensive template and primer design optimization. The optimized assay achieved a detection limit in the femtomolar range for target DNA, and when paired with specific affinity reagents, successfully detected Shiga toxin 2 as well as both Wuhan-1 and Omicron variants of the SARS-CoV-2 spike protein using a newly engineered binding protein targeting a conserved epitope. The authors conclude that DISCO-LAMP is a versatile, isothermal platform that can be adapted to detect a wide range of protein targets through the proximity ligation framework.

Recent advances in isothermal amplification techniques for detection of animal diseases.

Tooryan F, Enyertornye B, Choudhury FJ et al.. Recent advances in isothermal amplification techniques for detection of animal diseases.. Frontiers in veterinary science. 2026

This review examines isothermal amplification technologies as diagnostic tools for detecting pathogens in both livestock and companion animals. The authors synthesize recent advances in these methods, which amplify nucleic acids at a constant temperature and therefore do not require the thermocycling equipment central to conventional PCR-based approaches. The review covers innovations in assay design, improvements in analytical performance, and the integration of these techniques into portable platforms suited for field and resource-limited settings. The authors highlight how isothermal methods offer rapid, sensitive, and user-friendly alternatives that overcome the infrastructure, equipment, and personnel barriers associated with traditional molecular and conventional diagnostics. The review also addresses remaining challenges and outlines future directions for deploying these technologies in routine veterinary diagnostics and broader animal disease surveillance programs.

Numerical study of the ITER divertor plasma with the B2-EIRENE code package

V. Kotov. Numerical study of the ITER divertor plasma with the B2-EIRENE code package. Dokumentenrepositorium der RUB (Ruhr University Bochum). 2026

Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms

Purshottam J. Assudani, Balakrishnan . P., Anny Leema A et al.. Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms. Microchimica Acta. 2026

This review examines the current state and future direction of biosensing technologies designed to detect priority foodborne pathogens, including *E. coli*, *Salmonella*, and *Listeria monocytogenes*, which continue to represent serious global public health threats. The authors survey electrochemical, optical, and microfluidic sensing modalities, analyzing how advances in functional nanomaterials, aptamers, nanozymes, and surface engineering strategies have improved sensor sensitivity, specificity, and performance in complex food matrices. A central focus is the integration of artificial intelligence and machine learning into biosensor platforms, enabling intelligent pattern recognition, real-time analytics, and high-throughput multiplexed detection that transform conventional systems into smart diagnostic tools. The review critically evaluates biosensor design principles, signal transduction mechanisms, and performance validation across diverse food environments, while identifying persistent challenges such as matrix interference, bioreceptor instability, manufacturing scalability, and the absence of regulatory standardization. The authors propose mitigation strategies including ratiometric sensing, microfluidic pre-treatment, explainable AI, and printable electronics, and offer a forward-looking perspective encompassing self-powered biosensors, modular pathogen panels, and blockchain-integrated validation pipelines for next-generation food safety monitoring.

Antimicrobial Resistance in the Food Chain: Bridging Knowledge Gaps for Effective Detection and Control

Emílio Gomes, Tomás Gonçalves Mesquita, Patrícia Serra et al.. Antimicrobial Resistance in the Food Chain: Bridging Knowledge Gaps for Effective Detection and Control. Antibiotics. 2026

This review examines antimicrobial resistance as a global public health threat transmitted through the food chain, connecting animals, the environment, and humans under a One Health framework. The authors analyzed the key drivers of AMR spread across animal agriculture, aquaculture, and food processing, while evaluating detection methodologies ranging from traditional culture-based phenotypic testing to advanced molecular approaches including whole genome sequencing, metagenomics, and CRISPR-based diagnostics. Although molecular tools offer greater speed and resolution than conventional methods, the review identifies persistent challenges such as matrix interference, the viable but non-culturable bacterial state, and the disconnect between genotype and phenotype. The paper also describes integrated mitigation strategies encompassing on-farm antimicrobial stewardship, biofilm control, and consumer hygiene practices. The authors conclude that bridging technical and regulatory gaps in AMR surveillance is essential for developing effective interventions and ensuring food system safety.

Taenia solium neurocysticercosis: Its current epidemiological, diagnostic, therapeutic, and control landscapes

Javier A. Bustos, Christina M. Coyle, Kiran T. Thakur et al.. Taenia solium neurocysticercosis: Its current epidemiological, diagnostic, therapeutic, and control landscapes. PLoS neglected tropical diseases. 2026

Neurocysticercosis, caused by the tapeworm *Taenia solium*, is the most prevalent helminthic infection of the human central nervous system and presents with highly variable clinical manifestations. The paper is a comprehensive review synthesizing current knowledge on the epidemiology, diagnosis, treatment, and control of this disease, addressing both endemic regions and industrialized countries where it is increasingly seen in immigrant populations. Over the past decade, notable advances have emerged in anti-parasitic combination therapies, strategies to mitigate treatment-associated inflammation, novel surgical approaches, and more sensitive and specific immunological and neuroimaging diagnostic tools. Despite these advances, diagnostic difficulties remain and treatment strategies can vary depending on the form of the disease, creating ongoing challenges for clinical practice. The review aims to serve as a practical reference for clinicians by clarifying the variability in disease expression and guiding optimal diagnostic and therapeutic decision-making across different epidemiological settings.

Varicella Zoster Virus Infection: Clinical Features, Molecular Pathogenesis, Treatment, and Prevention

Lei Peng, Honghao Song, Tianying Li et al.. Varicella Zoster Virus Infection: Clinical Features, Molecular Pathogenesis, Treatment, and Prevention. MedComm. 2026

This review examines varicella zoster virus, a human herpesvirus that establishes lifelong latency and reactivates to cause herpes zoster, a condition whose incidence rises markedly with age and immune compromise. The authors synthesize current understanding of the virus's epidemiology, structure, and life cycle alongside the molecular mechanisms underlying latency, reactivation, neuronal injury, and immune evasion. The clinical burden of herpes zoster is described as extending well beyond skin disease to encompass postherpetic neuralgia, neurological complications, and systemic involvement. The review also surveys advances in diagnostic technologies, antiviral and anti-inflammatory therapeutic strategies, management of long-term sequelae, and preventive approaches including vaccination. The authors identify a gap between molecular insights and comprehensive clinical implementation, and propose an integrative framework intended to guide translation of fundamental discoveries into more effective prevention and treatment strategies.

Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation

Zhelun Wu. Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper examines a fundamental design flaw in systems that use language models to draw conclusions from multiple sources: the common practice of concatenating all sources into a single prompt conflates two operationally distinct tasks, namely interpreting individual sources and aggregating those interpretations into a decision. The authors propose separating these tasks and introduce a four-field evidence tuple as a structured interface between them. They identify a failure mode they term count-scale drift, wherein summing unnormalized weights produces a decision threshold that shifts with the number of sources consulted, and show that pooling calibrated log-likelihood ratios resolves both this drift and ordering inconsistencies that arise when source reliabilities differ. The framework is validated on a longitudinal corpus in two temporal settings, where the separation improves performance at different levels of granularity, and a combination of a small sequence encoder with a tree ensemble handling censored survival loss substantially outperforms a hand-crafted baseline on a clinical prediction metric. The authors also specify falsifiable predictions, acknowledged negative results, and confounded comparisons, framing the contribution as applicable beyond language models to any additive multi-signal decision system.

No Reduction of Tropical Convection with Warming Expected from Theory or Models

Zachary McGraw, Blaž Gasparini. No Reduction of Tropical Convection with Warming Expected from Theory or Models. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigates a longstanding apparent contradiction between theoretical predictions of weakening tropical convection under surface warming and the behavior seen in convection-resolving models. The authors examine two major theoretical arguments that have long been used to anticipate reduced convective mass transport as temperatures rise. They demonstrate that both arguments actually describe a redistribution of convective motions rather than an overall reduction, with weakening at certain altitudes being offset by strengthening higher up as the troposphere expands, and apparent mass flux reductions at fixed pressure levels reflecting decreasing air density rather than diminished convective activity. Convective motions and transported water are found to be highly invariant to warming when tracked along isotherms rather than fixed altitudes or pressure levels. These findings reconcile theory with model results and provide a simplified framework for understanding how tropical atmospheric circulation responds to a warming climate.

Residual-Controlled Douglas--Rachford Splitting for Differentiable Solver Layers

Kang Liu, Jianchen Hu. Residual-Controlled Douglas--Rachford Splitting for Differentiable Solver Layers. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the challenge of embedding constrained optimization solvers into end-to-end learning pipelines, where fixed-depth unrolling forces difficult trade-offs between solution quality, feasibility, and computational cost. The authors propose Residual-Controlled Douglas–Rachford Splitting (RCDRS), a differentiable solver layer for conic linear programs that treats the unrolled solver as a feedback-controlled dynamical system, with a causal controller adaptively tuning relaxation and objective-drive parameters while preserving the projection-splitting structure. Theoretically, they establish that each admissible block remains an averaged relaxed operator with finite-step fixed-point residual bounds, and they analyze time-varying rollouts as summable perturbations of a limiting averaged operator to recover primal-dual diagnostics. Experiments on mixed-cone benchmarks and engineering applications demonstrate that RCDRS improves solution quality, feasibility, and downstream decision performance compared to fixed unrolling approaches.

PACE-Bench: Benchmarking Physics Adaptation via Code Evolution in Dynamic Environments

Yuhao Zhan, Bingxiang He, Zecong Tang et al.. PACE-Bench: Benchmarking Physics Adaptation via Code Evolution in Dynamic Environments. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a benchmark designed to evaluate how well self-evolving AI agents can adapt code-based designs when the underlying physics of a simulation environment changes. The benchmark comprises adaptation pairs spanning six physics domains, where each pair presents a source environment and a mutated target environment sharing the same goal and interface, requiring agents to iteratively revise a solution that worked in the source but fails in the target. Ten self-evolving methods from four paradigms were compared using diagnostic sandbox feedback within a limited attempt budget. The benchmark proves far from solved, with the best-performing method succeeding on only a fraction of pairs, and even providing agents with exact information about the physical changes does not substantially raise performance. The results indicate that simulator-grounded reflection outperforms unverified self-revision, that memory mechanisms anchor agents to early designs, and that the core bottleneck is mechanism redesign rather than parameter inference.

MathForm: Scaling Mathematical Autoformalization with Knowledge Retrieval and Verification-Guided Refinement

Lushi Pu, Weiming Zhang, Xinheng Xie et al.. MathForm: Scaling Mathematical Autoformalization with Knowledge Retrieval and Verification-Guided Refinement. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of autoformalization, which involves translating natural-language mathematical statements into machine-verifiable formal languages like Lean 4. The authors developed MathForm, a framework that combines retrieval-augmented generation with iterative, verification-guided refinement to overcome the limitations of approaches that rely solely on a model's parametric memory or single-pass filtering pipelines. Their system uses a retrieval planner to fetch relevant definitions and existing formalizations from the Mathlib library before generation, then revises outputs using compiler diagnostics and semantic-consistency feedback. Using this framework, they constructed a large verified dataset of Lean 4 examples spanning diverse mathematical domains, which was used to train a model through supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning. The resulting model achieves strong performance across multiple benchmarks, outperforming larger specialized models of greater size on both syntactic and semantic consistency metrics.

APTER: Adaptive Post-Training with Expert-Grounded Rubrics

Xukai Wang, Liangqi Li, Zhiyue Xu et al.. APTER: Adaptive Post-Training with Expert-Grounded Rubrics. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of adapting large language models to professional domains where responses must satisfy structured domain constraints, incorporate critical evidence, and demonstrate complete reasoning. The authors propose APTER, a framework that grounds post-training evaluation in expert-constructed criteria frameworks, where stable professional capabilities are instantiated into query-specific rubrics without requiring reference answers. Unlike prior approaches that generate rubrics independently per query or rely on holistic preferences, APTER links rubric verdicts back to their source criteria, enabling diagnosis of persistent capability deficiencies across samples. These diagnostic signals trigger targeted supervised fine-tuning updates within a reinforcement learning loop, allowing the framework to adaptively repair identified weaknesses. Experiments on mathematical reasoning and medical question answering demonstrate consistent improvements across multiple model generations in both domains.
