Diagnostic value of cystatin C in acute kidney injury among patients with sepsis: a systematic review and meta-analysis.

Cheng L, Zhang B, Hu X et al.. Diagnostic value of cystatin C in acute kidney injury among patients with sepsis: a systematic review and meta-analysis.. Frontiers in medicine. 2026

This systematic review and meta-analysis evaluated the diagnostic accuracy of cystatin C (CysC) for the early detection of acute kidney injury in patients with sepsis. The researchers systematically searched electronic databases, assessed study quality using the QUADAS-2 tool, and applied a random-effects meta-analysis alongside summary receiver operating characteristic curve analysis across studies meeting their inclusion criteria. Cystatin C demonstrated good overall diagnostic performance for sepsis-associated acute kidney injury, with strong values for sensitivity, specificity, and area under the curve. Subgroup analyses revealed that studies using Sepsis 3.0 diagnostic criteria showed higher specificity and diagnostic odds ratios with less heterogeneity, and combining cystatin C with other biomarkers further improved diagnostic accuracy. The authors conclude that cystatin C is a promising biomarker for early clinical identification of sepsis-associated acute kidney injury, though heterogeneity among included studies warrants cautious interpretation.

Pancreatic Stone Protein as an Emerging Biomarker in Paediatric Nosocomial and Postoperative Sepsis: An Integrative Review of Diagnostic and Prognostic Performance.

Ramírez Quintana A, Gaman EL, Badea M et al.. Pancreatic Stone Protein as an Emerging Biomarker in Paediatric Nosocomial and Postoperative Sepsis: An Integrative Review of Diagnostic and Prognostic Performance.. International journal of molecular sciences. 2026

This narrative review examined the utility of pancreatic stone protein as a biomarker for detecting and risk-stratifying sepsis in paediatric patients, with particular focus on nosocomial and postoperative settings where distinguishing infectious from sterile inflammation is clinically difficult. A structured literature search across two major databases identified studies published over a recent multi-year period, with over fifty meeting inclusion criteria after screening. The review found that pancreatic stone protein levels rose significantly in children with sepsis compared to those with non-infectious systemic inflammation, and were associated with bacteraemia, organ dysfunction, and mortality. Diagnostic performance was comparable to or superior to conventional markers such as C-reactive protein and procalcitonin, with particularly strong sensitivity and specificity observed in neonatal and other high-risk populations. The authors conclude that pancreatic stone protein is a promising early diagnostic and prognostic tool for paediatric sepsis, while noting that variability across studies necessitates larger multicentre prospective trials and integration into multimarker diagnostic frameworks.

Early diagnostic accuracy of immature platelet fraction for sepsis: a systematic review and meta-analysis.

Wiranata S, Manuaba IBAP, Supadmanaba IGP et al.. Early diagnostic accuracy of immature platelet fraction for sepsis: a systematic review and meta-analysis.. Hematology, transfusion and cell therapy. 2026

This systematic review and meta-analysis examined whether the immature platelet fraction, a readily available marker of bone marrow thrombopoietic activity, can serve as an early diagnostic biomarker for sepsis. The authors searched major medical databases and identified studies meeting inclusion criteria, then pooled data to calculate sensitivity, specificity, diagnostic odds ratio, and summary receiver operating characteristic curves, including a subgroup analysis restricted to adult populations. The meta-analysis found that the immature platelet fraction demonstrated only moderate diagnostic accuracy for sepsis, with pooled sensitivity and specificity both around the low-to-mid seventies percent range and moderate heterogeneity among the included studies. The authors conclude that while the immature platelet fraction may serve as a complementary biomarker in sepsis identification, the small number of eligible studies and methodological variability limit confidence in the pooled estimates. They call for further large-scale, standardized prospective studies to clarify its clinical utility.

Diagnostic performance of interleukin-27 and C-reactive protein in neonatal sepsis: an updated systematic review and meta-analysis.

Liu W, Zhang Y, Liu W et al.. Diagnostic performance of interleukin-27 and C-reactive protein in neonatal sepsis: an updated systematic review and meta-analysis.. Frontiers in pediatrics. 2026

This systematic review and meta-analysis evaluated and compared the diagnostic accuracy of interleukin-27 (IL-27) and C-reactive protein (CRP) as biomarkers for neonatal sepsis, a condition where existing evidence had been inconsistent. Following PRISMA guidelines, the authors searched major medical databases for controlled studies published over nearly three decades, ultimately including five studies encompassing nearly five hundred neonates. Pooled analysis demonstrated that IL-27 achieved higher sensitivity and specificity than CRP for diagnosing neonatal sepsis, with summary receiver operating characteristic curve analysis confirming that IL-27 had a notably greater area under the curve than CRP. The authors conclude that IL-27 exhibits superior diagnostic accuracy compared to CRP and may serve as a more reliable biomarker for the early and accurate identification of neonatal sepsis in clinical settings.

Aligning neonatal sepsis triage diagnostics with WHO target product profiles in low- and middle-income countries.

Gleeson B, Blumel B, Ibarz Pavon AB et al.. Aligning neonatal sepsis triage diagnostics with WHO target product profiles in low- and middle-income countries.. BMJ global health. 2026

This paper examined the landscape of diagnostic tools for neonatal sepsis in low- and middle-income countries, where the condition remains a major driver of infant death despite limited diagnostic progress. The authors synthesized evidence across a range of current and emerging diagnostic approaches, including clinical algorithms, host-response biomarkers, haematological parameters, continuous monitoring, and artificial intelligence-based digital platforms, evaluating each against the WHO Target Product Profile for neonatal sepsis triage. They assessed not only diagnostic performance but also operational feasibility and suitability for resource-constrained and non-hospital settings. The analysis identified several promising technologies, including host-response biomarkers with greater accuracy than those currently in use and affordable tools capable of enhancing existing predictive algorithms. The authors conclude that an integrated, WHO-aligned strategy combining affordable point-of-care testing, routine clinical data, and digital decision support offers the most viable path toward earlier detection, more rational antibiotic use, and reduced neonatal mortality in these settings.

Calprotectin as a Diagnostic Marker for Lower Respiratory Tract Infection and Sepsis in the Emergency Department.

Wollmer M, Wessman T, Melander O et al.. Calprotectin as a Diagnostic Marker for Lower Respiratory Tract Infection and Sepsis in the Emergency Department.. Open forum infectious diseases. 2026

This study investigated whether calprotectin, a neutrophil activation marker measured in plasma, could serve as a useful diagnostic biomarker for sepsis and for identifying the source of infection in patients presenting to an emergency department. Researchers prospectively enrolled patients with suspected sepsis at a university hospital, measuring calprotectin at admission alongside established markers such as C-reactive protein. Calprotectin showed modest ability to distinguish sepsis from non-infectious systemic inflammatory response syndrome, but was not an independent predictor when combined with C-reactive protein in multivariable analysis. Notably, calprotectin demonstrated particular value in differentiating lower respiratory tract infections from both upper respiratory tract infections and urinary tract infections, and adding calprotectin to C-reactive protein significantly improved discriminatory performance for these comparisons. The authors conclude that calprotectin may enhance identification of lower respiratory tract infection source in sepsis presentations, though further research is needed to determine its role in routine clinical assessment.

Surviving Sepsis Campaign: International Guidelines for Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock 2026

Hallie C. Prescott, Massimo Antonelli, Waleed Alhazzani et al.. Surviving Sepsis Campaign: International Guidelines for Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock 2026. Critical Care Medicine. 2026

This paper presents the 2026 Surviving Sepsis Campaign international guidelines for the management of sepsis and septic shock in adult patients. The guidelines were developed by a large, geographically diverse committee organized into clinical subgroups covering screening, infection, hemodynamics, respiratory support, adjunctive therapies, and goals of care, using the GRADE methodology and an Evidence to Decision framework to formulate recommendations. The scope encompasses hospital, immediate prehospital, and immediate post-hospital settings, with explicit incorporation of antimicrobial stewardship principles including appropriate diagnostic strategies and de-escalation of therapy. Recommendations range from strong to conditional depending on the certainty of evidence and the balance of desirable versus undesirable effects, with particular attention to applicability in low- and middle-income country contexts. The guidelines aim to translate a large body of research into actionable clinical guidance to improve early identification, treatment, and outcomes for the millions of patients affected by sepsis globally each year.

The NLRP3 Inflammasome: Mechanisms of Activation, Regulation, and Therapeutic Opportunities

Chan Zou, Shilong Jiang, Hui Li et al.. The NLRP3 Inflammasome: Mechanisms of Activation, Regulation, and Therapeutic Opportunities. MedComm. 2026

This review paper examines the NLRP3 inflammasome, a key signaling complex of the innate immune system that detects a wide range of microbial, metabolic, and environmental danger signals. The authors synthesize current knowledge on the molecular mechanisms driving NLRP3 activation, including ion flux, mitochondrial damage, lysosomal rupture, reactive oxygen species, and post-translational modifications, as well as the negative regulatory pathways that keep inflammasome activity in check. Activation of this complex triggers recruitment of ASC and caspase-1, leading to maturation of pro-inflammatory cytokines and pyroptotic cell death, processes linked to autoinflammatory, metabolic, cardiovascular, neurodegenerative, and oncological diseases when dysregulated. The review also surveys emerging therapeutic strategies targeting NLRP3, encompassing direct inhibitors, allosteric modulators, biologics, and repurposed drugs, and evaluates their translational potential. The authors conclude that a better understanding of the balance between NLRP3 activation and inhibition could unlock new therapeutic avenues for a broad spectrum of inflammatory and immune-related conditions.

A systematic literature review on the effects of exercise on human Toll-like receptor expression

Kasper Favere, Matthias Bosman, Peter Delputte et al.. A systematic literature review on the effects of exercise on human Toll-like receptor expression. PubMed. 2026

This systematic review examined how different forms of exercise affect the expression of Toll-like receptors, a family of immune cell receptors that trigger inflammatory signalling cascades and have been implicated in the chronic low-grade inflammation associated with sedentary lifestyles and non-communicable diseases. The authors searched multiple databases for human studies in which exercise was the sole intervention, ultimately including studies categorised by exercise type and duration, spanning acute and chronic resistance, aerobic, and combined training modalities. Acute resistance exercise tended to upregulate TLR expression, while acute aerobic exercise showed a predominantly neutral or downregulatory effect, particularly in healthy individuals. Chronic resistance and combined training programs were mostly associated with unchanged or decreased TLR levels, whereas chronic aerobic training produced mixed results with the majority of measurements showing no significant change. The authors conclude that exercise interacts with TLR signalling in a manner dependent on exercise characteristics, but highlight that substantial heterogeneity in study designs limits firm conclusions and calls for further standardised research to clarify the underlying mechanisms and pathophysiological implications.

Exosomal bioactive molecules: molecular regulation, functional heterogeneity, and translational implications in disease diagnostics and therapeutics

Victor U. Chigozie. Exosomal bioactive molecules: molecular regulation, functional heterogeneity, and translational implications in disease diagnostics and therapeutics. Clinical and Experimental Medicine. 2026

This review paper examines the biology, diagnostic utility, and therapeutic potential of exosomes, which are nanoscale extracellular vesicles that mediate intercellular communication by shuttling bioactive cargo including proteins, lipids, and nucleic acids. The authors synthesize current understanding of the molecular machinery governing exosome biogenesis and selective cargo loading, with particular attention to the roles of ESCRT complexes, lipid domains, RNA-binding proteins, and post-translational modifications in generating exosomal heterogeneity. The review also covers state-of-the-art technologies and omics platforms used to characterize exosomal content, and evaluates the clinical performance of exosome-based liquid biopsies for cancer, metabolic, and neurodegenerative diseases. Therapeutic applications discussed include both native and engineered exosomes for targeted drug delivery, wound healing, and neuroregeneration, alongside insights from ongoing clinical trials. The authors identify persistent translational barriers such as isolation protocol variability and regulatory constraints, while highlighting emerging solutions including synthetic exosome mimetics and artificial intelligence–driven diagnostics as avenues toward exosome-based precision medicine.

Validating LLM-Modernized Scientific Software Through Differential Fault Injection

Evan Coleman, Yuzhong Shen, Masha Sosonkina et al.. Validating LLM-Modernized Scientific Software Through Differential Fault Injection. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper examined whether large language model agents that modernize legacy Fortran scientific software preserve not just nominal computational behavior but also the code's response to faults, perturbations, and reduced numerical precision. The researchers developed a differential fault-injection validation framework that instruments a shared computational driver at multiple sites and applies identical, deterministic faults to both original and modernized code, allowing direct comparison of how each version responds. Applied to integral kernels within a quantum chemistry software package across thousands of runs, the method characterized how transient and persistent faults propagate through self-consistent-field iterations, finding that fault absorption costs follow a predictable contraction-based model and that reduced precision can induce phase-dependent parallel deadlocks and false convergence. Critically, the original and LLM-modernized kernels agreed across all paired fault-injection experiments, and a synchronization fix guided by the campaign's measurements remained compatible with the modernization. The work demonstrates that differential fault injection can reveal subtle behavioral differences that nominal testing would miss, while also providing evidence that LLM-driven code modernization can faithfully preserve fault-response characteristics of legacy scientific software.

The Okinawa Lectures on Entropy

Klaas Landsman. The Okinawa Lectures on Entropy. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper presents a comprehensive set of lecture notes on entropy in both classical and quantum settings, aimed at readers with backgrounds in both mathematics and theoretical physics. The treatment begins with classical probability theory and large deviation theory — including results from Sanov, Cramér, Gärtner-Ellis, and Varadhan — and applies these to statistical physics in both Boltzmannian and Gibbsian frameworks. Quantum entropies, including those of Umegaki and their Rényi deformations, are shown to connect to classical counterparts through their shared role in statistical hypothesis testing. The notes then develop the more technically demanding problem of defining relative entropy for states on subsystems described by von Neumann algebras, requiring modular theory and the relative entropies of Araki and Uhlmann, as well as Haagerup's noncommutative spaces for explicit computation. Finally, the course covers a quantum analogue of Kolmogorov-Sinai dynamical entropy in the setting of von Neumann algebras, with the stated motivation being relevance to black hole thermodynamics, though that application itself is not discussed.

Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation

Zhelun Wu. Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper argues that systems using language models to draw conclusions from multiple sources make a fundamental design error by conflating two distinct operations: interpreting individual sources and aggregating those interpretations into a final decision. To address this, the authors propose a structured four-field evidence tuple that enforces a clean interface between these two stages, and they analyze a failure mode they term "count-scale drift," wherein thresholding aggregated unnormalized weights produces an operating point that shifts as the number of sources grows. They demonstrate that pooling calibrated log-likelihood ratios resolves this problem through arithmetic rather than architectural changes, and show the fix generalizes beyond language models to other score-summing systems such as diagnostic panels and multi-signal detectors. The framework is instantiated on a longitudinal corpus in two temporal settings—after and before outcome resolution—where the proposed separation yields strong predictive performance on a survival task, substantially outperforming a hand-crafted baseline. The authors also explicitly delineate what components transfer across domains from those requiring re-estimation, and enumerate falsifiable predictions and known negative results to bound the framework's claims.

RegRole: Regularized Role Detection and Prediction in Temporal Dynamic Networks

Emily J Evans, Weihong Guo, Carlotta Domenicon. RegRole: Regularized Role Detection and Prediction in Temporal Dynamic Networks. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of identifying and tracking roles that network nodes play over time in temporal dynamic networks. The authors developed a method based on temporally regularized Non-negative Matrix Factorization that produces a consistent set of roles across all time periods along with a universal transition matrix describing the probability of moving between roles, while a regularization penalty discourages abrupt changes in role membership between consecutive time steps. The approach was evaluated on multiple real-world and synthetic datasets using both engineered and automatically generated features. Results indicate that with appropriate regularization, the method reduces role prediction errors compared to existing techniques, and analysis of the transition matrices reveals a more stable system in which individuals tend to remain in their roles rather than undergoing arbitrary transitions. The model is also reported to scale efficiently to large and sparse graphs.

Twin: Playing an Unknown Game with a Test-Time Digital Twin

Alexy Skoutnev, Kirill Acharya, Gaston Longhitano et al.. Twin: Playing an Unknown Game with a Test-Time Digital Twin. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper presents Twin, a system designed to play unknown grid-based games by having a frontier coding agent construct and iteratively refine an executable world model at test time, without any prior knowledge of the game's rules or goals. Rather than relying on hand-engineered models, Twin infers game transitions and objectives purely through simulation and interaction, enforcing that its world model accurately reproduces every previously observed game transition before committing to an action, and using prediction mismatches as counterexamples to repair the model. The system was evaluated on a continual learning benchmark of grid games, where it achieved very high completion rates and surpassed human action efficiency on the majority of levels it cleared. A key finding is that the system can infer the game's goal before receiving any reward signal on most levels, with automatic search as a fallback, and that constructing a usable world model proved more tractable than anticipated. The authors conclude that the primary difficulty lies not in building the world model itself but in correctly identifying the goal of the game.

Isomorphism of tournaments with bounded VC dimension

Simon Raßmann, Pascal Schweitzer. Isomorphism of tournaments with bounded VC dimension. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigates the computational complexity of determining whether two tournaments (complete directed graphs) are isomorphic, a problem that represents a core bottleneck in graph isomorphism research. The authors focus on tournaments of bounded VC dimension, a class for which no prior algorithmic results existed, resolving an open problem by demonstrating that isomorphism can be decided in quasi-polynomial time parameterized by the VC dimension, with automorphism groups computable in polynomial time for bounded VC dimension. To achieve this, they develop a new isomorphism-invariant decomposition method for tournaments, introduce the concept of a patched tournament to enable recursion, and design a recursive algorithm that exploits near-twin structure while balancing piece sizes against their number. In a complementary direction, they also address the class of tournaments with bounded chromatic number—which captures all 2-colorable tournaments and thus characterizes hereditary classes of unbounded VC dimension—proving that isomorphism testing for this class is likewise decidable in polynomial time. Together, these results establish that neither bounded VC dimension nor bounded chromatic number forms an obstruction to efficient isomorphism testing for tournaments.

Rapid diagnostics innovations for urinary tract infections using molecular biology, artificial intelligence and antimicrobial resistance surveillance: a comprehensive review.

Goyal S, Sharma P, Aggarwal D et al.. Rapid diagnostics innovations for urinary tract infections using molecular biology, artificial intelligence and antimicrobial resistance surveillance: a comprehensive review.. Molecular biology reports. 2026

This review examines the current landscape and emerging innovations in diagnosing urinary tract infections, motivated by the clinical burden these infections impose through high recurrence, microbial diversity, biofilm formation, and rapidly spreading antimicrobial resistance. The authors survey a range of advanced molecular diagnostic technologies, including nucleic acid amplification techniques, biosensor-based platforms, microfluidic lab-on-chip systems, and omics-based approaches, positioning these as faster and more precise alternatives to traditional culture-based methods that suffer from slow turnaround times and limited resolution of resistance and virulence factors. A central focus is the integration of artificial intelligence and machine learning models with these molecular platforms to enable rapid pathogen identification and antibiotic susceptibility profiling, thereby supporting targeted rather than empirical antibiotic therapy. The review highlights translational applications of these combined technologies for improving diagnostic accuracy, guiding antimicrobial stewardship, and enhancing surveillance of antimicrobial resistance. Challenges surrounding medical ethics, regulatory frameworks, and practical implementation are also discussed as important considerations for clinical adoption.

Evaluation of an adapted relative growth method by MALDI-TOF MS for rapid determination of the susceptibility of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt; to levofloxacin.

Liu Y, Xie N, Hu W et al.. Evaluation of an adapted relative growth method by MALDI-TOF MS for rapid determination of the susceptibility of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt; to levofloxacin.. Microbiology spectrum. 2026

This study addressed the need for faster and more accurate antimicrobial susceptibility testing (AST) in the context of growing global antimicrobial resistance, focusing on *Escherichia coli* and levofloxacin. The researchers developed a platform called AI MedLab MS, which combines dynamic MALDI-TOF mass spectrometry spectral analysis over time with a robust dynamic relative growth algorithm and machine learning, rather than relying on single-timepoint or single-colony measurements. This approach captures the evolving mass spectral profiles of bacterial populations exposed to the antibiotic, thereby accounting for intrasample heterogeneity and resistant subpopulations that static methods may miss. The platform was developed on clinical isolates and prospectively validated on an independent set, delivering susceptibility results within two hours with strong overall accuracy and notably high precision for resistant isolates. The authors conclude that dynamic trajectory modeling of bacterial populations represents a meaningful advance over conventional endpoint-based AST, offering potential clinical utility for guiding timely and targeted treatment decisions.

ERS/ESCMID state-of-the-science statement on the role of rapid diagnostic tests for the diagnosis and management of community-acquired lower respiratory tract infections.

Polverino E, De Nardo P, Gentilotti E et al.. ERS/ESCMID state-of-the-science statement on the role of rapid diagnostic tests for the diagnosis and management of community-acquired lower respiratory tract infections.. The European respiratory journal. 2026

This paper presents a state-of-the-science statement from two major European respiratory and infectious disease societies examining the role of rapid diagnostic tests in managing community-acquired lower respiratory tract infections. Produced within the framework of a European Union–funded project aimed at combatting antimicrobial resistance, the work systematically reviews existing literature and current clinical practice to assess how such tests can support antibiotic stewardship. The statement evaluates the diagnostic accuracy of rapid tests for identifying causative pathogens, the utility of biomarkers and clinical decision algorithms, and how the introduction of rapid diagnostics has shifted understanding of infection aetiology. It also examines whether incorporating these tests into stewardship protocols leads to improved clinical outcomes and more appropriate antibiotic use. The overarching conclusion positions rapid diagnostic tests as potentially valuable tools for reducing unnecessary antimicrobial prescribing and curbing the emergence of multidrug resistance in respiratory infections.

Antibiotic resistance in &lt;i&gt;Pseudomonas aeruginosa&lt;/i&gt;: mechanisms, diagnostic challenges, and omics-based diagnostic solutions.

Rahman MS, Wakeman CA. Antibiotic resistance in &lt;i&gt;Pseudomonas aeruginosa&lt;/i&gt;: mechanisms, diagnostic challenges, and omics-based diagnostic solutions.. Frontiers in microbiology. 2026

This paper reviews antibiotic resistance in *Pseudomonas aeruginosa*, a multidrug-resistant opportunistic pathogen responsible for severe infections in immunocompromised individuals and a major contributor to the global antimicrobial resistance crisis. The authors examined the diverse mechanisms by which this organism evades antibiotics, including efflux pump overexpression, porin modification, enzymatic inactivation, target site alterations, and phenotypic adaptations such as biofilm and persister cell formation, all of which have driven the emergence of multidrug-resistant, extensively drug-resistant, and pan-drug-resistant strains. The paper identifies critical limitations of conventional culture-based diagnostics, particularly their slowness and inability to detect heteroresistance and biofilm-associated tolerance, which delay effective treatment. In response, the authors survey omics-based approaches spanning genomics, transcriptomics, proteomics, metabolomics, and related fields, alongside microfluidic platforms and machine learning-driven artificial intelligence, as more rapid and precise diagnostic alternatives. The authors conclude that integrating multiple omics technologies with advanced diagnostic platforms represents a transformative strategy for comprehensive resistance profiling, though standardized protocols, clinical validation, and cost-effective implementation remain necessary to translate this potential into clinical practice.

Preliminary Single-Center Evaluation of the Alifax Molecular Mouse Sepsis Panel for Rapid Detection of Bloodstream Pathogens.

Han JH, Kim JK, Kim JO et al.. Preliminary Single-Center Evaluation of the Alifax Molecular Mouse Sepsis Panel for Rapid Detection of Bloodstream Pathogens.. Diagnostics (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance of the Alifax Molecular Mouse sepsis panel for detecting bloodstream pathogens and antimicrobial resistance genes directly from positive blood culture broths. In a prospective single-center study, positive blood culture specimens were analyzed using the panel's cartridges targeting Gram-positive and Gram-negative bacteria and resistance genes, with results compared against conventional microbiological methods including MALDI-TOF identification and automated susceptibility testing, as well as the FilmArray BCID2 panel. The Molecular Mouse panel demonstrated moderate-to-good agreement with routine protocols for both bacterial identification and resistance gene detection, and showed comparable performance to the FilmArray BCID2. Notably, the panel generated clinically actionable results within approximately one hour, representing a substantial reduction in turnaround time compared to the conventional workflow, which requires one to two days. The authors conclude that the panel holds potential as a rapid adjunct to routine microbiological workflows for managing bloodstream infections.

Novel Rapid Phenotypic Susceptibility Testing Techniques That Enhance Antimicrobial Stewardship and Clinical Decision-Making.

Glover JS, Humphries R. Novel Rapid Phenotypic Susceptibility Testing Techniques That Enhance Antimicrobial Stewardship and Clinical Decision-Making.. Clinical chemistry. 2026

This review examined emerging rapid phenotypic antimicrobial susceptibility testing technologies and their role in addressing the global challenge of antimicrobial resistance. The authors surveyed the fundamentals of phenotypic approaches, with particular attention to growth-based methods and their clinical and operational implications. Evidence was synthesized demonstrating that these techniques can substantially reduce the time to effective therapy compared with conventional testing methods, which is especially critical in severe infections such as sepsis where treatment delays worsen outcomes. The review also addressed ongoing implementation challenges, including variability in test performance, absence of standardized protocols, and cost-benefit considerations. Overall, the authors conclude that rapid phenotypic susceptibility testing represents a meaningful advance in supporting antimicrobial stewardship and enabling more timely, evidence-based clinical decision-making.

Executive Summary: Surviving Sepsis Campaign: International Guidelines for Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock 2026

Hallie C. Prescott, Massimo Antonelli, Waleed Alhazzani et al.. Executive Summary: Surviving Sepsis Campaign: International Guidelines for Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock 2026. Critical Care Medicine. 2026

The 2026 Surviving Sepsis Campaign guidelines represent a comprehensive update to international recommendations for the identification and management of sepsis and septic shock in adult patients, incorporating evidence published through mid-2025. The guidelines were developed using GRADE methodology, encompassing screening, antimicrobial therapy, hemodynamic management, respiratory support, adjunctive therapies, and long-term outcomes across a total of statements that include strong recommendations, conditional recommendations, good practice statements, and acknowledgments of insufficient evidence. Notable new guidance includes conditional recommendations for prehospital sepsis screening and antibiotic administration for patients with prolonged transport times, age-stratified mean arterial pressure targets, selective digestive tract decontamination in low-resistance settings, and active fluid removal following the acute resuscitation phase. Key changes from the 2021 guidelines include a shift away from empirical antifungal therapy for most patients, updated guidance allowing either invasive or noninvasive blood pressure monitoring, and a preference for crystalloids alone over supplemental albumin for fluid resuscitation in most circumstances. Several recommendations were also upgraded in strength, including prolonged beta-lactam infusion and antimicrobial de-escalation, while others such as corticosteroid use and norepinephrine as first-line vasopressor were downgraded in certainty or strength based on updated evidence appraisal.

A comprehensive review of green synthesis methods and applications of nanoparticles derived from plant extracts and microorganisms

Shoaeb Mohammad Syed, Shivam Kulkarni, Mayur Patil et al.. A comprehensive review of green synthesis methods and applications of nanoparticles derived from plant extracts and microorganisms. Discover Green Chemistry. 2026

This review examines the field of green synthesis, which uses biological entities such as plants, bacteria, fungi, algae, and yeast as natural reducing and stabilizing agents to produce nanoparticles without toxic chemicals or harsh reaction conditions. The authors surveyed literature across multiple major scientific databases to critically assess advances in plant-mediated and microorganism-assisted nanoparticle fabrication, covering the mechanisms of particle formation, the role of biological constituents in determining particle size, morphology, and stability, and common characterization approaches. The functional applications of these biologically synthesized nanoparticles across biomedical, agricultural, catalytic, and environmental domains are evaluated, including their potential for photocatalytic degradation of pollutants. The review identifies persistent challenges surrounding reproducibility, standardization, and scalability that currently limit the translation of green nanotechnology from laboratory settings to industrial practice. Overall, the paper positions green synthesis as a viable and environmentally sustainable alternative to conventional nanoparticle production methods while outlining the research gaps that must be addressed for practical implementation.

Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration

Graphene oxide as smart sustainable nanomaterial: a versatile multifunctional material with transformative potential in advanced materials science research

CPI-Bench: A Comprehensive,Practical and Intelligent Benchmark for Real-World Image Editing

Qinye Zhou, Jun Zheng, Yongchao Du et al.. CPI-Bench: A Comprehensive,Practical and Intelligent Benchmark for Real-World Image Editing. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the limitations of existing image editing benchmarks, which are confined to simple single-image tasks and fail to differentiate performance across diverse models or capture real-world complexity. The authors propose CPI-Bench, a new benchmark comprising three subsets targeting general editing diversity (including multi-image editing), high-frequency practical user scenarios, and reasoning-intensive editing tasks. The benchmark was used to evaluate mainstream image editing models, revealing clearer performance differentiation and more reliable quantification of capability gaps across the three dimensions than prior benchmarks afford. Notably, ranking results from CPI-Bench show the highest alignment with a human-preference leaderboard, suggesting it faithfully reflects real-world user judgments and can serve as a robust proxy for practical deployment evaluation.

Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation

Zhelun Wu. Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper argues that systems using language models to draw conclusions from multiple sources conflate two distinct operations—interpreting individual sources and aggregating those interpretations—which have fundamentally different computational requirements. The authors propose separating these operations via a structured four-field evidence tuple that fixes the interface between interpretation and aggregation. They identify a failure mode called count-scale drift, wherein thresholding summed unnormalized weights produces an operating point that shifts with the number of sources examined, and show that neither vote rules nor posterior ordering can be reconciled by a single threshold when source reliabilities vary. Pooling calibrated log-likelihood ratios is proposed as an arithmetic rather than architectural fix applicable broadly to score-summing systems beyond language models, including diagnostic panels and additive multi-signal detectors. The framework is validated on a longitudinal corpus in both retrospective and prospective settings, where a small sequence encoder combined with a tree ensemble carrying a censored survival loss substantially outperforms a hand-crafted baseline, and the authors explicitly delineate transferable components from domain-specific ones while stating falsifiable predictions and acknowledged limitations.

Residual-Controlled Douglas--Rachford Splitting for Differentiable Solver Layers

Kang Liu, Jianchen Hu. Residual-Controlled Douglas--Rachford Splitting for Differentiable Solver Layers. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of embedding constrained optimization solvers into end-to-end differentiable learning systems, where fixed-depth unrolling forces difficult trade-offs between solution quality, feasibility, and computational cost. The authors propose Residual-Controlled Douglas–Rachford Splitting, a differentiable solver layer for conic linear programs that treats the unrolled solver as a feedback-controlled dynamical system, allowing a causal controller to adaptively tune relaxation and objective-drive parameters while preserving the projection-splitting structure. Theoretically, they establish that each admissible block remains an averaged relaxed operator with finite-step fixed-point residual bounds, and they analyze time-varying rollouts as summable perturbations of a limiting averaged operator to recover primal-dual diagnostics. Experiments on mixed-cone benchmarks and engineering applications demonstrate improvements in solution quality, feasibility, and downstream decision performance compared to fixed-depth unrolling approaches.

PACE-Bench: Benchmarking Physics Adaptation via Code Evolution in Dynamic Environments

Yuhao Zhan, Bingxiang He, Zecong Tang et al.. PACE-Bench: Benchmarking Physics Adaptation via Code Evolution in Dynamic Environments. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a benchmark designed to evaluate how well self-evolving AI agents can adapt previously successful code-based designs to environments where the underlying physics have changed. The benchmark consists of source-to-target adaptation pairs spanning six physics domains, where a design that works in the source environment fails in the mutated target, and agents must iteratively revise their solutions using sandbox feedback within a limited budget. Ten self-evolving methods from four paradigms were compared, revealing that the benchmark is far from solved and that simulator-grounded reflection outperforms unverified self-revision, while memory-based approaches anchor agents to early designs and broad tree search fails to converge. Notably, even providing agents with exact information about the physical changes did not substantially raise performance, indicating that the core challenge lies in redesigning mechanisms rather than inferring altered parameters.

A Survey of Large Models in Sports

Yichen Xu, Jianzhe Ma, Chuhan Wang et al.. A Survey of Large Models in Sports. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper presents a comprehensive survey examining the application of large models, particularly multimodal large language models, within the domain of sports. The authors systematically reviewed how these models are being used across diverse sports-related tasks and applications, covering different participant groups such as athletes, coaches, and fans. The survey catalogues and analyzes sports-specific datasets and benchmarks that have been developed to support this research area. The authors also critically discuss the current challenges facing the field and outline promising future research directions. The paper aims to serve as a foundational reference for researchers and practitioners seeking to advance large-model-driven intelligence in sports contexts.

The More Popular, The Harder to Forget: Adaptive Popularity for LLM Unlearning

Anna Borisiuk, Andrey Savchenko, Alexander Panchenko et al.. The More Popular, The Harder to Forget: Adaptive Popularity for LLM Unlearning. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates why popular facts are more resistant to removal during large language model unlearning, attributing the difficulty to deeper memorization during pretraining due to higher training-data frequency. The authors propose AdaPop, a method that scales gradient pressure according to a fact's popularity using external proxies and local token confidence, while automating the balance between forgetting and retention through a dual-ascent controller. Tested across multiple model families and benchmarks, AdaPop substantially reduces leakage of supposedly forgotten content under both paraphrased and adversarial queries compared to competing methods. Internal representational analyses confirm that the method pushes hidden states of forgotten content further from their pre-unlearning positions while preserving the representations of retained knowledge.

Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms

From bench to bedside: the promise and roadblocks of extracellular vesicle therapeutics

Jing Zhang, Yuanwei Pan, Peng She et al.. From bench to bedside: the promise and roadblocks of extracellular vesicle therapeutics. Theranostics. 2026

This review examines the current state of extracellular vesicle-based therapeutics, focusing on what prevents these promising agents from advancing from preclinical research into clinical use. Zinc finger protein arrays in an epoxy-functionalized, pump-free microfluidic device for amplification-free, colorimetric detection of double-stranded DNA.

Bhuiyan SAM, Shumway R, Trinkle C et al.. Zinc finger protein arrays in an epoxy-functionalized, pump-free microfluidic device for amplification-free, colorimetric detection of double-stranded DNA.. The Analyst. 2026

This study developed a microfluidic platform integrating engineered zinc finger proteins to detect pathogen-specific double-stranded DNA without amplification or labeling. The researchers designed zinc finger proteins targeting the *stx2* gene of *E. coli* O157 and covalently bound them to epoxy-functionalized surfaces within a pump-free, capillary-driven microfluidic device, coupling detection to sequence-enabled reassembly of β-lactamase for colorimetric readout. The device produces a visible color shift upon target binding and enzyme reconstitution, achieving sensitive and selective detection at low picomolar concentrations with specificity confirmed against non-target sequences. The capillary-driven flow followed predictable Lucas-Washburn behavior, and the assay operated at ambient temperature with no need for DNA denaturation, hybridization, or amplification, producing an instrument-free readout in approximately ten minutes. The authors conclude that this approach provides a low-cost, practical framework for translating programmable protein-DNA recognition into point-of-care pathogen detection directly from double-stranded DNA samples.

Microfluidic and Paper-Based Recombinase Polymerase Amplification Systems for Decentralized Diagnostics and Biosurveillance.

Wang HM, Lee SZ, Fu LM. Microfluidic and Paper-Based Recombinase Polymerase Amplification Systems for Decentralized Diagnostics and Biosurveillance.. Micromachines. 2026

This review examines the integration of recombinase polymerase amplification, an isothermal nucleic acid amplification method that operates at mild temperatures without the complex thermal cycling required by PCR, with microfluidic and paper-based platforms for decentralized molecular diagnostics. The authors surveyed recent progress across a broad range of device architectures, including centrifugal chips, capillary-driven systems, paper-based analytical devices, electrochemical biosensors, CRISPR-coupled assays, and sample-to-answer cartridges, applied to biomedical diagnostics, food safety, environmental monitoring, and One Health biosurveillance. The review highlights how pairing these amplification chemistries with compact, low-power formats enables detection of respiratory viruses, bacterial and parasitic pathogens, antimicrobial resistance markers, agricultural diseases, and cancer-related biomarkers, among other targets. Emerging trends include the incorporation of smartphone imaging, artificial intelligence-assisted result interpretation, digital partitioning, and cloud connectivity to enhance portability, consistency, and analytical decision-making. The authors conclude that while the field is advancing rapidly, challenges related to sample preparation, multiplexing, reagent storage, quantitative reliability, scalable manufacturing, and regulatory validation must still be addressed before widespread field deployment can be realized.

Single-atom nanozyme-based wireless microfluidic sensing platform for noninvasive hyperuricemia diagnosis.

Zhao J, Zheng J, Wei K et al.. Single-atom nanozyme-based wireless microfluidic sensing platform for noninvasive hyperuricemia diagnosis.. Biosensors & bioelectronics. 2026

The paper investigated non-invasive electrochemical detection of uric acid for diagnosing hyperuricemia, a condition linked to gout, kidney damage, and cardiovascular disease. The researchers synthesized single-atom platinum catalysts deposited on reduced graphene oxide/metal-organic framework composites via photochemical deposition, then integrated these materials into a portable wireless microfluidic electrochemical sensing platform with a hydrophilic surface-modified microfluidic device to improve biofluid collection. The resulting platform achieved a broad linear detection range and low detection limit for uric acid, with high selectivity and reproducibility. Validation studies using serum and saliva samples from hyperuricemia patients and healthy volunteers demonstrated a meaningful correlation between the two biofluids, supporting the platform's clinical utility for non-invasive point-of-care testing.

Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration

Thirumalaisamy Suryaprabha, Chunghyeon Choi, Yanfang Wu et al.. Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration. npj Flexible Electronics. 2026

This review examines smart wearable and implantable biosensors designed for continuous, real-time monitoring of biophysical and biochemical signals in the context of personalized and preventive healthcare. The paper surveys advances in flexible, stretchable, and biocompatible materials that support long-term comfort and integration with the body, as well as multimodal and multi-analyte sensing approaches that improve measurement robustness. The role of artificial intelligence in enhancing signal processing and generating predictive health insights is also assessed. Despite these advances, the review identifies persistent challenges including motion artifacts, energy autonomy, data privacy, and the clinical interpretability of sensor outputs. Overall, the paper synthesizes current knowledge across materials, device architectures, and AI-assisted strategies to outline the state of the field.

Machine learning–empowered smartphone platforms for sustainable point-of-care bacterial detection

Exosomal bioactive molecules: molecular regulation, functional heterogeneity, and translational implications in disease diagnostics and therapeutics

Victor U. Chigozie. Exosomal bioactive molecules: molecular regulation, functional heterogeneity, and translational implications in disease diagnostics and therapeutics. Clinical and Experimental Medicine. 2026

This review examines exosomes, nanoscale extracellular vesicles that mediate intercellular communication by shuttling bioactive cargo including proteins, lipids, and nucleic acids, with a focus on the molecular mechanisms governing their biogenesis and functional diversity. The authors synthesize current understanding of how molecular machinery — including endosomal sorting complexes, lipid domains, RNA-binding proteins, and post-translational modifications — controls the selective loading of cargo into exosomes and drives their heterogeneity. The review also evaluates state-of-the-art omics platforms and machine learning tools used to characterize exosomal content for diagnostic applications, particularly in liquid biopsies for cancer, metabolic disorders, and neurodegenerative diseases. On the therapeutic side, the authors assess the potential of both native and engineered exosomes for targeted drug delivery, wound healing, and neuroregeneration, drawing on evidence from ongoing clinical trials. The review concludes by identifying persistent translational barriers such as isolation standardization and regulatory constraints, while proposing that emerging approaches including synthetic exosome mimetics and artificial intelligence-driven diagnostics will advance exosome-based precision medicine.

Comprehensive Review on Candidemia: Epidemiology, Diagnosis, Treatment, and Future Directions

Haojie Dong, Minghui Song, Jingfang Sun et al.. Comprehensive Review on Candidemia: Epidemiology, Diagnosis, Treatment, and Future Directions. iFungi. 2026

This review examines candidemia, a serious nosocomial bloodstream infection caused by Candida species, focusing on the persistent challenges that make it difficult to manage effectively in clinical settings. The authors surveyed the epidemiology and species distribution of candidemia, then critically evaluated current diagnostic methods and their limitations, including delays inherent to conventional approaches. The review covers therapeutic strategies ranging from empiric to targeted antifungal regimens, addressing mixed infections, host-drug interactions, and the growing problem of antifungal resistance, with particular attention to combination therapy as a potential countermeasure. Emerging diagnostic technologies, novel antifungal agents in development, and compounds targeting new fungal pathways are highlighted as promising advances. The review concludes by exploring future directions including artificial intelligence-assisted diagnostics, vaccine-based prophylaxis, and synergistic treatment strategies, with the goal of supporting clinicians and researchers in improving patient outcomes.

Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation

Zhelun Wu. Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper argues that systems using language models to draw conclusions from multiple sources incorrectly conflate two distinct operations: interpreting individual sources and aggregating those interpretations into a decision. The authors propose separating these operations and define a four-field evidence tuple as a structured interface between them, which they argue clarifies the design of both halves. They identify a failure mode they term "count-scale drift," in which thresholding a sum of unnormalized weights implicitly performs posterior thresholding at an operating point that shifts as the number of sources grows, and they show that vote rules and posterior rules order instances differently when source reliabilities vary, with no single threshold reconciling them. Pooling calibrated log-likelihood ratios is proposed as an arithmetic fix applicable beyond language models to score-summing triage engines, diagnostic panels, and additive multi-signal detectors. The framework is validated on a longitudinal corpus in two settings—post-outcome and pre-outcome—with the pre-outcome instantiation combining a small sequence encoder with a tree ensemble achieving substantially higher precision-recall performance than a hand-crafted baseline.

Residual-Controlled Douglas--Rachford Splitting for Differentiable Solver Layers

Kang Liu, Jianchen Hu. Residual-Controlled Douglas--Rachford Splitting for Differentiable Solver Layers. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of embedding constrained optimization solvers into end-to-end learning pipelines, where fixed-depth unrolling forces trade-offs between solution quality, feasibility, and computational cost. The authors propose Residual-Controlled Douglas–Rachford Splitting (RCDRS), a differentiable solver layer for conic linear programs that treats the unrolled solver as a feedback-controlled dynamical system. A causal controller dynamically adapts relaxation and objective-drive parameters during the unrolling process while preserving the projection-splitting structure of Douglas–Rachford splitting. Theoretical analysis establishes that each admissible block remains an averaged relaxed operator with finite-step fixed-point residual bounds, and safeguarded time-varying rollouts are analyzed as summable perturbations of a limiting averaged operator. Experiments on mixed-cone benchmarks and engineering applications demonstrate that RCDRS improves solution quality, feasibility, and downstream decision performance compared to fixed-depth unrolling approaches.

PACE-Bench: Benchmarking Physics Adaptation via Code Evolution in Dynamic Environments

Yuhao Zhan, Bingxiang He, Zecong Tang et al.. PACE-Bench: Benchmarking Physics Adaptation via Code Evolution in Dynamic Environments. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces PACE-Bench, a benchmark designed to evaluate how well self-evolving AI agents can adapt code-based solutions when the underlying physical environment changes. The benchmark comprises adaptation pairs spanning six physics domains, where each pair presents a source environment and a mutated target environment sharing the same goal and interface, requiring agents to iteratively revise a previously successful design using sandbox feedback within a limited attempt budget. Ten self-evolving methods representing four paradigms were evaluated, and none came close to saturating the benchmark, with the best-performing combination achieving success on only two-thirds of a subset. Key findings indicate that simulator-grounded reflection outperforms unverified self-revision, while memory mechanisms tend to anchor agents to early designs and broad tree search fails to converge on solutions. Notably, even providing agents with the exact physical changes does not substantially raise performance, suggesting that the central bottleneck is mechanism redesign rather than parameter inference.

PriCoRec: A Privacy-Aware Cloud-Device Collaborative Framework for Ad Recommendation under Feature Constraints

Dairui Liu, Zhongyi Lu, Jitao Lu et al.. PriCoRec: A Privacy-Aware Cloud-Device Collaborative Framework for Ad Recommendation under Feature Constraints. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the tension between privacy regulations that restrict cloud access to sensitive user data and the need for effective personalized advertising recommendations. The authors propose a cloud-device collaborative framework called PriCoRec, which splits the recommendation pipeline into a cloud-based pre-ranking stage using non-sensitive features and an on-device ranking stage that incorporates sensitive personal features locally. To overcome the quality degradation inherent in naive pipeline splitting, the framework introduces a diversity regularizer for the pre-ranking stage to improve candidate quality, alongside a cloud-guided training mechanism that keeps the on-device model lightweight while boosting its performance. Experiments demonstrate that the framework successfully preserves recommendation quality while ensuring sensitive user features never leave the user's device, offering a practical path to privacy-compliant ad recommendation at scale.

Large-scale workflow placement in serverless computing using integer nonlinear programming

Joshua Adamek, Natalie Carl, Trever Schirmer et al.. Large-scale workflow placement in serverless computing using integer nonlinear programming. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the problem of efficiently deploying large computational workflows across heterogeneous servers and edge devices in serverless edge computing environments, with the goal of minimizing both monetary costs and workflow execution times. The authors developed a mathematical framework to model workflow and cloud node characteristics, formulating the optimal placement problem as a nonlinear integer program. To handle the scalability challenges posed by large numbers of cloud and edge nodes, as well as scenarios where knowledge of node attributes is distributed rather than centralized, they introduced a novel decomposition strategy for solving the optimization problem. A case study demonstrated that this decomposition approach scales favorably to larger infrastructures and achieves meaningful improvements in placement quality compared to a simple deployment heuristic.

Native multi-qubit gates on a single-junction unimon circuit

Sasu Tuohino, Mikko Möttönen, Matti Silveri. Native multi-qubit gates on a single-junction unimon circuit. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper introduces a superconducting circuit architecture called the multiunimon, which encodes multiple qubits within a single Josephson junction embedded in a coplanar waveguide structure, enabling native multi-qubit gate operations. Building on the unimon qubit design, the multiunimon retains desirable properties including high anharmonicity and protection against low-frequency charge noise, while also offering partial protection against flux noise. The authors designed a three-qubit version of this device and employed a leakage-aware encoding scheme for computational states, then simulated all twelve variants of controlled-controlled-NOT gates using simple sine-squared pulses of comparable duration to single-qubit gates. The simulations achieved high mean gate fidelity, with performance primarily limited by incoherent errors from dielectric loss, and the authors suggest that further improvements in noise protection, device design, and pulse shaping could push fidelities close to fault-tolerant thresholds. The results position the multiunimon as a promising building block for highly connected superconducting quantum processors capable of efficient near-term algorithm implementation.

Graphene oxide as smart sustainable nanomaterial: a versatile multifunctional material with transformative potential in advanced materials science research

This review paper examines graphene oxide, a two-dimensional carbon-based nanomaterial with a distinctive layered architecture, as a multifunctional and sustainable platform for addressing challenges in energy, environmental remediation, and healthcare. The authors survey the synthesis chemistry and property modification strategies that underpin GO's tunable characteristics, including its adjustable band gap, high surface area, structural flexibility, and adaptive surface chemistry, which together enable configurations spanning zero-dimensional to three-dimensional nanostructures. Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms

Purshottam J. Assudani, Balakrishnan . P., Anny Leema A et al.. Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms. Microchimica Acta. 2026

This review examines the current state and future directions of biosensing technologies designed to detect priority foodborne pathogens, specifically focusing on platforms applicable to both food safety monitoring and clinical healthcare settings. The authors surveyed advances across electrochemical, optical, and microfluidic sensing modalities, considering how functional nanomaterials, aptamers, nanozymes, and surface engineering strategies have collectively improved sensor sensitivity and specificity in complex food matrices. A central theme is the integration of artificial intelligence and machine learning into these biosensing systems, enabling real-time analytics, intelligent pattern recognition, and high-throughput multiplexing that transform conventional detection tools into smart diagnostic devices. The review critically evaluates biosensor design principles, signal transduction mechanisms, and performance validation while identifying persistent challenges such as matrix interference, bioreceptor instability, manufacturing scalability, and the absence of regulatory standardization, alongside mitigation strategies including ratiometric sensing, microfluidic pre-treatment, and explainable AI. Looking ahead, the authors advocate for self-powered biosensors, modular multiplexed pathogen panels, blockchain-based data integrity, and standardized validation pipelines as pathways toward regulation-ready platforms for next-generation food safety monitoring.

Comprehensive Review on Candidemia: Epidemiology, Diagnosis, Treatment, and Future Directions

Haojie Dong, Minghui Song, Jingfang Sun et al.. Comprehensive Review on Candidemia: Epidemiology, Diagnosis, Treatment, and Future Directions. iFungi. 2026

This review examines candidemia, a serious bloodstream infection caused by *Candida* species that represents a major source of hospital-acquired infection with high mortality and significant healthcare burden. A Modular APEX Biosensor Enables Sensitive and Visualized Monitoring of Viral Protease Activity.

Li B, Dong B, Chen Y et al.. A Modular APEX Biosensor Enables Sensitive and Visualized Monitoring of Viral Protease Activity.. Virologica Sinica. 2026

The paper addresses the need for accurate intracellular screening platforms for viral protease inhibitors, noting that existing methods such as fluorescent protein assays and FRET are prone to non-specific conformational interference and fail to capture true intracellular inhibitory effects. The researchers designed two modular biosensors built around engineered ascorbate peroxidase (APEX), in which target protease cleavage of a recognition sequence triggers reassembly of split APEX fragments, restores enzymatic activity, and produces amplified fluorescent signals. Using the coronavirus main protease as a model system, one sensor achieved an exceptionally high signal-to-noise ratio for enzyme activity detection, while the second sensor reliably identified potent inhibitors and avoided the false positives that plague conventional approaches. By simply swapping the protease recognition sequence, the platform could be reconfigured to detect and evaluate inhibitors of proteases from multiple coronaviruses and other viruses, including enteroviruses and herpesviruses. The authors conclude that these biosensors provide a sensitive, specific, and adaptable intracellular tool to accelerate broad-spectrum antiviral drug discovery.

Multiple cross displacement amplification coupled with a lateral flow biosensor for ultra-rapid and highly sensitive detection of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt; in neonatal sepsis.

Xiao R, Zou P, Zhou J et al.. Multiple cross displacement amplification coupled with a lateral flow biosensor for ultra-rapid and highly sensitive detection of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt; in neonatal sepsis.. Microbiology spectrum. 2026

The study addressed the need for rapid and sensitive detection of *Escherichia coli* in neonatal sepsis, where conventional culture methods are too slow and insufficiently sensitive for timely clinical decision-making. The researchers developed a multiple cross-displacement amplification assay coupled with a lateral flow biosensor targeting the *uidA* gene, optimized to run at a fixed temperature for under an hour using a set of ten specific primers. The assay achieved femtogram-level analytical sensitivity without cross-reactivity against non-*E. coli* strains, and when validated on sterile body fluid specimens from neonates clinically suspected of *E. coli* infection, it showed complete agreement with quantitative PCR while identifying far more positive cases than conventional culture. The entire workflow from nucleic acid extraction to visual readout was completed within approximately one hour, requiring minimal equipment. The authors conclude that this method offers a rapid, sensitive, and cost-effective approach well suited to point-of-care and resource-limited settings for early diagnosis of neonatal *E. coli* sepsis.

Target-to-signal conversion and spatial enrichment cascade boost CRISPR/Cas12a biosensing for trace-level pathogen detection.

Liu Y, Yue X, Li B et al.. Target-to-signal conversion and spatial enrichment cascade boost CRISPR/Cas12a biosensing for trace-level pathogen detection.. Analytical methods : advancing methods and applications. 2026

This study addressed the need for rapid and sensitive detection of *Salmonella typhimurium*, a major cause of foodborne illness, by developing a CRISPR/Cas12a-based biosensor. The approach combined DNAzyme-catalyzed amplification with hybridization chain reaction to convert pathogen-specific targets into branched DNA nanowire structures bearing PAM sequences, which then activated Cas12a-mediated cleavage to restore a quenched fluorescent signal. This cascade strategy—linking target recognition, enzymatic signal conversion, and spatial enrichment through serial hairpin amplification—achieved an exceptionally low detection limit and a broad linear range spanning several orders of magnitude in bacterial concentration. The biosensor demonstrated straightforward operation and rapid response while maintaining high sensitivity and specificity. The authors further suggest that by swapping the recognition and primer modules, the platform could be adapted for the highly sensitive detection of other foodborne pathogens.

Simultaneous Dual-Gene Detection of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt; O157:H7 Based on a CRISPR/Cas13-Mediated Biosensor.

He Y, Lin X, Zhang X et al.. Simultaneous Dual-Gene Detection of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt; O157:H7 Based on a CRISPR/Cas13-Mediated Biosensor.. JACS Au. 2026

This study addressed the need for rapid, accurate detection of *Escherichia coli* O157:H7, a major foodborne pathogen, by developing a one-pot CRISPR/Cas13-based biosensor. The system simultaneously targets two key genetic markers of the pathogen by exploiting the distinct collateral cleavage preferences of two different Cas13 proteins, creating dual fluorescence channels that enable serotype-specific identification while reducing false positives. Target gene amplification via recombinase polymerase amplification, transcription, and CRISPR-mediated sensing were all integrated into a single reaction tube, eliminating complex equipment and contamination risks. The biosensor achieved high sensitivity in controlled tests and was validated using fecal samples from a mouse infection model, with results consistent with standard PCR methods. Overall, this dual-channel approach improves specificity and serotype discrimination compared to conventional CRISPR diagnostics, offering a simple and field-deployable tool for food safety, clinical diagnostics, and infectious disease surveillance.

Graphene foam biosensors for molecular diagnostics of antimicrobial resistance and emerging pathogen threats.

Wang KY, Schulze H, Lozano Sanchez P et al.. Graphene foam biosensors for molecular diagnostics of antimicrobial resistance and emerging pathogen threats.. Biosensors & bioelectronics. 2026

This paper developed an electrochemical biosensor platform designed for rapid, point-of-care detection of antimicrobial resistance genes and emerging pathogen targets. The researchers constructed disposable graphene foam electrodes functionalized with pyrene-modified peptide nucleic acid probes, using non-covalent π-π stacking to attach the probes without disrupting the electrode's electronic properties or surface area. A key innovation was a heat-assisted functionalization protocol that reduced sensor preparation time from many hours to just two minutes while maintaining detection performance. The platform achieved amplification-free and label-free detection of the colistin resistance gene and could be readily retargeted to detect a carbapenem resistance gene simply by swapping the probe sequence. The biosensors demonstrated consistent performance across independently fabricated devices, supporting their potential for scalable manufacturing and decentralized diagnostic deployment against antimicrobial resistance and pandemic threats.

Advanced biosensing strategies for high-risk foodborne pathogens: a comprehensive review of &lt;i&gt;Salmonella&lt;/i&gt; and &lt;i&gt;Listeria monocytogenes&lt;/i&gt;.
