This review examines advances in biosensor technologies specifically designed to detect two major foodborne pathogens, *Salmonella* spp. and *Listeria monocytogenes*, which are leading causes of foodborne illness and mortality globally. Rather than broadly surveying biosensing across many pathogens, the authors take a pathogen-specific approach, systematically mapping trends in bioreceptor development and transducer selection for these two organisms. A notable focus is placed on how artificial intelligence tools can accelerate biosensor design, optimization, and decision-making for detecting these particular pathogens, which the authors describe as a novel contribution to the literature. The review also critically evaluates which biosensing strategies hold genuine promise for scalability, portability, and practical deployment within the food industry, explicitly addressing the gap between laboratory-based research and real-world implementation. The authors position the review as a practical roadmap intended to guide future research toward field-ready and industry-relevant detection solutions.

From bench to bedside: the promise and roadblocks of extracellular vesicle therapeutics

Jing Zhang, Yuanwei Pan, Peng She et al.. From bench to bedside: the promise and roadblocks of extracellular vesicle therapeutics. Theranostics. 2026

This review examines the current state and translational barriers of extracellular vesicle-based therapeutics, which have attracted interest for their biocompatibility, cargo-carrying capacity, and ability to traverse biological barriers. The authors systematically survey the core obstacles preventing most EV products from advancing beyond preclinical stages, including difficulties in isolation, storage stability, characterization, biosafety evaluation, and quality control. They then assess emerging technologies intended to address these bottlenecks, such as advanced bioreactors for scalable production, microfluidic platforms for high-purity separation, single-vesicle analysis methods to handle heterogeneity, and machine learning tools to support standardization and clinical decision-making. The review concludes by outlining future directions centered on harmonizing production practices, strengthening safety assessment frameworks, and achieving regulatory compliance. Overall, the paper aims to map a clearer pathway for translating EV-based therapies from laboratory research into clinical application.

Oxidative Stress in Health and Disease: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Perspectives

Shehwaz Anwar, Hajed Obaid A. Alharbi, Ali Yousif Babiker et al.. Oxidative Stress in Health and Disease: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Perspectives. International Journal of Molecular Sciences. 2026

This review paper examines the dual role of reactive oxygen species in biological systems, where they serve as essential signaling molecules at physiological levels but drive disease pathology when produced in excess. The authors synthesize current knowledge on the major types, cellular sources, and molecular mechanisms underlying both normal redox signaling and oxidative stress-mediated damage across a range of conditions including cancer, diabetes, cardiovascular disease, neurodegeneration, and inflammatory disorders. The review also critically evaluates contemporary and emerging methods for detecting and quantifying reactive oxygen species, encompassing biochemical assays, fluorescent probes, biosensors, and advanced imaging techniques. Drawing these threads together, the authors argue for the development of precision redox medicine, emphasizing targeted antioxidant-based diagnostic and therapeutic strategies informed by improved detection technologies and a deepened mechanistic understanding of redox biology.

Waste to value: microbial electrochemical technologies for sustainable water, material, and energy cycles

Uwe Schröder, Falk Harnisch, Elizabeth Heidrich et al.. Waste to value: microbial electrochemical technologies for sustainable water, material, and energy cycles. Frontiers in Science. 2026

Microbial electrochemical technologies (METs) were examined as a sustainable alternative to conventional wastewater treatment, which currently leaves nearly half of global wastewater untreated and relies on energy- and resource-intensive processes. The review assessed how METs, including microbial fuel cells and microbial electrolysis cells, exploit electrogenic microorganisms to oxidize organic waste while simultaneously generating electricity or producing energy carriers such as hydrogen and methane, and recovering agricultural nutrients like ammonium and phosphates. Pilot-scale demonstrations have shown substantial organic matter removal and useful energy outputs, and integration with established treatment processes such as anaerobic digestion, membrane bioreactors, and constructed wetlands has advanced the technology toward higher readiness levels. Nevertheless, commercialization remains constrained by high capital costs, material limitations, and energy efficiency barriers, with scaling from laboratory to real-world deployment representing the central challenge. The authors conclude that progress in electrode design, modular configurations, deeper understanding of microbial communities, and interdisciplinary collaboration are needed to fully realize METs' potential to convert wastewater into energy, clean water, and nutrients within a circular economy framework.

Blazar Boosted Dark Matter in IceCube

Alberto M. Gago, Jaime Hoefken Zink, Joel Jones-Pérez et al.. Blazar Boosted Dark Matter in IceCube. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the ability of the IceCube neutrino detector to probe dark matter through a novel mechanism in which dark matter particles are accelerated to relativistic energies by proton beams in blazars. The researchers modeled a fermionic dark matter scenario with a massive vector mediator coupling to quarks, computing the diffuse dark matter flux from a large sample of blazars whose proton spectra were inferred from multiwavelength observations, while incorporating dark matter spike profiles around central supermassive black holes and accounting for Earth attenuation. The dark matter-nucleon scattering cross section was calculated across elastic, resonant single pion production, and deep inelastic scattering regimes to ensure smooth coverage of the transition between low- and high-energy interactions. Using existing IceCube data, the study derives constraints on the model's parameter space and finds that this approach can surpass conventional direct-detection experiments for dark matter masses below approximately one GeV. The signal is found to be dominated by deep inelastic scattering, making it more sensitive to heavier mediators, while resonance processes moderately reduce the predicted event rate near the detection threshold.

Cosmic Ray Diffusion and the Origin of Very High Energy Gamma-Ray Emission in Young Massive Stellar Clusters

Lucas Barreto-Mota, Elisabete M. de Gouveia Dal Pino, Gaetano Di Marco et al.. Cosmic Ray Diffusion and the Origin of Very High Energy Gamma-Ray Emission in Young Massive Stellar Clusters. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how cosmic rays are accelerated and transported within young massive stellar clusters, with the goal of understanding extended very high energy gamma-ray emission detected around such sources. The authors developed a theoretical framework showing that mirror diffusion combined with pitch-angle scattering in turbulent magnetic fields produces an energy-dependent diffusion coefficient scaling as the cube root of energy, representing anomalously slow transport. They coupled three-dimensional magnetohydrodynamic simulations of a young massive stellar cluster with Monte Carlo cosmic-ray propagation calculations, using the cluster W43 as a benchmark case and testing both centrally injected and shell-injected cosmic-ray geometries. Their results demonstrate that this slow diffusion regime, paired with a standard cosmic-ray injection spectrum, successfully reproduces the gamma-ray spectra observed by Fermi and LHAASO, whereas conventional stronger energy-dependent diffusion requires an unusually hard injection spectrum to match observations. A key conclusion is that the observed gamma-ray emission is governed primarily by the diffusive propagation regime rather than the specific location where cosmic rays are injected, with the balance between leptonic and hadronic emission also depending sensitively on how diffusion is treated.

ATLAS. IV. A JWST+MUSE Demographic Study of Ly$α$ Profiles in Little Red Dots

Yuta Kageura, Masami Ouchi, Hiroto Yanagisawa et al.. ATLAS. IV. A JWST+MUSE Demographic Study of Ly$α$ Profiles in Little Red Dots. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper presents a demographic study of Lyman-alpha emission profiles in "little red dots," a class of compact high-redshift objects detected by JWST, using both ground-based and space-based spectroscopy spanning redshifts from approximately three to nine. The researchers assembled a sample of LRDs observed with VLT/MUSE and JWST/NIRSpec from multiple survey programs, identifying Lyman-alpha emission in a subset of objects and characterizing whether that emission was broad or narrow. They found that the vast majority of Lyman-alpha–emitting LRDs display narrow line profiles comparable to those of ordinary high-redshift star-forming galaxies, with only two objects showing the broad Lyman-alpha emission previously associated with this population. While LRDs show a somewhat elevated fraction of broad Lyman-alpha emitters compared to star-forming galaxies, stacking analyses of the larger sample reveal no significant broad component beyond those two individual detections. The authors conclude that broad Lyman-alpha emission is not a ubiquitous property of LRDs but rather characterizes a rare subset that may represent a particular evolutionary phase, possibly linked to unusually strong outflows or other distinctive physical conditions.

Finding Vulnerabilities via LLM-Augmented Semantics-Aware Type-Checking

Ruizhe Wang, Meng Xu, N. Asokan. Finding Vulnerabilities via LLM-Augmented Semantics-Aware Type-Checking. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the limitation of traditional static analysis tools for vulnerability detection, which rely on syntactic pattern matching and miss deeper semantic information encoded in variable and function names. The authors propose SETYPE, a semantics-aware type system that derives type information directly from the natural language meanings of symbols and expressions in source code, using large language models to perform both type inference and type checking. A failed type check in this framework signals a potential vulnerability. They implemented a prototype called PYSETYPE targeting Python web applications and evaluated it on real-world software, achieving strong detection precision and accuracy. The system successfully identified previously unknown zero-day vulnerabilities, a substantial portion of which were confirmed by developers, demonstrating the practical viability of LLM-augmented semantic reasoning for security analysis.

Marionette: Predicting World States, Rendering Geometry, Painting Appearance

Zian Meng, Zhen Li, Chuanhao Li et al.. Marionette: Predicting World States, Rendering Geometry, Painting Appearance. arXiv. 2026 preprint

Marionette is a world model for interactive games featuring articulated characters that addresses the fragility of long-horizon generation in existing approaches, which implicitly encode geometric structure within learned latent or pixel sequences. Rather than autoregressing visual observations directly, the system decomposes generation into three explicit stages: a dynamics model predicts a high-dimensional interpretable 3D world state encoding multi-entity skeletal poses, trajectories, and rotations; a parameter-free graphics renderer converts this state into structured pose-control videos with geometrically correct occlusion; and a video diffusion model then synthesizes photorealistic RGB frames conditioned on these controls. This explicit factorization makes the world state directly interpretable and controllable, as demonstrated by the finding that injecting mismatched action streams measurably shifts joint error. Long-horizon failures such as character drift and ground penetration, which emerge freely in the predicted state, can be corrected by imposing simple geometric rules on that state alone without retraining the appearance model. The approach achieves visual fidelity comparable to generation conditioned on recorded pose, suggesting that routing appearance synthesis through an explicit geometric representation incurs no detectable perceptual cost.

The deepest color-magnitude diagrams for the benchmark open cluster NGC 2437 from Gaia and VVVX

Tali Palma, Matías Gómez, Dante Minniti et al.. The deepest color-magnitude diagrams for the benchmark open cluster NGC 2437 from Gaia and VVVX. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study characterized the physical properties of the large open cluster NGC 2437 by combining optical data from Gaia DR3 with near-infrared photometry from the VVV Extended Survey (VVVX). The researchers used Gaia proper motions to identify cluster members and constructed both optical and near-infrared color-magnitude and color-color diagrams, including the deepest near-infrared color-magnitude diagram yet produced for this cluster using newly built VVVX deep stack images. By fitting isochrones and analyzing the resulting diagrams, they determined key parameters including the cluster's distance, reddening, age, core radius, total luminosity, and binary fraction. The deep VVVX stacks substantially increased photometric depth in the near-infrared, nearly doubling the number of detected point sources compared to shallower surveys. The revised cluster parameters were also used to reassess the properties of several nearby objects, including a neighboring open cluster, a planetary nebula, and an evolved stellar source projected near the cluster field.

multiplex PCR respiratory pathogen panel

Topic mode · generated August 17, 2026 at 13:11 UTC · 18 items

Twenty-Two Months of Syndromic Multiplex-PCR Testing for Acute Infections in a Southern Italian Hospital: Pathogen Epidemiology, Diagnostic Appropriateness and the Cost of Negative Results.

Chirizzi D, Spedicato A, Bianco G et al.. Twenty-Two Months of Syndromic Multiplex-PCR Testing for Acute Infections in a Southern Italian Hospital: Pathogen Epidemiology, Diagnostic Appropriateness and the Cost of Negative Results.. Microorganisms. 2026

This study examined the real-world use of syndromic multiplex-PCR panels for diagnosing acute infections across four clinical syndromes at a southern Italian hospital over nearly two years. Researchers retrospectively analyzed thousands of panels covering central nervous system, upper and lower respiratory, and gastrointestinal infections, removing duplicate determinations to ensure each record represented a unique diagnostic episode. They found that nearly three in five determinations returned entirely negative results, though appropriateness varied markedly by panel type, with meningitis/encephalitis and gastrointestinal panels yielding far higher negativity rates than respiratory panels. Key pathogen findings included viral predominance in central nervous system infections, rhinovirus/enterovirus dominance in respiratory specimens, clustering of antimicrobial resistance markers in Enterobacterales co-infections, and diarrhoeagenic Escherichia coli pathotypes leading gastrointestinal detections. Based on these patterns, the authors advocate for stricter pre-test gating criteria, tiered diagnostic algorithms, and mandatory reflex sequencing of unsubtypeable influenza A specimens to reduce wasteful testing and close surveillance gaps for novel zoonotic influenza strains.

Season-matched comparison of pediatric respiratory pathogen detection patterns before and after the lifting of COVID-19 restrictions in Guangzhou.

Hua L, Liang W, Li W et al.. Season-matched comparison of pediatric respiratory pathogen detection patterns before and after the lifting of COVID-19 restrictions in Guangzhou.. Frontiers in pediatrics. 2026

This study examined how the lifting of COVID-19 non-pharmaceutical interventions affected the detection patterns of respiratory pathogens in children in Guangzhou, a subtropical region of southern China. Researchers retrospectively analyzed nasopharyngeal swab samples from children under sixteen years old who presented with fever or respiratory symptoms, using a consistent multiplex PCR panel to compare a pre-lifting period with a season-matched post-lifting period. Following the removal of restrictions, detection rates of Influenza A, respiratory syncytial virus, and human rhinovirus increased markedly in the season-matched comparison, while Influenza B and human metapneumovirus declined substantially. The post-lifting cohort also included a higher proportion of infants and greater frequencies of fever, wheezing, and lower respiratory tract diagnoses, though the authors caution that changes in health-care-seeking behavior and admission thresholds may confound these clinical comparisons. The findings support ongoing multiplex surveillance and age-targeted prevention strategies, while emphasizing that causal attribution to restriction lifting alone is limited by residual confounding from seasonality, concurrent SARS-CoV-2 circulation, and shifting healthcare utilization patterns.

Scenario-Driven Rapid Testing for Top Pathogens in Pediatric Respiratory Infections: Clinical and Economic Value from Emergency Triage to Precision Anti-Infective Management in the PICU.

Chen J, Wang H, Li Y et al.. Scenario-Driven Rapid Testing for Top Pathogens in Pediatric Respiratory Infections: Clinical and Economic Value from Emergency Triage to Precision Anti-Infective Management in the PICU.. Pathogens (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

This narrative review examines how rapid diagnostic testing for pediatric respiratory infections can be optimized to deliver genuine clinical and economic value across different care settings. The authors address the diagnostic challenge posed by overlapping presentations of viral, bacterial, and atypical pathogens in children, and the consequent tendency toward empirical antimicrobial use despite a predominantly viral etiology. Rather than evaluating diagnostic technologies in isolation, they propose a scenario-driven framework in which "top pathogens" are defined contextually by age group, syndrome, season, care setting, and factors such as transmissibility, treatment implications, and antimicrobial resistance relevance. The review synthesizes evidence across clinical scenarios ranging from emergency triage through inpatient ward care, severe pneumonia, and intensive care to outbreak and hospital-acquired infection settings, and analyzes the economic mechanisms by which targeted testing may reduce unnecessary antibiotics, enable timely antiviral therapy, shorten hospital stays, and limit nosocomial transmission. The authors conclude that the value of rapid diagnostics depends not on analytical breadth but on applying the right test to the right patient within predefined decision pathways, and they propose implementation principles integrating diagnostic stewardship, antimicrobial stewardship, and local epidemiological context.

Etiology of severe acute respiratory infections among adults in northern Thailand using multiplex PCR: A post-COVID-19 surveillance study (2023-2024).

Kham-Kjing N, Kawila R, Tariyo P et al.. Etiology of severe acute respiratory infections among adults in northern Thailand using multiplex PCR: A post-COVID-19 surveillance study (2023-2024).. PloS one. 2026

This study investigated the causes of severe acute respiratory infections among hospitalized adults in northern Thailand during the post-COVID-19 pandemic period. Researchers retrospectively analyzed respiratory samples from adult patients admitted to a regional hospital in Chiang Mai with SARI symptoms between late 2023 and early 2024, using multiplex real-time PCR to screen for a broad panel of respiratory pathogens. Nearly half of patients tested positive for at least one respiratory pathogen, with adenovirus and rhinovirus emerging as the most frequently detected viruses, and a small proportion of cases involving co-infections. Seasonal patterns were observed, with influenza A and rhinovirus peaking in January while adenovirus and enterovirus circulated throughout the study period. The findings highlight that viral pathogens account for a substantial proportion of post-pandemic SARI cases and underscore the need for expanded rapid molecular diagnostics to reduce reliance on empiric antibiotic treatment.

Standardization of a quantitative multiplex real-time PCR system for the detection of bovine respiratory disease pathogens in clinical samples of dairy cattle from southern Brazil.

Rodriguez MC, Silva IV, Perotta JH et al.. Standardization of a quantitative multiplex real-time PCR system for the detection of bovine respiratory disease pathogens in clinical samples of dairy cattle from southern Brazil.. Journal of microbiological methods. 2026

This study developed and validated a multiplex quantitative real-time PCR platform capable of simultaneously detecting ten viral and bacterial pathogens associated with bovine respiratory disease in dairy cattle from southern Brazil, filling a gap where only single-plex molecular assays had previously been used. The researchers designed the system to target a broad panel of pathogens including coronaviruses, herpesviruses, and several bacterial agents, then evaluated its analytical performance through standard curve analysis, repeatability, and reproducibility testing before applying it to field-collected nasal swab samples. The assay demonstrated high sensitivity and robust performance across single, duplex, triplex, and fourplex configurations, with no significant differences detected between formats. Field testing revealed that a substantial majority of sampled cattle were positive for at least one pathogen, with bacterial agents predominating and co-infections of varying complexity identified across animals of different ages and clinical statuses. Pathogen occurrence was significantly associated with animal age, season, and farm-level factors, and the platform is presented as the first validated multiplex qPCR tool for bovine respiratory disease diagnostics in Brazil.

VisPan: real-time visualisation of multiplex amplicon-based sequencing panels for rapid syndromic surveillance and pathogen detection.

Lechat P, Kwasiborski A, Vincent R et al.. VisPan: real-time visualisation of multiplex amplicon-based sequencing panels for rapid syndromic surveillance and pathogen detection.. Bioinformatics (Oxford, England). 2026

This paper addresses the bioinformatics bottleneck in real-time pathogen detection by developing a visualization tool for multiplex amplicon-based sequencing panels. The authors adapted the existing RAMPART software to support multiplex PCR syndromic panels combined with Oxford Nanopore Technologies long-read sequencing, enabling dynamic visual tracking of sequencing runs and amplicon coverage. The tool processes raw sequencing data through to taxonomic assignment within minutes, providing an accessible and user-friendly interface suitable for field or clinical diagnostic workflows. Validation experiments using complex clinical and environmental samples confirmed that the system reliably and accurately identified all pathogens present. The tool is presented as an integrated solution for rapid syndromic surveillance and genomic pathogen monitoring in the context of emerging and re-emerging infectious diseases.

Enterococcus Species: Multifaceted Probiotic Potential and Safety Considerations

Ojonugwa Precious John, Kayode Olayinka Afolabi, Anayochukwu Chibuike Ngene et al.. Enterococcus Species: Multifaceted Probiotic Potential and Safety Considerations. Microorganisms. 2026

The paper reviews the genus *Enterococcus*, which comprises commensal bacteria found in the gastrointestinal tracts of humans and animals as well as in food and environmental sources, and which are increasingly used as probiotics, antibiotic alternatives, and protective cultures. The authors examined the beneficial roles these bacteria play, including pathogen control, microbiome modulation, food fermentation enhancement, and production of bioactive compounds such as bacteriocins with pathogen-inhibitory properties. At the same time, the review addresses serious safety concerns, particularly the association of certain enterococcal strains with healthcare-associated infections and the potential spread of antimicrobial resistance genes. The authors argue that these dual characteristics necessitate rigorous safety evaluations before enterococci can be broadly deployed in clinical or agrifood contexts. The work is presented as a comprehensive reference intended to inform future research, regulatory decision-making, and practical applications across health and food systems.

Genomic analysis of high pathogenicity avian influenza viruses from Antarctica reveals multiple introductions from South America

Maria Ogrzewalska, Ralph Eric Thijl Vanstreels, Elisa Cavalcante Pereira et al.. Genomic analysis of high pathogenicity avian influenza viruses from Antarctica reveals multiple introductions from South America. Nature Communications. 2026

This study investigated the spread of highly pathogenic avian influenza virus H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b into Antarctica, where it poses a significant threat to polar wildlife. Researchers detected the virus in carcasses of eight species across the South Shetland Islands during two consecutive austral summers, then applied whole-genome sequencing, mutational profiling, and phylogenetic reconstruction to characterize the viruses and trace their origins. The analyses revealed that the detected viruses did not derive from a single introduction event but instead descended from multiple independent incursions. One group of strains, recovered from gulls, skuas, fur seals, a tern, and an elephant seal, clustered with H5N1 strains previously identified in South American marine mammals, consistent with at least two separate introductions into Antarctica. A second group, from petrels, shags, and skuas, clustered with strains from South Georgia and a previously reported Antarctic Peninsula detection, suggesting South Georgia functions as an epidemiological stepping stone linking South America to Antarctica.

Evolution of a truncated nucleocapsid protein enhances SARS-CoV-2 fitness by suppressing antiviral responses

Expert Consensus on the Prevention of Secondary Mucormycosis Following Respiratory Viral Infections

Junyan Qu, Xinyao Liu, Min Quan et al.. Expert Consensus on the Prevention of Secondary Mucormycosis Following Respiratory Viral Infections. iFungi. 2026

This paper addresses the clinical challenge of secondary mucormycosis arising after respiratory viral infections, particularly those caused by SARS-CoV-2 and influenza viruses. A multidisciplinary group of international experts was convened to develop a formal consensus document aimed at guiding prevention strategies for this complication. The consensus identifies high-risk populations and provides a set of preventive recommendations organized around four domains: education and training, personal protection, early diagnosis and treatment, and infection control. The document also acknowledges areas where evidence remains limited or contested and calls for future research to strengthen the evidence base. Overall, the consensus aims to systematize prevention efforts and raise awareness of mucormycosis as a serious secondary complication of respiratory viral disease.

Point-of-care molecular diagnostics and drug-resistance mechanisms in neglected infectious diseases: current advances and future therapeutic opportunities

Qi Zhang, Xiajun Zhang, Jie Yang et al.. Point-of-care molecular diagnostics and drug-resistance mechanisms in neglected infectious diseases: current advances and future therapeutic opportunities. Frontiers in Cellular and Infection Microbiology. 2026

This paper reviews the current state and future prospects of point-of-care molecular diagnostics for neglected infectious diseases, with particular attention to antimicrobial resistance. The authors examined a range of emerging technologies, including isothermal nucleic acid amplification, rapid polymerase chain reaction, CRISPR-based detection, nanomaterial biosensors, and microfluidic platforms, all designed to deliver rapid, laboratory-quality results without centralized infrastructure. The review also considered how artificial intelligence and cloud-based digital health systems can support automated result interpretation, resistance surveillance, and antimicrobial stewardship. These next-generation platforms are shown to offer mechanistic insights into drug resistance in diseases such as tuberculosis, malaria, and bacterial infections, though the authors identify clinical validation, cost, scalability, and equitable access as persistent barriers. The paper concludes that the convergence of molecular diagnostics, nanotechnology, and AI analytics presents transformative opportunities for precision therapy, resistance monitoring, and infectious disease preparedness in low-resource settings.

Joint Estimation of Sparse Multilayer Networks via Graph Limits

Youngseok Song, Sofia C. Olhede. Joint Estimation of Sparse Multilayer Networks via Graph Limits. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of estimating the structure of multiplex networks, where multiple types of interactions occur among the same set of individuals. The authors model such data using graph limits known as scaled graphons, which flexibly accommodate varying sparsity across network layers, and develop a nonparametric joint estimator called the multi-network histogram based on blockmodel approximations. Shared latent variables link the layers, allowing information from denser or better-observed layers to improve estimation in sparser ones. The theoretical analysis establishes an upper bound on the weighted mean integrated squared error and derives the optimal bandwidth for minimizing this error, showing that joint modeling yields a smaller optimal bandwidth and lower error than single-layer approaches. The framework is validated through simulations and applied to socioeconomic multiplex networks from an Indian village, demonstrating practical gains from joint estimation.

Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation

Zhelun Wu. Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper argues that systems using language models to synthesize conclusions from multiple sources incorrectly conflate two distinct operations: interpreting individual sources and aggregating those interpretations into a decision. The authors propose separating these operations and define a four-field evidence tuple as the interface between them, which they argue simultaneously constrains the design of both the interpretation and aggregation components. They identify a failure mode called count-scale drift, where thresholding aggregated evidence produces an operating point that shifts with the number of sources consulted, and show that pooling calibrated log-likelihood ratios resolves this problem arithmetically rather than architecturally. The framework is validated on a longitudinal corpus in two settings—after and before outcome resolution—where the proposed separation improves performance, with a small sequence encoder combined with a tree ensemble carrying a survival loss substantially outperforming a hand-crafted baseline on a clinical prediction task. The authors also explicitly delineate which components transfer across domains, which must be re-estimated, enumerate falsifiable predictions, and report negative results.

CytoBERT: A Foundation Model for Cytometry Data

Syed Abdul Haseeb Qadri, Bjarne C. Hiller, Felix Blanke et al.. CytoBERT: A Foundation Model for Cytometry Data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

CytoBERT is a foundation model designed to address the challenges of analyzing highly heterogeneous and unstandardized single-cell cytometry data, which measures characteristics such as protein expression in immune cells. The model was pretrained in a self-supervised fashion on a large curated corpus of human cytometry datasets spanning heterogeneous marker panels and tens of millions of cells, using marker standardization to harmonize the data. By learning transferable inter-marker relationships at the single-cell level, CytoBERT aims to overcome the difficulty of applying machine learning across studies that differ in experimental protocols and measured features. Fine-tuning the pretrained model for sample-level classification demonstrated that transfer learning is feasible across diverse cytometry datasets. The authors release CytoBERT as a publicly available, open-source, open-weight model intended to serve as a generalizable starting point for scalable cytometry analysis.

Change Point Detection and Localization in High-Dimensional Time Series

Patrick Bastian, Daria Tieplova, Nina Dörnemann et al.. Change Point Detection and Localization in High-Dimensional Time Series. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper develops new statistical methodology for detecting and localizing change points in high-dimensional time series, addressing both sequential testing on incoming data streams and retrospective identification of multiple change points with confidence intervals controlling error at a global level. The test statistics are constructed using the maximum norm to maintain power against sparse and asynchronous changes, and are based on multiscale statistics that examine the data at many different resolutions simultaneously. A key theoretical contribution is the development of Hölder-Gauss-approximations, which extend classical Hölderian invariance principles to high dimensions by providing Gaussian approximations for the Hölder-norm of high-dimensional partial sum processes, a result with broader applicability beyond change point detection. The methods are validated through simulation studies examining finite-sample performance, and applied to real data on air contamination from California wildfires, where changes occur asynchronously across a network of monitoring stations.

A Reflective Metasurface for High-Efficiency Terahertz Cross-Polarization Conversion

Muhammad Fayyaz Kashif, Shobit Agarwal, Antonio Iodice et al.. A Reflective Metasurface for High-Efficiency Terahertz Cross-Polarization Conversion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated a reflective metasurface designed to convert the polarization state of terahertz radiation with high efficiency. The researchers constructed a device based on a double-ring aluminum resonator on a quartz substrate and used full-wave electromagnetic simulations to optimize the geometry and understand the underlying conversion mechanism through phase analysis, polarization ellipses, and surface-current distributions. The optimized design achieved near-unity polarization conversion ratios at two distinct terahertz resonance frequencies in simulation. Experimental validation using reflection-mode terahertz time-domain spectroscopy confirmed the simulated predictions, with measured polarization conversion ratios exceeding a high threshold at both resonance frequencies, demonstrating effective cross-polarization conversion in reflection mode.

MRI-guided cardiac radiotherapy using a 1.5 T MR-linac: surveying emerging patterns of care

O. Akdag, S. Mandija, J. Pomp et al.. MRI-guided cardiac radiotherapy using a 1.5 T MR-linac: surveying emerging patterns of care. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated the emerging use of MRI-guided radiotherapy delivered on a high-field MR-linac system as a noninvasive treatment approach for cardiac tumours, which are rare and otherwise managed through complex surgical resections. The authors conducted an international survey distributed to clinical users of the MR-linac platform who had treated patients with cardiac tumours, gathering detailed information on imaging protocols, treatment planning, motion management strategies, and clinical outcomes. The survey captured responses from six international institutions reporting on a small series of cardiac treatments performed over a multi-year period, revealing that prescribed doses ranged broadly and that the majority of cases were delivered in five fractions using hypofractionated regimens. Respiratory motion monitoring through cine MRI was employed in nearly all cases, and daily adaptive planning was a common feature of the workflow. The authors found that local control was achieved in nearly all reported cases without serious adverse events, supporting the feasibility of this approach and providing a foundation for developing standardized treatment recommendations and protocol harmonization across institutions.

lateral flow immunoassay diagnostics

A Dual-Hue Recognizable Lateral Flow Immunoassay Corresponding to Distinct Sensitivity Ranges for Timely and Quantitative Diagnosis of Prostate Cancer

Rong Hu, Xi Zhang, Liang Huang et al.. A Dual-Hue Recognizable Lateral Flow Immunoassay Corresponding to Distinct Sensitivity Ranges for Timely and Quantitative Diagnosis of Prostate Cancer. ACS Nano. 2026

This paper developed a lateral flow immunoassay platform designed to simultaneously detect two prostate cancer biomarkers—total and free prostate-specific antigen—with both visual and smartphone-based readout capabilities. The system exploits dual-ratiometric fluorescence transitions driven by the inner filter effect, creating two distinct color-change pathways with different sensitivity ranges that are theoretically and experimentally characterized. This design allows the clinically ambiguous diagnostic "gray zone," where standard tests struggle to differentiate benign from malignant conditions, to be assessed semiquantitatively by the naked eye, while a smartphone app analyzing color tone provides precise risk stratification based on the ratio of the two biomarkers. Validated against clinical serum samples, the platform achieved diagnostic accuracy comparable to established chemiluminescent assays while being substantially faster and simpler to operate. The authors propose that this dual-hue ratiometric framework represents a generalizable approach for multiplexed point-of-care diagnostics of noncommunicable diseases.

Recent advances in lateral flow immunoassay for virus detection: current achievements, challenges and future prospects.

Li L, Liu F, He X et al.. Recent advances in lateral flow immunoassay for virus detection: current achievements, challenges and future prospects.. Talanta. 2026

This review examines lateral flow immunoassay technology as a diagnostic platform for viral infectious diseases, reframing it not merely as a point-of-care tool but as a point-of-need self-testing platform capable of supporting large-scale epidemic surveillance and pandemic preparedness. The authors systematically survey recent advances across three dimensions: signal generation and transduction strategies employing diverse nanomaterials such as colorimetric, fluorescent, magnetic, surface-enhanced Raman scattering, and photothermal systems; multimodal and multiplex detection approaches designed to improve sensitivity, accuracy, and throughput; and the integration of machine learning for signal interpretation and system optimization. The review also comprehensively covers applications in detecting viral antigens, antibodies, and nucleic acids. Current limitations including sensitivity constraints, target specificity issues, and practical challenges related to cost, usability, and stability are critically assessed, with future directions emphasizing advanced nanomaterials, intelligent data processing, and multi-target detection strategies. Overall, the authors argue that lateral flow immunoassay, particularly colorimetric formats, holds transformative potential as an emergency screening tool and for routine decentralized testing in strengthening global public health infrastructure.

Polydopamine nanoparticle-based colorimetric-photothermal dual-mode lateral flow immunoassay for thromboembolism diagnosis.

Yin J, Qiu Y, Nie Y et al.. Polydopamine nanoparticle-based colorimetric-photothermal dual-mode lateral flow immunoassay for thromboembolism diagnosis.. RSC advances. 2026

This paper investigated whether polydopamine nanoparticles could serve as superior signal labels in lateral flow immunoassay platforms to overcome the sensitivity limitations of conventional gold nanoparticle-based single-mode colorimetric detection. The researchers constructed a dual-mode colorimetric and photothermal lateral flow immunoassay using polydopamine nanoparticles, exploiting their high molar extinction coefficient and broadband ultraviolet-to-near-infrared absorption, and applied it to detect two thromboembolism-related biomarkers, thrombomodulin and α₂-plasmin inhibitor-plasmin complex, in clinical serum samples. The photothermal readout mode substantially amplified detection sensitivity beyond the colorimetric mode alone, and clinical validation against established clinical methods using serum samples demonstrated good agreement. The authors conclude that this polydopamine nanoparticle-based dual-mode platform offers a practical strategy for improving the sensitivity and reliability of lateral flow immunoassays for point-of-care diagnostics.

Boronate-mediated antibody orientation: a molecular simulation-guided strategy for ultra-sensitive lateral flow immunoassays.

Yang Z, Yan X, Yang Z et al.. Boronate-mediated antibody orientation: a molecular simulation-guided strategy for ultra-sensitive lateral flow immunoassays.. Mikrochimica acta. 2026

This paper investigated how antibody orientation during immobilization affects the sensitivity and consistency of lateral flow immunoassays, with random immobilization identified as a key limiting factor. The researchers developed a boronate affinity-based strategy that exploits covalent reactions between boronic acid and cis-diol groups on Fc-region glycans to achieve site-specific antibody orientation, and used molecular dynamics simulations to characterize the mechanistic basis of this approach. Simulations revealed that the boronate-antibody configuration exhibited highly favorable binding energy and rapid conformational stabilization, proceeding through sequential electrostatic guidance followed by hydrogen-bond and covalent bond formation. Experimentally, the oriented immobilization yielded near-complete antibody conjugation efficiency, high Fab exposure, and superior antigen-binding affinity compared to conventional passive adsorption. When applied to detecting the mycotoxin zearalenone in corn, the oriented lateral flow assay achieved a detection limit dramatically lower than traditional colloidal gold assays, with recovery rates and correlation to confirmatory mass spectrometry analysis demonstrating the method's accuracy and practical utility.

On-site serological screening of Usutu virus in wild birds: Field evaluation of a lateral flow microarray immunoassay using the WHO REASSURED framework.

Godarzi B, Holicki CM, Chandler FD et al.. On-site serological screening of Usutu virus in wild birds: Field evaluation of a lateral flow microarray immunoassay using the WHO REASSURED framework.. Journal of virological methods. 2026

This study evaluated a lateral flow microarray immunoassay for detecting antibodies against Usutu virus and West Nile virus in wild birds directly in the field, addressing the lack of on-site, multi-analyte serological tools for arbovirus surveillance. Researchers captured wild birds at ringing sites in the Netherlands and tested them using the species-independent assay, then compared results against the gold-standard focus reduction neutralization test for a subset of birds. The assay demonstrated high sensitivity and specificity for Usutu virus detection and successfully identified seroconversion in a Eurasian blackbird, which was independently confirmed. Performance was also evaluated against the WHO REASSURED framework for point-of-care diagnostics, where the assay achieved a strong proportion of the maximum possible score. The findings support the feasibility of this rapid, equipment-light assay for on-site arbovirus surveillance in both Dutch ringing sites and resource-limited settings, though further validation is recommended.

Large-scale synthesis of phenylboronic acid-functionalized gold nanoparticles enables an oriented antibody interface for sensitive point-of-care diagnostics.

Xu J, Li Q, Lu B et al.. Large-scale synthesis of phenylboronic acid-functionalized gold nanoparticles enables an oriented antibody interface for sensitive point-of-care diagnostics.. Colloids and surfaces. B, Biointerfaces. 2026

This paper investigated how antibody orientation on gold nanoparticles affects the performance of lateral flow immunoassays, and whether a new nanoparticle synthesis strategy could overcome the limitations of conventional approaches. The researchers developed a one-pot, room-temperature method to produce phenylboronic acid-functionalized gold nanoparticles at large scale, enabling oriented antibody immobilization through boronate-affinity interactions with the carbohydrate moieties on antibody Fc fragments, rather than relying on random physical adsorption. This oriented coupling approach was designed to preserve accessibility of antigen-binding sites, directly improving diagnostic sensitivity. When tested for fecal occult blood detection, the new nanoparticles dramatically outperformed conventional citrate-capped gold nanoparticles in sensitivity, while the synthesis process was also substantially faster and more scalable than traditional high-temperature reflux methods. The authors conclude that this platform offers strong potential for clinical translation in high-sensitivity point-of-care diagnostics.

Dual-enhancement lateral flow immunoassay &lt;i&gt;via&lt;/i&gt; gold-decorated ferritin nanoprobes and SpyTag/SpyCatcher site-directed conjugation.

Yuan X, Zhu X, Xu Y et al.. Dual-enhancement lateral flow immunoassay &lt;i&gt;via&lt;/i&gt; gold-decorated ferritin nanoprobes and SpyTag/SpyCatcher site-directed conjugation.. Analytical methods : advancing methods and applications. 2026

This paper addressed the limited sensitivity of conventional lateral flow immunoassays, which stems from weak optical signals, randomly oriented antibodies, and inconsistent nanoprobe fabrication. The researchers developed a dual-enhancement approach combining gold-decorated ferritin nanocages with SpyTag/SpyCatcher chemistry, where engineered ferritin displaying SpyTag peptides served as a precise template for controlled gold nanocluster growth while retaining functional sites for subsequent antibody attachment. The resulting core-shell nanostructures were highly uniform, and the SpyCatcher-fused antibodies conjugated to them via spontaneous covalent isopeptide bonds in a site-specific, oriented manner that greatly improved antigen-binding capacity compared to random adsorption. Testing against human chorionic gonadotropin as a model analyte, the optimized assay achieved a limit of detection dramatically lower than commercial gold nanoparticle-based strips, while also demonstrating reliable performance in urine samples with acceptable spike-recovery rates. The authors conclude that this synergistic strategy offers a versatile platform for substantially improving the sensitivity of point-of-care diagnostic tests.

Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms

Purshottam J. Assudani, Balakrishnan . P., Anny Leema A et al.. Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms. Microchimica Acta. 2026

This review examines the current state of biosensing technologies designed to detect high-priority foodborne pathogens, including Escherichia coli, Salmonella, and Listeria monocytogenes, with an emphasis on clinical relevance and field deployment. The authors survey electrochemical, optical, and microfluidic sensing platforms, exploring how advances in functional nanomaterials, aptamers, nanozymes, and surface engineering have improved sensor sensitivity and specificity in complex food matrices. A central theme is the integration of artificial intelligence and machine learning into these platforms, which the review argues transforms conventional detection systems into smart diagnostic tools capable of real-time analytics, pattern recognition, and high-throughput multiplexed detection. The review critically assesses persistent challenges such as matrix interference, bioreceptor instability, manufacturing scalability, and regulatory standardization, and proposes mitigation strategies including ratiometric sensing, microfluidic pre-treatment, and explainable AI. Looking forward, the authors advocate for self-powered biosensors, modular pathogen panel hubs, blockchain-based traceability, and standardized validation pipelines as pathways toward regulation-ready platforms for next-generation food safety monitoring.

Oxidative Stress in Health and Disease: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Perspectives

Shehwaz Anwar, Hajed Obaid A. Alharbi, Ali Yousif Babiker et al.. Oxidative Stress in Health and Disease: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Perspectives. International Journal of Molecular Sciences. 2026

This review paper examines the dual biological roles of reactive oxygen species, which act as essential signaling molecules at physiological concentrations but drive disease when produced in excess or dysregulated. The authors synthesize current knowledge on ROS types, their cellular and molecular origins, and the key pathways through which redox imbalance causes cellular damage and signaling disruption. The review also critically evaluates contemporary detection and quantification methods for oxidative stress, including biochemical assays, fluorescent probes, biosensors, and advanced imaging approaches applicable to both clinical and preclinical settings. The contribution of oxidative stress to major diseases such as cancer, diabetes, cardiovascular disease, neurodegeneration, and inflammatory disorders is examined within this framework. The paper concludes by outlining future directions in precision redox medicine, emphasizing targeted antioxidant-based diagnostic and therapeutic strategies informed by improved ROS detection technologies and a deeper mechanistic understanding of redox biology.

Spherical Nucleic Acids: Turning Synthetic Advances and Fundamental Discovery into Translational Breakthroughs in Chemistry, Materials Development, Biology, and Medicine

Connor M. Forsyth, Rachel R. Chan, Tanner D. Fink et al.. Spherical Nucleic Acids: Turning Synthetic Advances and Fundamental Discovery into Translational Breakthroughs in Chemistry, Materials Development, Biology, and Medicine. Accounts of Chemical Research. 2026

Spherical nucleic acids (SNAs) are nanostructures consisting of a nanoparticle core densely coated with a radially oriented shell of oligonucleotides, and this review traces their development over three decades as a case study in how fundamental nanoscience drives translational innovation. The paper examines how advances in synthetic control over nanostructure size, shape, and composition, combined with the discovery of emergent phenomena, have produced a well-defined set of design rules for SNA construction across dozens of structural classes. A key focus is the distinctive properties that arise from the SNA architecture, including enhanced nucleic acid binding affinity, resistance to enzymatic degradation, reduced immune activation, and efficient cellular uptake without transfection agents, which together underpin their use as intracellular probes, gene regulation tools, vaccines, and CRISPR-based gene editing platforms. In parallel, SNAs function as programmable building blocks for assembling DNA-mediated colloidal crystals with tunable lattice symmetries and nanoparticle compositions, enabling metamaterials with properties such as negative refractive indices and shape memory. The authors argue that SNAs illustrate a broader paradigm in which synthetic mastery and fundamental discovery together catalyze interdisciplinary breakthroughs spanning chemistry, biology, materials science, and medicine.

Rethinking Extracellular Vesicle Signaling

Wojciech Chrzanowski, Joy Wolfram. Rethinking Extracellular Vesicle Signaling. Advanced Materials. 2026

This paper examines how extracellular vesicles communicate between cells, challenging the dominant paradigm that their signaling occurs primarily through cellular uptake and release of internal cargo into the cytoplasm. The authors propose two alternative surface-binding mechanisms — termed "bind-and-stay" and "bind-and-leave" — in which vesicles signal through interactions at the cell surface without being internalized. The "bind-and-leave" mode is particularly notable because it allows a single vesicle to transiently bind and activate signaling in multiple recipient cells sequentially, directly contradicting the conventional assumption that each vesicle acts on only one cell. The paper offers a forward-looking perspective on the relative efficiency and effectiveness of these different signaling modes and identifies experimental approaches that could be used to investigate them. The authors argue that a more complete understanding of extracellular vesicle signaling mechanisms is essential for both fundamental cell biology and the development of vesicle-based therapeutics.

Cosmic Ray Diffusion and the Origin of Very High Energy Gamma-Ray Emission in Young Massive Stellar Clusters

Lucas Barreto-Mota, Elisabete M. de Gouveia Dal Pino, Gaetano Di Marco et al.. Cosmic Ray Diffusion and the Origin of Very High Energy Gamma-Ray Emission in Young Massive Stellar Clusters. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates young massive stellar clusters as potential sources capable of accelerating cosmic rays to extreme energies, focusing on how cosmic ray transport within these environments shapes the observed gamma-ray emission. The authors developed a theoretical framework showing that mirror diffusion combined with pitch-angle scattering in turbulent magnetic fields suppresses cosmic ray transport, producing an energy-dependent diffusion coefficient scaling as energy to the one-third power. They coupled three-dimensional magnetohydrodynamic simulations of a young massive stellar cluster with Monte Carlo cosmic ray propagation calculations, using the cluster W43 as a benchmark, and tested two different cosmic ray injection geometries alongside multiple diffusion regimes. Their results demonstrate that the slow mirror-plus-scattering diffusion regime, combined with a standard cosmic ray injection spectrum scaling as energy to the negative two, successfully reproduces the gamma-ray spectra observed by Fermi and LHAASO, whereas stronger energy-dependent diffusion requires an anomalously hard injection spectrum to match observations. The study concludes that the observed gamma-ray emission is governed primarily by the diffusive propagation regime rather than the specific location of cosmic ray injection within the cluster.

Comment on arXiv:2106.08363v3 [math.NA], E. Abreu, A. Espirito Santo, W. Lambert, and J. Perez, Convergence of a Lagrangian--Eulerian scheme by a weak asymptotic analysis for one-dimensional hyperbolic problems

Frederico Furtado, Felipe Pereira, Li-Ming Yeh. Comment on arXiv:2106.08363v3 [math.NA], E. Abreu, A. Espirito Santo, W. Lambert, and J. Perez, Convergence of a Lagrangian--Eulerian scheme by a weak asymptotic analysis for one-dimensional hyperbolic problems. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This comment targets a published numerical analysis paper that introduced space-time control volumes with lateral boundaries called "no-flow curves," claimed as a novel contribution for solving one-dimensional hyperbolic problems via a Lagrangian–Eulerian scheme. The commenting authors demonstrate, through equation-by-equation comparison, that these no-flow curves are mathematically identical to the space-time integral curves already defined in a earlier locally conservative Eulerian–Lagrangian method, and that the bounding tube structure also appeared in subsequent related work. A key technical point is emphasized: the characteristic velocity used to move control volumes matters critically, as two candidate expressions coincide only for linear fluxes, and only one of them correctly ensures vanishing lateral mass flux. The comment further documents, using a corpus of papers including works published after the commented article, a pattern of shifting terminology and incomplete attribution that obscures this priority. The discrete numerical contributions of the later works are explicitly not disputed; the concern is solely with the attribution of the continuous geometric object.

Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation

Zhelun Wu. Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper argues that systems using language models to draw conclusions from multiple sources make a conceptual error by conflating two distinct operations: interpreting individual sources and aggregating those interpretations into a decision. The authors propose separating these operations and define a structured four-field evidence tuple as the interface between them, which they argue determines the design of both halves. They identify a failure mode they call count-scale drift, where summing unnormalized weights creates a decision threshold that shifts with the number of sources consulted, and show that the vote rule and posterior ordering diverge when source reliabilities differ, with no threshold able to reconcile them. Pooling calibrated log-likelihood ratios is proposed as an arithmetic rather than architectural fix applicable broadly to score-summing systems beyond language models, including diagnostic panels and multi-signal detectors. The framework is validated on a longitudinal corpus in both prospective and retrospective settings, where the proposed separation achieves substantially higher predictive performance than a hand-crafted baseline, and the authors explicitly enumerate falsifiable predictions and acknowledged negative results.

Optimal Scheduling of Road Maintenance Jobs Considering Impact on Traffic Flows

Charitha Nandepu, Lohitha Kalepu, Gabriele Ciavarella et al.. Optimal Scheduling of Road Maintenance Jobs Considering Impact on Traffic Flows. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper examines how to efficiently schedule road maintenance at the network level while accounting for how maintenance-induced capacity reductions affect traffic flows. Because repeatedly solving equilibrium traffic assignment models within a scheduling optimization is computationally prohibitive, the authors developed data-driven surrogate models that approximate equilibrium arc flows directly from origin-destination demand data, using optimization-based solutions as ground truth for training. The approach was validated through a real-world case study using traffic data from a metropolitan area, demonstrating that the surrogate models can serve as a scalable substitute for full equilibrium computations. The work positions these surrogate models as practical building blocks for embedding traffic-impact considerations into future network-level maintenance scheduling frameworks.

The Fisher Metric of the Ricci Flow Heat Kernel

Bennett Chow, Robert Koirala. The Fisher Metric of the Ricci Flow Heat Kernel. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces and systematically studies a Fisher information metric associated to the conjugate heat kernel of a Ricci flow, a tensor that quantifies how the pointed heat-kernel measure varies as the base point moves at a fixed scale. The authors establish fundamental analytic properties of this metric, including monotonicity in scale and a sharp upper bound by the Riemannian metric, and they derive a matrix square identity for the Fisher defect that characterizes the gap between the two metrics. This identity yields a rigidity theorem: on closed connected Ricci flows the Fisher metric is strictly sandwiched between zero and the Riemannian metric at every positive scale, while in the complete case equality forces a Euclidean splitting. Building on this framework, the authors derive several consequences including a sharp reverse Poincaré inequality for the heat semigroup, a contraction formula for generalized divergences along conjugate heat flow, and a canonical construction of splitting maps from large eigenvalues of the Fisher endomorphism. As applications, they connect pointed Nash entropy near zero to small Fisher deficit at comparable scales and establish a codimension-one Fisher-metric criterion for invoking an existing epsilon-regularity theorem in Ricci flow.

Memory Allocation for Constant-Bounded Programs

Vinícius Silva, Kael Soares, Márcio Costa e Fernando Magno Quintão Pereira. Memory Allocation for Constant-Bounded Programs. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates memory allocation strategies specifically for constant-bounded programs — programs whose execution length is syntactically constrained regardless of input, such as verified kernel extensions, cryptographic routines, and fixed-shape machine-learning models. The authors demonstrate that this class of programs allows control flow to be modeled as a tree, enabling a polynomial-time approximation of optimal stack usage through a tree-scan allocation strategy combined with memory defragmentation. Their approach provides a theoretical guarantee that memory usage is bounded by the maximum live memory plus at most the size of the largest buffer, achieving optimality when in-place swapping is allowed. The allocator was validated in two practical settings: as a stack spiller in an Elixir-to-eBPF compiler and as a static heap allocator for bounded programs using the MLIR Structured Control-Flow dialect. Results show dramatic stack usage reductions on real workloads, with defragmentation being rarely necessary and memory consumption remaining far below that of naive allocation approaches.

Feasibility, Acceptability, and Test Performance of Point-of-Care Nucleic Acid Tests for HIV Testing and Viral Load Monitoring in the United States: Prospective Longitudinal Mixed-Methods Study.

Niemann LA, Violette LR, Cornelius-Hudson A et al.. Feasibility, Acceptability, and Test Performance of Point-of-Care Nucleic Acid Tests for HIV Testing and Viral Load Monitoring in the United States: Prospective Longitudinal Mixed-Methods Study.. JMIR research protocols. 2026

This paper describes the design and enrollment progress of a prospective longitudinal mixed-methods study evaluating point-of-care nucleic acid tests (POC NATs) for HIV diagnosis and viral load monitoring in the United States. The study enrolled participants across a hospital-based clinic and a community LGBTQ+ center in Seattle, using two versions of a whole-blood POC NAT alongside standard laboratory testing, and included a randomized clinical trial arm for people with HIV to compare standard care with and without POC viral load monitoring. Participants also completed acceptability surveys and qualitative interviews, and clinic patient flow was observed to assess the operational impact of POC testing. The abstract reports enrollment figures and study procedures but does not yet present test performance, clinical, or qualitative findings, as analyses are anticipated in future publications. The paper is therefore primarily a protocol and feasibility description rather than a report of completed outcomes.

Brief Report: Association Between Pharmacologic Tenofovir Adherence Measures and Subsequent 24-Week Viral Load Outcomes for People With HIV in South Africa.

Bodley N, Govender K, Moodley P et al.. Brief Report: Association Between Pharmacologic Tenofovir Adherence Measures and Subsequent 24-Week Viral Load Outcomes for People With HIV in South Africa.. Journal of acquired immune deficiency syndromes (1999). 2026

This study examined whether pharmacologic measures of tenofovir adherence could predict future HIV viremia among people living with HIV in South Africa. Researchers compared three adherence metrics — a point-of-care urine tenofovir antibody-based assay, quantitative urine tenofovir measured by liquid chromatography-mass spectrometry, and tenofovir-diphosphate concentrations in dried blood spots — and assessed their associations with current and subsequent viral load outcomes at twenty-four weeks using logistic regression. Tenofovir-diphosphate in dried blood spots was the only measure significantly associated with viremia at twenty-four weeks, with higher concentrations linked to lower odds of detectable virus, suggesting it may be useful for predicting future treatment failure. In contrast, neither the point-of-care urine assay nor quantitative urine tenofovir showed significant associations with subsequent viremia, though undetectable point-of-care urine tenofovir was associated with current viremia, lower CD4 counts, and likely recent antiretroviral therapy initiation. The findings suggest that dried blood spot tenofovir-diphosphate measurement may offer the greatest utility for identifying patients at risk of future viremia and targeting adherence interventions.

Feasibility of integrating a point of care triage test into routine antiretroviral therapy monitoring in Mozambique: a qualitative evaluation

Myburgh H, Saura-Lázaro A, van den Bogaart E et al.. Feasibility of integrating a point of care triage test into routine antiretroviral therapy monitoring in Mozambique: a qualitative evaluation. 2026

This qualitative study examined perceptions of integrating a point-of-care triage test measuring interferon-gamma-induced protein 10 (IP-10), a chemokine correlated with HIV viral load, into routine antiretroviral therapy monitoring in Mozambique. Conducted within a clinical evaluation at two primary healthcare facilities, the researchers interviewed people living with HIV who underwent IP-10 testing across multiple rounds, as well as healthcare providers and HIV programme stakeholders, analyzing data through an inductive-deductive thematic approach. Participants broadly valued routine viral load monitoring but identified significant barriers including laboratory delays, access difficulties, and health system constraints. The IP-10 point-of-care test was widely acceptable, with same-day results perceived to reduce patient anxiety, support treatment adherence, and enable timely clinical decisions, while providers recognized its potential to prioritize patients needing confirmatory viral load testing. The authors conclude that successful integration would require attention to training, workflow adaptation, quality assurance, and clear patient communication, but that the test could contribute to more efficient, differentiated HIV care by reducing unnecessary testing among virally suppressed individuals.

Can exercise affect immune function to increase susceptibility to infection?

Richard J. Simpson, Richard J. Simpson, Maree Gleeson et al.. Can exercise affect immune function to increase susceptibility to infection?. PubMed. 2026

This debate article examines whether exercise itself can suppress immune function and increase susceptibility to infection, a central and contested question in exercise immunology. The authors present opposing arguments addressing whether high-performance athletes face greater infection risk than the general population, whether exercise independently alters immunity apart from confounding factors such as sleep disruption, anxiety, travel, and nutritional deficits, and whether available biomarkers and study designs are adequate to draw firm conclusions. While there is general agreement that regular moderate-intensity exercise of shorter duration is beneficial for immune defense, the picture is more complex for arduous exercise practiced by elite athletes and military personnel, where high infection burdens have historically been attributed to exercise-induced immune suppression. Both sides of the debate agree that infection susceptibility is multifactorial in nature, making it difficult to isolate exercise as an independent causative factor. The article concludes that the question of whether exercise per se drives increased infection risk in athletes remains unresolved and calls for more rigorous empirical research.

Exosomal bioactive molecules: molecular regulation, functional heterogeneity, and translational implications in disease diagnostics and therapeutics

Victor U. Chigozie. Exosomal bioactive molecules: molecular regulation, functional heterogeneity, and translational implications in disease diagnostics and therapeutics. Clinical and Experimental Medicine. 2026

This review examines exosomes — nanoscale extracellular vesicles that carry proteins, lipids, and nucleic acids between cells — with a focus on the molecular mechanisms governing their biogenesis, cargo loading, and functional heterogeneity. The authors synthesize current understanding of the regulatory machinery involved, including ESCRT complexes, lipid domains, RNA-binding proteins, and post-translational modifications, and survey state-of-the-art omics platforms and machine learning tools used to characterize exosomal content. The review evaluates the diagnostic utility of exosomes as liquid biopsy biomarkers across cancer, metabolic, and neurodegenerative diseases, as well as their therapeutic applications in targeted drug delivery, wound healing, and neuroregeneration, including ongoing clinical trials. The authors identify persistent translational barriers such as isolation protocol variability, cargo heterogeneity, targeting specificity, and regulatory constraints. They conclude by proposing a forward-looking framework that integrates biological, engineering, and clinical perspectives — encompassing synthetic exosome mimetics and AI-driven diagnostics — to advance exosome-based precision medicine.
