Oral Nirmatrelvir–Ritonavir for Covid-19 in Higher-Risk Outpatients

Christopher C. Butler, Andrew Pinto, Victoria Harris et al.. Oral Nirmatrelvir–Ritonavir for Covid-19 in Higher-Risk Outpatients. New England Journal of Medicine. 2026

These two open-label randomized trials examined whether nirmatrelvir–ritonavir reduces hospitalizations and deaths in higher-risk outpatients with COVID-19 who were predominantly vaccinated or had prior immunity, a population distinct from the unvaccinated high-risk patients in whom the drug originally demonstrated benefit. Participants aged fifty or older, or younger adults with coexisting conditions, who tested positive for SARS-CoV-2 within five days of symptom onset were randomly assigned to receive either usual care plus a five-day course of nirmatrelvir–ritonavir or usual care alone. Across both trials, rates of hospitalization or death within twenty-eight days were very low and did not differ meaningfully between treatment and control groups, with neither trial demonstrating a statistically credible benefit of the antiviral. Although a substudy confirmed that the drug did reduce viral load by the end of treatment, this biological effect did not translate into improved clinical outcomes. The findings indicate that nirmatrelvir–ritonavir does not reduce hospitalization or death in vaccinated higher-risk outpatients with SARS-CoV-2 infection.

Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications

Yuqing Liu, Kun Zhu, Weijun Peng et al.. Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines how the integration of multiomics technologies and artificial intelligence is reshaping the landscape of precision drug discovery and translational medicine. The authors synthesize how multiomics approaches—spanning genomics, transcriptomics, proteomics, and metabolomics—generate deep molecular insights into disease pathogenesis, while advanced deep learning architectures enable the deciphering of complex biological datasets to construct predictive models. The review covers the application of this combined framework across the drug development pipeline, including target identification, drug repositioning, de novo compound discovery, virtual screening, pharmacokinetic modeling, and safety assessment. Disease areas such as oncology, neurodegeneration, and cardiovascular conditions are examined as illustrative cases, with the authors arguing that the fusion of these technologies accelerates hypothesis generation and data-driven therapeutic innovation. The review also acknowledges persistent challenges, including the harmonization of disparate omics data, reproducibility concerns, and the need to address algorithmic biases, while presenting an optimistic vision for bench-to-clinic translational medicine.

Participatory Moral AI Is Not Neutral: The Invisible Hand of Developers

Taenyun Kim, Edyta Bogucka, Daniele Quercia. Participatory Moral AI Is Not Neutral: The Invisible Hand of Developers. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated whether moral preference elicitation—a method in which developers poll people on ethical dilemmas to train AI decision-making policies—is as neutral or objective as it is often assumed to be. The researchers examined three key choices developers make before any voting occurs: which features are included in the dilemmas, which people are invited to vote, and how the questions are worded. Across two study phases and three deployment contexts involving AI systems for kidney allocation, workplace simulation, and generative depictions of deceased individuals, they found that each of these pipeline choices meaningfully shapes the moral preferences that emerge. Morally relevant features varied across deployment contexts, ideological differences in preferences appeared for a substantial portion of features and sometimes reversed direction depending on the voter pool's composition, and question framing could shift ideological gaps by as much as a full scale point while also altering how moral foundations related to participants' judgments. The authors conclude that aggregation-based voting cannot on its own produce fair or transparent AI alignment, and that each stage of the moral elicitation pipeline requires systematic auditing and public disclosure.

Effect of Twisted-Yarn Architecture on Pressure and Proximity Sensing Characteristics of Textile Capacitive Sensors for Robotic Skin

Ishtia Zahir, Eslam Saleh, Maryam Rezayati et al.. Effect of Twisted-Yarn Architecture on Pressure and Proximity Sensing Characteristics of Textile Capacitive Sensors for Robotic Skin. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study investigated how the twisted-yarn architecture of textile capacitive sensors affects their pressure and proximity sensing performance, with applications in wearable electronics and robotic skin. Silver-coated yarns were coated with polydimethylsiloxane and assembled into one-, two-, and four-layer twisted configurations, allowing systematic examination of how electrode overlap area and inter-fiber separation govern capacitive transduction. The researchers found that increasing the number of layers improved both mechanical properties and sensing sensitivity, with the four-layer configuration achieving the highest pressure sensitivity, low hysteresis, minimal thermal drift, and stable performance over tens of thousands of cycles. However, a trade-off was identified between sensitivity and proximity detection range, as higher-layer sensors exhibited greater pressure sensitivity but shorter proximity detection distances. Integration of a textile sensing array into a robotic arm demonstrated real-time spatial contact mapping and proximity detection, establishing yarn architecture as a controllable design parameter for tuning the characteristics of textile-based capacitive sensing systems.

DeaMoE: Efficient MoE Structure for Fast Small-Batch Decoding

Zewen Jin, Shen Fu, Zeping Duan et al.. DeaMoE: Efficient MoE Structure for Fast Small-Batch Decoding. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses a performance bottleneck in Mixture-of-Experts language models during small-batch decoding, a regime common in latency-sensitive applications like coding assistants and real-time audio-video systems. The authors identify that under small-batch conditions, MoE inference becomes memory-bound due to the cost of loading expert weights, and existing remedies such as weight compression or fine-grained expert design trade off accuracy or introduce additional overhead. To solve this, they propose DeaMoE, an architecture that organizes experts into departments where experts within the same department share most of their parameters while retaining a small set of private parameters to preserve individual specialization. They also introduce a customized two-stage routing strategy that avoids redundant weight loading during decoding. The resulting system substantially reduces the amount of weights loaded per decoding step and achieves meaningful end-to-end speedups on standard hardware for both a pretrained model and in benchmarks applied to a large-scale existing MoE model.

Local and Global Regimes of Geometric Complexity in Language Model Representations

Arwa Osman, Marco Baroni, Iuri Macocco. Local and Global Regimes of Geometric Complexity in Language Model Representations. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how intrinsic dimensionality, a measure of representational complexity in language models, is affected by dataset construction choices rather than purely by properties of language itself. The researchers focused on lexical diversity, specifically the number of unique final tokens in a dataset, and examined how it shapes intrinsic dimensionality estimates across different scales. They discovered a scale-dependent transition between two distinct regimes: at low lexical diversity, fewer unique final words paradoxically yield higher intrinsic dimensionality, while at high lexical diversity the relationship reverses, with more unique words producing higher intrinsic dimensionality. The researchers derived an exact, parameter-free formula that accurately predicts the transition point between these two regimes at every scale tested. These findings caution against naively interpreting intrinsic dimensionality as a direct reflection of representational complexity, while also revealing a general organizational principle governing how linguistic data is structured within language model representation spaces.

Accelerating Large-scale Bundle Adjustment for LiDAR Mapping via Parallel Computing

Yixi Cai, Rundong Li, Yuhan Xie et al.. Accelerating Large-scale Bundle Adjustment for LiDAR Mapping via Parallel Computing. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the computational bottleneck of LiDAR bundle adjustment, a technique used to build globally consistent large-scale point cloud maps. The authors propose the first fully parallel computing framework for this task, introducing three core innovations: an adaptive asynchronous data loading strategy for memory-constrained GPUs, a bottom-up voxelization method for parallelized planar feature extraction, and a majorization-minimization-based optimization that parallelizes residual, Jacobian, and Hessian computations alongside a parallel increment solver. The framework's time complexity is analyzed both theoretically and experimentally, and the approach is benchmarked across multiple large-scale public datasets and computational platforms. Results demonstrate substantial gains in computational efficiency compared to existing methods while maintaining mapping accuracy on par with state-of-the-art approaches.

Launching Jets in Tidal Disruption Events: Magnetic Flux Advection and Plasma Loading

Rin Oikawa, Yuri Sato. Launching Jets in Tidal Disruption Events: Magnetic Flux Advection and Plasma Loading. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates why only a small fraction of tidal disruption events, in which a star is torn apart by a black hole's tidal forces, produce relativistic jets. The authors propose a scenario in which magnetic flux stored in a pre-existing low-Eddington accretion disk is advected toward the black hole by the super-Eddington accretion flow that develops after stellar disruption, with low-density stars such as red giants being capable of supplying sufficient magnetic flux to power a jet via the Blandford–Znajek mechanism. They find that once enough magnetic flux accumulates near the black hole, an equatorial current sheet forms where magnetic reconnection generates high-energy gamma rays, and subsequent photon–photon pair production supplies the magnetosphere with the plasma needed to launch and sustain the jet. This mechanism is further shown to simultaneously provide enough radiating particles to explain the observed prompt emission from jetted tidal disruption events, thereby addressing both the magnetic flux origin and the plasma loading problems in a unified framework.

Brief Report: Sustainability of the Effects of Linkage Case Management on Posthospitalization HIV Outcomes: The Daraja Randomized Clinical Trial.

Willkens M, Issarow B, Kisigo GA et al.. Brief Report: Sustainability of the Effects of Linkage Case Management on Posthospitalization HIV Outcomes: The Daraja Randomized Clinical Trial.. Journal of acquired immune deficiency syndromes (1999). 2026

The study examined whether the benefits of a linkage case management intervention for hospitalized people with HIV would persist beyond the original intervention period. Participants from the Daraja randomized clinical trial were followed for an additional year past the primary endpoint, extending observation to two years after hospital discharge, with outcomes including continuity in HIV care, antiretroviral therapy adherence, and viral load suppression. Intervention participants demonstrated significantly higher rates across all three outcomes compared to those receiving standard care, with differences of more than ten percentage points sustained at the two-year mark. A notable proportion of control participants remained untreated even two years after discharge, underscoring the durability of the intervention's advantage. The findings indicate that linkage case management initiated at hospitalization produces lasting improvements in HIV care engagement and treatment outcomes well beyond the active intervention period.

New to cohort, not new to care: leveraging historical data in HIV clinical cohorts in the United States.

Haw NJL, Hogan B, Humes E et al.. New to cohort, not new to care: leveraging historical data in HIV clinical cohorts in the United States.. American journal of epidemiology. 2026

This paper investigated how to distinguish people newly entering HIV care from those transferring between care settings within United States clinical cohorts, a distinction that requires access to historical patient data. The researchers examined the availability of five types of historical HIV data — antiretroviral therapy prescriptions, CD4 counts, viral loads, suppressed viral load at enrollment, and AIDS-defining conditions — across cohorts participating in a large North American collaborative network spanning two decades. Using these data types, they developed an algorithm to classify participants as new to care, transfers, re-engaging after a gap in care, or previously in care with unknown transfer status. They found that the proportion of participants who were transfers grew substantially over time while those genuinely new to care declined, and that the historical timespan between a participant's earliest known HIV date and cohort enrollment also lengthened considerably. The authors recommend that HIV cohorts systematically collect all five data types and apply their classification algorithm to improve the accuracy of care-history characterization in research.

Diagnostic performance of a field-deployable iiPCR platform and viral load dynamics of MPXV Clade IIb in a cohort with high prevalence of HIV.

Liu LT, Chen CJ, Lin PC et al.. Diagnostic performance of a field-deployable iiPCR platform and viral load dynamics of MPXV Clade IIb in a cohort with high prevalence of HIV.. Annals of medicine. 2026

This study evaluated a field-deployable insulated isothermal PCR platform as an alternative to quantitative real-time PCR for diagnosing Mpox caused by MPXV Clade IIb, with particular attention to performance in a cohort with high HIV prevalence. Researchers prospectively enrolled confirmed Mpox patients, testing multiple specimen types with both the portable iiPCR system and reference qPCR, while also assessing viral load dynamics across specimen types and time since symptom onset. The two platforms showed identical analytical limits of detection and complete qualitative concordance, with lesion-derived specimens yielding the highest detection rates and lowest cycle threshold values, while non-lesion specimens showed more variable positivity. Among people living with HIV who were receiving antiretroviral therapy and had preserved immune function, no differences in diagnostic performance were observed compared to HIV-negative individuals. The findings support the potential of the iiPCR platform as a decentralized diagnostic tool for Mpox, particularly when lesion specimens are available.

Comparative evaluation of a multi-antigen malaria rapid diagnostic test against qPCR and microscopy in a co-endemic area of Southern Ethiopia.

Bogale A, Mitiku A, Tefera S et al.. Comparative evaluation of a multi-antigen malaria rapid diagnostic test against qPCR and microscopy in a co-endemic area of Southern Ethiopia.. Scientific reports. 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance of a multi-antigen rapid diagnostic test for malaria in a region of southern Ethiopia where both *Plasmodium falciparum* and *Plasmodium vivax* co-circulate, a setting where conventional HRP2-based tests are limited by low parasite densities and gene deletions. Researchers enrolled febrile patients from health facilities during peak transmission season and compared the Bioline Malaria multi-Ag RDT, which detects HRP2 and dual pLDH, against expert microscopy and quantitative PCR as reference standards. The multi-antigen RDT showed high specificity and strong agreement with microscopy, but its sensitivity declined substantially when qPCR served as the reference, revealing that submicroscopic and low-density infections were frequently missed. qPCR detected the highest overall prevalence of malaria and identified more cases of each species as well as mixed infections than either RDT or microscopy. The authors conclude that while the multi-antigen RDT is clinically useful for routine diagnosis, more sensitive tools are needed for malaria surveillance and elimination efforts in endemic settings.

Characterization of arbovirus infections in patients within Haiti by screening discarded malaria rapid diagnostic test cassettes.

Matulis GA, Smith HP, Hall G et al.. Characterization of arbovirus infections in patients within Haiti by screening discarded malaria rapid diagnostic test cassettes.. PLoS neglected tropical diseases. 2026

This study investigated circulating arboviruses in Haiti during a period when conventional surveillance was disrupted by political instability and security concerns. Researchers repurposed discarded malaria rapid diagnostic test cassettes collected from febrile patients across two Haitian departments, extracting nucleic acids from the test strips and applying a series of real-time PCR assays targeting flaviviruses, alphaviruses, and Oropouche virus, followed by next-generation sequencing and phylogenetic analysis. Dengue virus serotype three was identified as the sole arbovirus detected, with all positive samples originating from collections made in a single year, and phylogenetic analysis placed these sequences in close relationship to strains circulating across the Caribbean region during the same period. No evidence of alphavirus or Oropouche virus infection was found in the subset of samples tested. The findings demonstrate that discarded malaria rapid diagnostic tests can serve as a practical surveillance tool for arboviral pathogens in resource-limited or conflict-affected settings where traditional public health infrastructure is compromised.

Macrophage metabolic exhaustion and PANoptotic cell death drive chronic tissue inflammation in rheumatoid arthritis

Tao Huang, Yoshinori Takashima, Jitendra Kumar et al.. Macrophage metabolic exhaustion and PANoptotic cell death drive chronic tissue inflammation in rheumatoid arthritis. Immunity. 2026

The paper investigated why chronic joint inflammation persists in rheumatoid arthritis despite the presence of macrophages that are normally associated with tissue repair. The researchers found that patient-derived pro-repair macrophages in the synovium produce high levels of the complement protein C1q while simultaneously expressing its receptor, creating an autocrine signaling loop. C1q stimulation drove metabolic exhaustion in these macrophages through the enzyme SARM1, which depleted mitochondrial NAD by converting it into cyclic ADP ribose, ultimately triggering a form of inflammatory programmed cell death called PANoptosis. In vivo experiments confirmed that C1q worsened synovitis while pharmacological inhibition of SARM1 provided therapeutic benefit. The study identifies SARM1 as both a marker of metabolically stressed macrophages and a key executor of the pro-inflammatory cell death that sustains pathological tissue inflammation in rheumatoid arthritis.

Safety of a large language model-based clinical decision support system in African primary healthcare

Ambrose Agweyu, Paul Mwaniki, Wilkister Musau et al.. Safety of a large language model-based clinical decision support system in African primary healthcare. Nature Health. 2026

This study evaluated the safety of a large language model-based clinical decision support system embedded in electronic medical records across primary care clinics in Kenya. Trained physicians retrospectively reviewed over a thousand patient encounter records to assess the frequency of hallucinations, guideline adherence, and the clinical safety of the system's recommendations. The system rarely hallucinated and its guidance overwhelmingly aligned with local clinical guidelines, demonstrating strong technical performance. However, a concerning pattern emerged in how clinicians interacted with the tool: harmful recommendations from the model appeared in final documentation at a meaningful rate, while clinicians less consistently adopted the model's beneficial suggestions that could have mitigated pre-existing risks in their own notes. The authors conclude that while the tool shows promise for supporting quality improvement in primary care settings, the asymmetric uptake of harmful versus beneficial outputs highlights the urgent need for usability improvements, locally tailored safeguards, and prospective trials to establish patient-level benefit.

A Systematic Literature Review of You Only Look Once Architectures (v1–v12) in Healthcare Systems

Özgür Koray Şahingöz, Gozde Karatas Baydoğmus, Emin Kugu. A Systematic Literature Review of You Only Look Once Architectures (v1–v12) in Healthcare Systems. Diagnostics. 2026

This systematic review examined the application of the full YOLO (You Only Look Once) family of object detection architectures, spanning versions one through twelve, to medical imaging and diagnostic tasks in healthcare. Following established reporting guidelines, the authors retrieved and synthesized peer-reviewed studies that applied YOLO models to tasks including disease and lesion detection across radiological, pathological, ophthalmological, and endoscopic domains, evaluating performance through metrics such as average precision, accuracy, inference time, and computational efficiency. The review found that successive architectural generations have brought progressive improvements in diagnostic performance, with two particular versions emerging as the most widely adopted due to their favorable balance between accuracy and computational demand. Despite strong overall performance across diverse clinical imaging contexts, the review identifies persistent challenges around model interpretability, generalization across institutions, and deployment on resource-constrained edge devices. The authors conclude that future research should prioritize explainability in YOLO-based diagnostic systems and the development of energy-efficient model designs.

CPI-Bench: A Comprehensive,Practical and Intelligent Benchmark for Real-World Image Editing

Qinye Zhou, Jun Zheng, Yongchao Du et al.. CPI-Bench: A Comprehensive,Practical and Intelligent Benchmark for Real-World Image Editing. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses limitations in existing image editing benchmarks, which are restricted to simple single-image tasks and struggle to differentiate performance across diverse models or capture real-world complexity. To overcome these shortcomings, the authors propose CPI-Bench, a benchmark comprising three subsets targeting general editing diversity (including multi-image editing), high-frequency practical user scenarios, and reasoning-intensive editing tasks. Evaluations of mainstream image editing models using CPI-Bench demonstrate improved performance differentiation compared to prior benchmarks, providing more reliable quantification of gaps across general, practical, and reasoning-based editing capabilities. Notably, ranking analysis shows that CPI-Bench achieves the highest alignment with a human-preference leaderboard, suggesting it faithfully reflects real-world user judgments and can serve as a robust proxy for human evaluation.

Validating LLM-Modernized Scientific Software Through Differential Fault Injection

Evan Coleman, Yuzhong Shen, Masha Sosonkina et al.. Validating LLM-Modernized Scientific Software Through Differential Fault Injection. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether large language model agents can reliably modernize legacy Fortran scientific software while preserving not just nominal behavior but also responses to faults, perturbations, and reduced precision. The researchers developed a differential fault-injection validation method that instruments a shared computational driver at multiple sites and applies identical, deterministic faults to both original and LLM-modernized code versions, focusing on converted integral kernels within the quantum chemistry package GAMESS. Running thousands of paired injection experiments, they characterized how transient and persistent faults propagate through the self-consistent-field procedure, finding that fault absorption costs followed a predictable contraction-based model and that the campaigns revealed phase-dependent parallel deadlocks and false convergence under reduced precision that nominal testing would miss. Critically, the original and modernized kernels agreed across all paired fault injections, and a synchronization fix made necessary by the fault campaigns also composed correctly with the modernization. The work demonstrates that differential fault injection provides a rigorous validation regime for LLM-driven code modernization beyond what standard correctness testing offers.

The Okinawa Lectures on Entropy

Klaas Landsman. The Okinawa Lectures on Entropy. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper presents a comprehensive set of lecture notes surveying classical and quantum entropy from both mathematical and physical perspectives. It develops classical entropies through the framework of large deviation theory, covering foundational theorems and applications to statistical physics in both Boltzmannian and Gibbsian formulations. Quantum entropies are then explored, with emphasis on their deep connection to classical entropies through statistical hypothesis testing, encompassing Kullback-Leibler, Umegaki, and Rényi relative entropies within a unified framework. The lectures address the distinctly quantum challenge of defining relative entropy for states on subsystems formalized as von Neumann algebras, requiring modular theory and Haagerup's noncommutative function spaces to rigorously recover and compute the Araki and Uhlmann relative entropies. The treatment concludes with the Connes-Størmer-Narnhofer-Thirring entropy as a quantum analogue of the Kolmogorov-Sinai entropy from ergodic theory, with the entire course aimed at being mathematically rigorous while remaining relevant to theoretical physicists, particularly those working on black hole thermodynamics.

Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation

Zhelun Wu. Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper argues that systems using language models to draw conclusions from multiple sources make a design error by concatenating all sources into a single prompt, conflating two fundamentally distinct operations: interpreting individual sources and aggregating those interpretations into a decision. The authors propose separating these operations and define a structured four-field evidence tuple as the interface between them, which they argue constrains the design of both halves. They identify a failure mode they term count-scale drift, where summing unnormalized weights produces a decision threshold that shifts with the number of sources consulted, and show that the vote rule and posterior ordering diverge when source reliabilities differ, with no threshold able to reconcile them. Pooling calibrated log-likelihood ratios is proposed as an arithmetic fix applicable beyond language models to any score-summing system, including triage engines and additive multi-signal detectors. The framework is validated on a longitudinal corpus in both prospective and retrospective settings, where the proposed separation yields substantially improved predictive performance over a hand-crafted baseline, and the authors explicitly delineate what transfers across domains, what must be re-estimated, and what comparisons remain confounded.

RegRole: Regularized Role Detection and Prediction in Temporal Dynamic Networks

Emily J Evans, Weihong Guo, Carlotta Domenicon. RegRole: Regularized Role Detection and Prediction in Temporal Dynamic Networks. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper presents a method for discovering and tracking roles in temporal dynamic networks, where the structure of connections changes over time. The approach uses temporally regularized Non-negative Matrix Factorization to produce a consistent set of roles across all time periods, accompanied by a universal transition matrix describing the probability of moving between roles, with a regularization penalty to prevent abrupt changes in role membership between time steps. The method was evaluated on multiple real-world and synthetic datasets using both engineered and automatically learned features. Results indicate that with appropriate regularization weighting, the approach reduces prediction errors compared to existing techniques and produces a more stable system in which individuals tend to remain in their roles with fewer arbitrary transitions. The model is also reported to scale efficiently to large, sparse graphs.

tuberculosis rapid diagnostic test

Topic mode · generated August 17, 2026 at 13:03 UTC · 18 items

Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.

Walzl G, McHugh TD, Chakaya J et al.. Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.. The Lancet. Microbe. 2027

This review synthesizes major advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarker research over roughly a five-year period, culminating in updated WHO policy guidance that consolidates recommendations for detecting tuberculosis disease and drug resistance into a single framework. Key developments include the expanded role of digital chest radiography with computer-aided detection as a frontline screening tool, and the broadened deployment of decentralised molecular diagnostics such as low-complexity automated and manual nucleic acid amplification tests across diverse specimen types and populations. Progress in drug-resistance testing has been marked by new molecular platforms and the formal endorsement of targeted next-generation sequencing for comprehensive drug-susceptibility profiling at reference laboratory level, while novel specimen types including tongue swabs and pooled sputum have improved bacteriological confirmation in children and others unable to produce sputum. Urine lipoarabinomannan assays offer diagnostic gains in people with HIV and advanced disease, though their broader applicability remains limited, and host blood transcriptomic signatures show promise for triage and prognosis without yet meeting criteria for point-of-care use. The authors conclude that substantial gaps in performance, implementation, and equity persist, and that no biomarker has yet achieved the WHO target product profile for reliably distinguishing infection from disease, particularly subclinical tuberculosis.

The changing landscape of diagnosing HIV-associated tuberculosis.

Maartens G. The changing landscape of diagnosing HIV-associated tuberculosis.. South African medical journal = Suid-Afrikaanse tydskrif vir geneeskunde. 2026

This narrative review examines how the diagnosis of HIV-associated tuberculosis has evolved from 1990 to the present, focusing on the particular challenges posed by this co-infection. People with HIV present diagnostic difficulties because they tend to have lower bacillary loads in sputum, higher rates of extrapulmonary or disseminated disease, and nonspecific clinical features that overlap with other opportunistic infections, while simultaneously requiring rapid diagnosis due to swift disease progression. The review traces the limitations of earlier approaches, including smear microscopy and smear-negative diagnostic algorithms, which contributed to both underdiagnosis and overdiagnosis with consequent increases in mortality and missed alternative diagnoses. The introduction of nucleic acid amplification tests and the urine lipoarabinomannan lateral flow assay, alongside the widespread use of antiretroviral therapy, has substantially improved the diagnostic landscape and contributed to reductions in tuberculosis incidence and deaths. Nevertheless, the authors conclude that significant gaps remain, as no currently available rapid test achieves ideal sensitivity and only urine lipoarabinomannan detection is simultaneously affordable and suitable for point-of-care use, underscoring the continued need for novel accurate diagnostic tools.

Operationalization of rapid molecular test coverage for tuberculosis in different international contexts: a scoping review.

Silva DHMD, Valença ABM, Ferreira MRL et al.. Operationalization of rapid molecular test coverage for tuberculosis in different international contexts: a scoping review.. Revista da Escola de Enfermagem da U S P. 2026

This scoping review examined how coverage of rapid molecular testing for tuberculosis has been conceptualized and measured across different international settings. The authors systematically searched multiple major databases spanning over a decade to identify and synthesize relevant studies on the topic. The review found substantial conceptual heterogeneity in how coverage was defined and operationalized, ranging from testing proportions in specific population groups to indicators of equipment availability and geographic access, with no standardization across countries. Reported coverage values varied enormously, and socioeconomic, structural, and financial barriers were consistently identified as obstacles to test uptake. The authors conclude that meaningful comparison and expansion of rapid molecular test coverage requires greater methodological standardization, governmental commitment, decentralization of testing services, and alignment with international guidelines.

Emerging blood-based diagnostic strategies for tuberculosis.

Yue C, Geng X, Sun X et al.. Emerging blood-based diagnostic strategies for tuberculosis.. Clinical chemistry and laboratory medicine. 2026

This review examines blood-based diagnostic approaches for tuberculosis, a persistent global health threat caused by *Mycobacterium tuberculosis*. The authors survey current serological biomarkers being explored for TB diagnosis and discuss how advanced diagnostic technologies can be integrated with these markers. Serological methods are highlighted for their simplicity, speed, and cost-effectiveness, making them particularly valuable for diagnosing extrapulmonary TB and for patients who test negative using conventional sputum culture or rapid molecular assays. The review argues that continued identification of novel biomarkers, combined with emerging diagnostic platforms, holds meaningful potential to improve TB detection across varied clinical contexts.

Diagnostic accuracy, operational feasibility, and cost considerations of Truenat MTB Plus and Xpert MTB/RIF Ultra for pulmonary tuberculosis.

Sidiq Z, Anand A, Dwivedi KK et al.. Diagnostic accuracy, operational feasibility, and cost considerations of Truenat MTB Plus and Xpert MTB/RIF Ultra for pulmonary tuberculosis.. PloS one. 2026

This study compared two WHO-endorsed nucleic acid amplification tests, Truenat MTB Plus and Xpert MTB/RIF Ultra, for detecting pulmonary tuberculosis and rifampicin resistance in presumptive TB patients in India. Using mycobacterial culture and phenotypic drug susceptibility testing as reference standards, the researchers prospectively tested paired sputum specimens from over six hundred patients at a tuberculosis centre in New Delhi. Xpert MTB/RIF Ultra demonstrated higher sensitivity and specificity for both TB detection and rifampicin resistance identification, and produced substantially fewer invalid and indeterminate results compared to Truenat MTB Plus. However, Truenat offered a considerably lower cost per test, making it more attractive for decentralized deployment despite the increased operational burden associated with its higher rates of invalid and repeat testing. The authors conclude that a tiered diagnostic strategy leveraging both platforms according to their respective strengths could help expand molecular testing access and advance global TB elimination efforts.

Feasibility and acceptability of pooled sputum testing for tuberculosis: a multicountry qualitative study of the Start4All project.

Iem V, Vo LNQ, Codlin AJ et al.. Feasibility and acceptability of pooled sputum testing for tuberculosis: a multicountry qualitative study of the Start4All project.. BMJ open. 2026

This study examined the feasibility and acceptability of pooled sputum testing for tuberculosis, a strategy in which samples from multiple individuals are combined into a single molecular assay to reduce costs and extend access to rapid diagnostics in resource-limited settings. Using a multicountry qualitative design, researchers conducted key informant interviews and focus group discussions with a range of stakeholders—including national tuberculosis programme representatives, laboratory technicians, people with tuberculosis, and international experts—across seven high-burden countries in Africa, Asia, and South America. Feasibility was found to depend on compatibility with existing diagnostic algorithms and laboratory workflows, biosafety standards, staff training, and clear standard operating procedures. Acceptability operated at two levels: securing professional buy-in through early engagement with healthcare providers, and ensuring that people undergoing testing received clear communication about the approach. Overall, pooled testing was considered a pragmatic and workable strategy in settings with sufficiently robust laboratory infrastructure, though readiness varied considerably across countries, with some requiring system strengthening before implementation could be scaled up.

Implementation of the NHS England Lung Cancer Screening Programme over 5 years

Richard W. Lee, Arjun Nair, Haval Balata et al.. Implementation of the NHS England Lung Cancer Screening Programme over 5 years. Nature Medicine. 2026

The paper reports on the implementation and outcomes of the NHS England Targeted Lung Health Check Programme, a national lung cancer screening initiative that began in 2019 and aims for full coverage by 2030. Individuals aged 55 to 74 who had ever smoked were invited for low-dose computed tomography screening if they met risk thresholds determined by multivariable models, delivered through a regionally federated clinical infrastructure guided by national frameworks. Over five years the programme invited more than two million people and diagnosed thousands of lung cancers, with the majority detected at early tumor-node-metastasis stages, representing a meaningful shift toward earlier-stage diagnosis across England and particularly in socioeconomically deprived regions. The authors conclude that large-scale, rapid implementation of lung cancer screening is feasible and scalable in reaching high-risk and underserved populations, though inequalities in participation remain a challenge to be addressed. They argue these findings support broader adoption of lung cancer screening across the United Kingdom and internationally, and offer practical frameworks for adaptation elsewhere.

Osteoarthritis: molecular pathogenesis and potential therapeutic options

Yi Zhang, Yanqi Han, Ying Sun et al.. Osteoarthritis: molecular pathogenesis and potential therapeutic options. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines the molecular mechanisms underlying osteoarthritis, a chronic joint disorder characterized by pain, inflammation, and structural bone and cartilage damage. The authors synthesized current knowledge on nociceptive pathways connecting the knee joint to the central nervous system, as well as pathological changes in bone architecture and chondrocytes driven by inflammatory cytokines and organelle dysfunction. Key drivers of disease progression identified include mitochondrial dysfunction with excessive reactive oxygen species production, lysosomal destabilization caused by hydroxyapatite digestion, and unresolved endoplasmic reticulum stress arising from compensatory protein overproduction. Based on these mechanistic insights, the review outlines emerging pharmacological strategies that simultaneously target inflammatory cascades, restore organelle homeostasis through approaches such as mitophagy enhancement and lysosomal integrity preservation, and attenuate pain signaling. The authors further highlight organelle-targeted drug delivery systems as a means to improve therapeutic efficacy, collectively proposing a multimodal framework for novel osteoarthritis interventions.

The NLRP3 Inflammasome: Mechanisms of Activation, Regulation, and Therapeutic Opportunities

Chan Zou, Shilong Jiang, Hui Li et al.. The NLRP3 Inflammasome: Mechanisms of Activation, Regulation, and Therapeutic Opportunities. MedComm. 2026

This review examines the NLRP3 inflammasome, a key innate immune signaling platform capable of detecting a wide range of microbial, metabolic, and environmental danger signals. The authors synthesize current knowledge on the molecular mechanisms driving NLRP3 activation, including ion flux, mitochondrial damage, lysosomal rupture, reactive oxygen species, and post-translational modifications, as well as the negative regulatory pathways that keep inflammasome activity in check. Activation of this complex recruits ASC and caspase-1, leading to maturation of the pro-inflammatory cytokines IL-1β and IL-18 and induction of pyroptotic cell death, processes that when dysregulated contribute to autoinflammatory syndromes, metabolic and cardiovascular diseases, neurodegeneration, and cancer. The review also surveys therapeutic strategies targeting NLRP3, encompassing direct inhibitors, allosteric modulators, biologics, and repurposed drugs, evaluating their translational prospects. The authors conclude that a deeper understanding of the balance between NLRP3 activation and inhibition opens meaningful opportunities for treating a broad spectrum of inflammatory and immune-related diseases.

EULAR recommendations for the management of Behçet’s syndrome: 2025 update

Gulen Hatemi, Sofia Ramiro, Yeşim Özgüler et al.. EULAR recommendations for the management of Behçet’s syndrome: 2025 update. Annals of the Rheumatic Diseases. 2026

This paper presents an updated set of clinical practice recommendations from a major European rheumatology alliance for the management of Behçet's syndrome, a complex multisystem inflammatory disorder. A large multinational task force spanning multiple medical specialties, including patient representatives, used a systematic literature review informed by structured research questions to revise and expand upon prior guidance. The updated framework comprises five overarching principles and twelve recommendations organized by organ involvement, with one entirely new recommendation and the majority of existing ones substantively modified. Key guidance emphasizes individualizing treatment to disease activity and prognostic factors, with colchicine as first-line therapy for mucocutaneous and joint disease, apremilast or TNFα inhibitors for refractory cases, and early use of monoclonal anti-TNFα agents alongside glucocorticoids and immunosuppressives for organ-threatening or life-threatening manifestations. The recommendations also stress the importance of a multidisciplinary approach, patient education, and shared decision-making to optimize care quality across all affected organ systems.

A systematic literature review on the effects of exercise on human Toll-like receptor expression

Kasper Favere, Matthias Bosman, Peter Delputte et al.. A systematic literature review on the effects of exercise on human Toll-like receptor expression. PubMed. 2026

This systematic review examined how different forms of exercise affect the expression of Toll-like receptors (TLRs), pattern recognition receptors on immune cells that drive inflammatory signalling and are implicated in chronic low-grade inflammation associated with sedentary behaviour and non-communicable diseases. The authors searched multiple databases and included studies in which exercise was the sole intervention in human participants, categorising findings by exercise modality (resistance, aerobic, or combined) and duration (acute or chronic training programs). Acute resistance exercise tended to upregulate TLR expression, whereas acute aerobic exercise more often produced neutral or downregulatory effects, particularly in healthy individuals. Chronic resistance and combined training programs predominantly left TLR levels unchanged or reduced them, while chronic aerobic training produced mixed results with most measurements showing no significant change. The authors conclude that exercise and TLR signalling interact in a modality- and duration-dependent manner, but caution that substantial heterogeneity in study designs limits firm conclusions and that further research is needed to clarify the underlying mechanisms and pathophysiological implications.

CPI-Bench: A Comprehensive,Practical and Intelligent Benchmark for Real-World Image Editing

Qinye Zhou, Jun Zheng, Yongchao Du et al.. CPI-Bench: A Comprehensive,Practical and Intelligent Benchmark for Real-World Image Editing. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses limitations in existing image editing benchmarks, which are restricted to simple single-image tasks with narrow coverage and poor ability to distinguish between model capabilities. To remedy this, the authors introduce CPI-Bench, a new benchmark comprising three subsets targeting general editing diversity (including multi-image editing), high-frequency real-user application scenarios, and reasoning-intensive editing tasks. Evaluations of mainstream image editing models using CPI-Bench show improved differentiation among models across general, practical, and reasoning-based editing dimensions. Notably, the benchmark's rankings align more closely with a human-preference leaderboard than existing alternatives, suggesting it serves as a reliable proxy for real-world user experience and can guide future model optimization.

Validating LLM-Modernized Scientific Software Through Differential Fault Injection

Evan Coleman, Yuzhong Shen, Masha Sosonkina et al.. Validating LLM-Modernized Scientific Software Through Differential Fault Injection. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether large language model agents can reliably modernize legacy Fortran scientific software while preserving not just nominal behavior but also fault-handling characteristics. The researchers developed a differential fault-injection validation framework that instruments a shared computational driver at multiple sites, applying identical deterministic faults to both original and modernized code to isolate and compare the behavior of converted integral kernels. Running thousands of injection experiments, they characterized how transient and persistent faults propagate through the self-consistent-field calculations, finding that fault absorption costs followed a predictable model and that the campaigns revealed phase-dependent parallel deadlocks and false convergence under reduced precision. Critically, the original and LLM-modernized kernels produced fully consistent results across all paired fault injections, and a synchronization fix motivated by the fault campaign also composed correctly with the modernization. The work demonstrates that differential fault injection can serve as a rigorous validation complement to nominal testing when assessing LLM-driven code transformations in production scientific software.

The Okinawa Lectures on Entropy

Klaas Landsman. The Okinawa Lectures on Entropy. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper presents a comprehensive set of lecture notes covering classical and quantum entropy from both mathematical and physical perspectives. The work systematically develops entropy concepts within the frameworks of classical and quantum probability theory, beginning with large deviation theory to ground classical entropies such as Kullback-Leibler relative entropy before extending to their quantum counterparts, including the Umegaki and Rényi entropies, with statistical hypothesis testing serving as the unifying conceptual thread. A central technical challenge addressed is the definition and computation of relative entropy for quantum states on subsystems formalized as von Neumann algebras, which requires the machinery of Tomita-Takesaki modular theory and leads to the Araki and Uhlmann relative entropies as general constructions, with Haagerup's noncommutative function spaces enabling concrete computation in terms of density operators. The notes also introduce the Connes-Størmer-Narnhofer-Thirring entropy as the quantum analogue of Kolmogorov-Sinai entropy in ergodic theory. The material is motivated by connections to black hole thermodynamics and is intended to be simultaneously rigorous for mathematicians and accessible to theoretical physicists with standard graduate prerequisites.

Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation

Zhelun Wu. Split the Labor: Separating Evidence Interpretation from Decision Aggregation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper examines systems that use language models to draw conclusions from multiple sources, arguing that concatenating all sources into a single prompt conflates two fundamentally distinct operations: interpreting individual sources and aggregating those interpretations into a final decision. The authors propose separating these operations and define a four-field evidence tuple as the interface between them, which they argue constrains the design of both the interpretation and aggregation components. They identify a failure mode they term count-scale drift, whereby thresholding summed unnormalized weights functions as posterior thresholding at an operating point that shifts with the number of sources consulted, and show that vote rules and posterior rules order instances differently when source reliabilities vary. Pooling calibrated log-likelihood ratios is offered as an arithmetic rather than architectural fix applicable beyond language models to a broader class of score-summing and additive multi-signal systems. The framework is validated on a longitudinal corpus in both retrospective and prospective settings, where a small sequence encoder combined with a tree ensemble carrying a censored survival loss substantially outperforms a hand-crafted baseline, and the authors explicitly delineate what transfers across domains, what must be re-estimated, and which claims remain open to falsification.

RegRole: Regularized Role Detection and Prediction in Temporal Dynamic Networks

Emily J Evans, Weihong Guo, Carlotta Domenicon. RegRole: Regularized Role Detection and Prediction in Temporal Dynamic Networks. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of detecting and tracking roles that nodes play in networks as they evolve over time. The authors developed a dynamic role discovery method based on temporally regularized Non-negative Matrix Factorization, which enforces consistency in role definitions across time periods and applies a regularization penalty to prevent abrupt changes in role membership between consecutive time steps. A universal transition matrix is learned to characterize the probabilities of nodes moving between roles over time. The method was evaluated on multiple real-world and synthetic datasets using both engineered and automatically extracted features, demonstrating reduced prediction errors compared to existing approaches when an appropriate regularization weight is chosen. Analysis of the transition matrices further showed that the resulting system is more stable, with nodes tending to remain in their roles rather than making arbitrary transitions, and the approach scales efficiently to large, sparse graphs.

Twin: Playing an Unknown Game with a Test-Time Digital Twin

Alexy Skoutnev, Kirill Acharya, Gaston Longhitano et al.. Twin: Playing an Unknown Game with a Test-Time Digital Twin. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces Twin, a Test-time World-model Inference system designed to tackle continual learning tasks such as ARC-AGI-3 games, where the rules and goals of each game are hidden and must be discovered through interaction alone. Rather than hand-engineering a custom model for each task, a frontier coding agent constructs an executable world model by simulating and interacting with the game environment, using a validation mechanism that requires the world model to reproduce every previously observed game transition before any action is taken. When the world model's predictions diverge from actual outcomes, the discrepancy is treated as a counterexample used to repair the model, iteratively refining it toward the true underlying rules. The system achieves near-complete success on the benchmark, clearing the vast majority of levels and outperforming human efficiency on most of those it clears, with the world model approach dramatically improving performance over both the base model alone and a standard off-the-shelf harness. A key finding is that constructing a functional world model proved more tractable than anticipated, while correctly inferring the game's goal—which the system accomplishes before receiving any reward on most cleared levels—emerged as the harder underlying challenge.

sexually transmitted infection point-of-care testing

Community peer-led STI/HIV point-of-care testing and knowledge, attitude and practice (KAP) survey in men who have sex with men (MSM) attending an NGO-led clinic in the outskirts of Beirut (Lebanon).

Abou Shahla W, Rossoni I, Padovese V. Community peer-led STI/HIV point-of-care testing and knowledge, attitude and practice (KAP) survey in men who have sex with men (MSM) attending an NGO-led clinic in the outskirts of Beirut (Lebanon).. Sexually transmitted infections. 2026

This pilot study examined the utility of point-of-care testing for HIV, syphilis, and hepatitis among men who have sex with men attending a non-governmental organisation-led clinic on the outskirts of Beirut, while also assessing their sexual health knowledge, attitudes, and practices. Using a cross-sectional design over four months, a physician conducted sexual health consultations, administered STI and HIV tests, and collected data through an anonymous questionnaire covering demographics, risk behaviours, condom use, chemsex, sexual violence, and perceived stigma. The cohort was predominantly young, educated Lebanese men, though a notable minority were Syrian, and a substantial proportion were living with HIV. Despite high levels of STI and HIV awareness among participants, condomless sex and recreational drug use were common, and HIV-related stigma emerged as a significant barrier to testing and care-seeking. The study concludes that integrating point-of-care testing into community sexual health services enables same-day diagnosis, counselling, and linkage to confirmatory care, representing a valuable approach for reaching this population.

Point-of-care screening for curable sexually transmitted infections in antenatal care in Zimbabwe: a costing study.

Martin K, Bath D, Nyamwanza R et al.. Point-of-care screening for curable sexually transmitted infections in antenatal care in Zimbabwe: a costing study.. Sexually transmitted infections. 2026

This study examined the costs of integrating point-of-care screening for three curable sexually transmitted infections into antenatal care in Zimbabwe, compared to the standard syndromic management approach. Researchers implemented screening at two primary healthcare clinics in Harare and collected economic cost data through staff interviews, time-and-motion studies, and review of study records, then modelled implementation in a low-volume routine care setting over a twelve-month period. The prevalence of the targeted infections was notably high among pregnant women screened, with test kits and personnel representing the dominant cost components. The incremental costs per person screened and treated were substantially higher for point-of-care testing than for syndromic management, primarily driven by test kit and personnel expenses. The authors conclude that reductions in the cost of point-of-care diagnostic tests will be essential for this screening approach to become a feasible and scalable component of routine antenatal care.

Cost-effectiveness of a decentralised molecular point-of-care testing programme for sexually transmitted infections in remote primary care health services in Australia.

Watts CG, Causer LM, Matthews SJ et al.. Cost-effectiveness of a decentralised molecular point-of-care testing programme for sexually transmitted infections in remote primary care health services in Australia.. Sexually transmitted infections. 2026

This study evaluated the cost-effectiveness of a decentralised molecular point-of-care testing programme for sexually transmitted infections in remote Australian First Nations communities, where the burden of chlamydia, gonorrhoea, and trichomonas is disproportionately high among young people. Using a decision analytic model with a government payer perspective and a ten-year time horizon, the researchers compared point-of-care testing against standard laboratory-based testing in terms of costs and quality-adjusted life years gained. The programme was found to be cost-effective relative to the willingness-to-pay threshold used, generating a favourable incremental cost per quality-adjusted life year ratio. Beyond cost-effectiveness, the model predicted meaningful reductions in pelvic inflammatory disease diagnoses and preterm or low birth weight births among those with infections. The analysis concludes that sustained, scaled point-of-care testing in remote primary care settings is a cost-effective strategy likely to improve reproductive health outcomes for this population.

Cost-Effectiveness of Testing and Screening Strategies for Trichomoniasis among Asymptomatic and Symptomatic Women in U.S. Outpatient Clinical Settings.

Rong R, Stoecker C, Kissinger PJ et al.. Cost-Effectiveness of Testing and Screening Strategies for Trichomoniasis among Asymptomatic and Symptomatic Women in U.S. Outpatient Clinical Settings.. Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America. 2026

This study evaluated the cost-effectiveness of various testing and screening strategies for trichomoniasis, the most common non-viral sexually transmitted infection in women, across both symptomatic and asymptomatic populations in U.S. primary care settings. Researchers constructed a decision tree model applied to a hypothetical cohort of women aged 15 to 59 years, comparing eleven strategies using combinations of wet mount microscopy, rapid antigen testing, and nucleic acid amplification testing, while accounting for downstream health consequences including preterm birth, HIV acquisition, and cervical cancer over the cohort's lifetime. They found that adding nucleic acid amplification testing to wet mount microscopy for symptomatic women improved health outcomes and fell within a commonly accepted willingness-to-pay threshold, making it a cost-effective approach. By contrast, adding nucleic acid amplification testing to rapid antigen testing for symptomatic women, as well as universal nucleic acid amplification testing screening of asymptomatic women, exceeded that threshold and were not considered cost-effective at standard benchmarks. The findings suggest that while enhanced testing can improve outcomes for symptomatic women, broader asymptomatic screening would require substantially higher societal willingness-to-pay thresholds to be economically justified.

Mechanisms, management and prevention of anorectal sexually transmitted infections.

Latt PM, Kong FYS, Khosropour CM et al.. Mechanisms, management and prevention of anorectal sexually transmitted infections.. Nature reviews. Gastroenterology & hepatology. 2026

This review examines the epidemiology, pathogenic mechanisms, diagnosis, and prevention of anorectal bacterial sexually transmitted infections, focusing primarily on chlamydia, gonorrhoea, and syphilis. The authors synthesize current evidence on why these infections are rising globally despite available testing, noting that anorectal infections are frequently asymptomatic—particularly among men who have sex with men—due to bacterial adaptation to rectal tissues and attenuated local immune responses. Molecular diagnostics have improved detection, but challenges remain in distinguishing viable from non-viable organisms, expanding point-of-care testing, and managing antimicrobial stewardship, especially as gonorrhoea develops resistance to existing treatments. The review highlights doxycycline post-exposure prophylaxis and artificial intelligence applications as promising prevention advances, while acknowledging that implementing these tools broadly across diverse clinical settings remains difficult. The authors identify key gaps in knowledge and propose research priorities to strengthen control of these infections.

Technological effects on gender studies: An intersectional perspective

Şevki Işıklı, Esra Fatma FAZLIOĞLU. Technological effects on gender studies: An intersectional perspective. Feminist Encounters A Journal of Critical Studies in Culture and Politics. 2026

This paper examines how digitalization and technologization intersect with gender dynamics in contemporary digital culture, asking whether digital transformation constitutes a paradigmatic shift in how gender identities are constructed, performed, and contested. Through a systematic literature review of recent high-impact research, the study draws on feminist theory, sociology, communication studies, health informatics, and STEM education to analyze these intersections from an intersectional perspective. The findings reveal that digital technologies carry genuine emancipatory potential by enabling anonymity, accessibility, and broader public participation, yet simultaneously replicate and intensify patriarchal norms through mechanisms such as cyberviolence, algorithmic bias, digital exclusion, and persistent underrepresentation of women in STEM. Digital spaces are therefore characterized not as ideologically neutral or inherently egalitarian, but as contested terrains where power relations are renegotiated in novel forms. The paper concludes by calling for intersectional, gender-sensitive policies and multi-stakeholder collaborations to resist the reinforcement of masculine dominance and to cultivate inclusive, equitable technological ecosystems.

Compulsive sexual behavior disorder (CSBD) and problematic pornography use (PPU): A comprehensive, interdisciplinary, and expert-informed narrative review with suggested future directions

Campbell Ince, Stephanie Antons, Sarah Ashton et al.. Compulsive sexual behavior disorder (CSBD) and problematic pornography use (PPU): A comprehensive, interdisciplinary, and expert-informed narrative review with suggested future directions. Journal of Behavioral Addictions. 2026

This narrative review examines the current state of knowledge on Compulsive Sexual Behavior Disorder and Problematic Pornography Use, two widely studied but contested constructs relating to dysregulated sexual behavior. Drawing on contributions from experts across psychology, psychiatry, sexology, addiction science, and neuroscience, the authors synthesized empirical and theoretical literature across eleven key domains, including historical context, assessment, epidemiology, etiology, co-occurring disorders, potential harms, and treatment. The review also addresses underrepresented groups such as adolescents, women, and LGBTQ+ individuals, as well as the role of cultural factors in shaping how these behaviors are understood and experienced. The authors identify persistent methodological challenges and ongoing conceptual controversies that have hampered progress in the field. The work concludes by calling for greater interdisciplinary collaboration and more rigorous research to strengthen clinical practice and inform public health policy.

An epidemiological approach to sexually transmitted diseases – with special reference to contact tracing and screening

Kristina Ramstedt. An epidemiological approach to sexually transmitted diseases – with special reference to contact tracing and screening. Acta Dermato Venereologica. 2026

This thesis investigated epidemiological approaches to controlling sexually transmitted diseases, with a focus on contact tracing and screening as tools for identifying asymptomatic infected individuals. The research evaluated contact tracing routines, compared characteristics of chlamydia-infected women and their partners identified through different clinical settings, and assessed whether contact tracing data could inform mathematical models of partner choice and disease spread. Contact tracing successfully controlled a gonorrhoea microepidemic and identified previously unknown HIV-positive individuals, while chlamydia screening in family planning clinics revealed meaningful prevalence and identified key risk factors including young age, short relationship duration, and inconsistent condom use. Comparisons between chlamydia cases found through screening versus those presenting at sexually transmitted disease clinics showed only minor differences in partner numbers and infection rates, leading to the conclusion that both groups warrant equivalent effort in partner management. Partner choice analysis indicated that steady partnerships were relatively restricted while casual partnerships followed a more random pattern, findings that do not support a purely random mixing model for describing disease spread.

A Novel Index Measure of Housing-Related Risk as a Predictor of Overdose Among Young People Who Inject Drugs and Injection Networks

Kathleen Kristensen, Leslie D. Williams, Charlie Kaplan et al.. A Novel Index Measure of Housing-Related Risk as a Predictor of Overdose Among Young People Who Inject Drugs and Injection Networks. Journal of Urban Health. 2026

This study examined the relationship between housing instability and drug overdose risk among young people who inject drugs, with a focus on developing a more comprehensive measure of housing instability than those used in prior research. Using baseline data from a network-based longitudinal study of young people who inject drugs and their social networks in metropolitan Chicago, the researchers constructed a multi-dimensional housing instability risk index comprising five dichotomous variables, then analyzed its association with lifetime overdose count using negative binomial regression. After adjusting for multiple covariates, a significant positive association was found between higher housing instability risk scores and greater lifetime overdose counts. The findings support the value of a multi-dimensional, holistic approach to measuring housing instability over single-dimension operationalizations such as housing type or homelessness status alone. The authors conclude that interventions targeting overdose risk should incorporate this broader conceptualization of housing instability to better identify and address vulnerability in this population.

Ultrasensitive Enzyme-Free Detection of Proteins on Magnetic Beads

Karan Malhotra, Louise L. Hansen, Samuel Han et al.. Ultrasensitive Enzyme-Free Detection of Proteins on Magnetic Beads. Journal of the American Chemical Society. 2026

This paper developed an enzyme-free platform for ultrasensitive protein biomarker detection intended to make advanced diagnostics accessible in resource-limited settings. The researchers adapted the Molecular On-bead Signal Amplification for Individual Counting approach by replacing enzymatic signal amplification with hybridization chain reaction-based amplifiers on magnetic beads, simplifying the assay workflow. The resulting platform, termed efMOSAIC, achieved detection limits comparable to established single-molecule detection methods such as Simoa, which are orders of magnitude more sensitive than conventional ELISA. A key practical advantage demonstrated was that the signal amplifier reagents remained stable at room temperature for at least six months, eliminating the need for cold chain infrastructure. The platform was validated for detecting cytokines in human plasma and shown to support multiplexed biomarker detection, positioning it as a promising tool for ultrasensitive diagnostics in low- and middle-income countries and point-of-care settings.

Cancer Incidence in Women After Medically Assisted Reproduction

Claire Melissa Vajdic, Adrian Walker, Antoinette Anazodo et al.. Cancer Incidence in Women After Medically Assisted Reproduction. JAMA Network Open. 2026

This large population-based cohort study examined cancer incidence among Australian women who underwent medically assisted reproduction, comparing outcomes against age- and calendar year-matched general population data using linked administrative health and cancer registry records. Three treatment groups were studied: assisted reproductive therapy, intrauterine insemination with ovarian stimulation, and ovulation induction with clomiphene citrate, encompassing hundreds of thousands of women followed over many years. Overall invasive cancer incidence was comparable to the general population for all three treatment groups, though the clomiphene citrate group showed a slight elevation. Certain site-specific cancers, including uterine cancer, melanoma, and ovarian cancer, were elevated across some or all cohorts, while cervical and lung cancers occurred less frequently than expected; however, the absolute excess numbers of cases were small. The authors conclude that while medically assisted reproduction does not appear to meaningfully increase overall cancer risk, the findings on specific cancer types warrant attention in clinical counselling, though causation cannot be established from this descriptive evidence.

Validating LLM-Modernized Scientific Software Through Differential Fault Injection

Evan Coleman, Yuzhong Shen, Masha Sosonkina et al.. Validating LLM-Modernized Scientific Software Through Differential Fault Injection. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of validating large language model-driven modernization of legacy scientific software, specifically questioning whether such transformations preserve not just nominal behavior but also how code responds to faults, numerical perturbations, and reduced precision. The researchers developed a differential fault-injection methodology that instruments a shared computational driver in the quantum chemistry package GAMESS at multiple sites, applying identical deterministic faults to both original and modernized code versions to isolate differences in the converted components. Across thousands of runs, the method characterized how transient and persistent faults propagate through self-consistent-field calculations, revealing phase-dependent parallel deadlocks and false convergence under reduced precision that nominal testing would miss. Critically, the original and LLM-modernized integral kernels showed complete agreement across all paired fault injections, and a synchronization fix derived from the campaign also composed correctly with the modernization. The work establishes differential fault injection as a rigorous complement to nominal validation for assessing the fidelity of automated code modernization.

The Okinawa Lectures on Entropy

Klaas Landsman. The Okinawa Lectures on Entropy. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper presents a comprehensive set of lecture notes covering classical and quantum entropy from both mathematical and physical perspectives. The work develops classical entropies through large deviation theory and connects them to statistical physics, then extends the treatment to quantum entropies by emphasizing their role in statistical hypothesis testing as the deeper structural link to their classical counterparts. The quantum treatment progresses through increasingly sophisticated mathematical machinery, including Tomita-Takesaki modular theory and Haagerup's noncommutative function spaces, to rigorously define and compute relative entropies such as those introduced by Araki and Uhlmann on von Neumann algebras. The lectures also introduce a quantum analogue of Kolmogorov-Sinai dynamical entropy in the setting of von Neumann algebras and ergodic theory. The course is designed to be simultaneously rigorous for mathematicians and accessible to theoretical physicists, with black hole thermodynamics as a motivating application, though that application itself is not covered.

Learning-to-Transition for Large-scale and High-Order MIMO Detection

Yubo Zhang, Yiyao Liu, Xiaodong Wang. Learning-to-Transition for Large-scale and High-Order MIMO Detection. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of high-order MIMO detection, which requires searching over large discrete symbol spaces while providing reliable soft information for channel decoding. The authors develop a learning-to-transition framework that models MIMO detection as a stochastic sequence of complete-vector transitions, using a channel-coupled Transformer with a blockwise autoregressive factorization to capture inter-stream dependencies at manageable complexity. For hard-output detection, the system is trained through a residual-to-bit-error-rate curriculum that first learns the search geometry from residual metrics before aligning with transmitted-bit accuracy. For soft-output detection within an iterative detection and decoding receiver, the trained hard-output policy is transferred to each receiver layer through a tied-to-untied parameter cloning strategy, allowing layer-specific specialization under decoder feedback while preserving the original search dynamics. The framework incorporates Bayesian prior tilting during candidate generation and a multi-stage training strategy to stabilize the transition from hard to soft detection, ultimately producing posterior and extrinsic log-likelihood ratios compatible with LDPC decoding.

RegRole: Regularized Role Detection and Prediction in Temporal Dynamic Networks

Emily J Evans, Weihong Guo, Carlotta Domenicon. RegRole: Regularized Role Detection and Prediction in Temporal Dynamic Networks. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the problem of identifying and tracking roles that nodes play in networks that evolve over time. The authors developed a dynamic role discovery method based on temporally regularized Non-negative Matrix Factorization, which enforces consistency in role definitions across all time periods and applies a regularization penalty to prevent abrupt changes in role membership between consecutive time steps. Unlike existing approaches, the method also learns a universal transition matrix that quantifies the probability of moving between roles over time. Testing on multiple real-world and one synthetic dataset, using both engineered and automatically generated node features, showed that appropriate regularization reduces role prediction errors compared to alternative techniques. Analysis of the transition matrices further revealed that the resulting system is more stable, with individuals tending to remain in their roles rather than making arbitrary transitions, while the approach also scales efficiently to large, sparse graphs.

Twin: Playing an Unknown Game with a Test-Time Digital Twin

Alexy Skoutnev, Kirill Acharya, Gaston Longhitano et al.. Twin: Playing an Unknown Game with a Test-Time Digital Twin. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a system called Twin (Test-time World-model Inference) designed to play unknown games by constructing executable world models at test time, without any prior knowledge of the game's rules or goals. Rather than relying on hand-engineered models, the system uses a frontier coding agent to infer game transitions and objectives purely through simulation and interaction, with a digital twin world model used to validate actions by ensuring the program reproduces every previously observed game transition before any real action is taken. Mismatches between world model predictions and actual outcomes serve as counterexamples that trigger automatic repair of the model. The system was evaluated on a continual learning benchmark of grid-based games, where it successfully cleared the vast majority of levels, outperforming human efficiency on most of them and frequently inferring the correct goal before receiving any reward signal. A key finding is that constructing a functional world model proved more tractable than anticipated, while correctly identifying the goal emerged as the more challenging aspect of the problem.

Isomorphism of tournaments with bounded VC dimension

Simon Raßmann, Pascal Schweitzer. Isomorphism of tournaments with bounded VC dimension. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigates the computational complexity of the tournament isomorphism problem, which is a central bottleneck in developing faster algorithms for graph isomorphism. The authors focus on tournaments of bounded VC dimension, a class for which no prior algorithmic results existed despite the VC dimension being a fundamental notion of combinatorial tameness. To resolve an open problem, they develop a new isomorphism-invariant decomposition method for tournaments, introduce the concept of a patched tournament to enable recursion, and design a recursive algorithm that balances piece sizes against their number while exploiting near-twin structure. Their main result establishes that isomorphism of tournaments with VC dimension bounded by a parameter can be decided in quasipolynomial time in the input size, and that automorphism groups of such tournaments can be computed in polynomial time. As a complementary result, they also show that tournament isomorphism is polynomial-time decidable for tournaments of bounded chromatic number, addressing the class that characterizes unbounded VC dimension in hereditary tournament families.
