CRISPR-Cas12a assay for rapid and specific detection of &lt;i&gt;Shigella flexneri 2a&lt;/i&gt; in clinical samples.

Liao J, Su Y, Jiang F. CRISPR-Cas12a assay for rapid and specific detection of &lt;i&gt;Shigella flexneri 2a&lt;/i&gt; in clinical samples.. Journal of clinical microbiology. 2026

The paper developed and validated a CRISPR-Cas12a diagnostic assay for the rapid, specific detection of *Shigella flexneri* 2a, the leading serotype causing shigellosis and a significant public health burden in developing countries. The assay couples recombinase polymerase amplification with Cas12a-mediated collateral cleavage, targeting two genes unique to the *S. flexneri* 2a serotype, and supports both fluorescence and lateral flow readouts without requiring specialized equipment. The method achieves a detection limit of ten copies per microliter, completes within one hour at room temperature, and showed complete analytical specificity with no cross-reactivity against other bacteria. Clinical validation using a large cohort of stool specimens demonstrated diagnostic sensitivity and specificity comparable to conventional PCR. The authors conclude that the assay's simplicity, speed, and accuracy make it well-suited for point-of-care diagnostics and field-based surveillance in resource-limited settings.

An integrated centrifugal microfluidic CRISPR-based diagnostics platform for multiplexed point-of-care testing of respiratory pathogens.

Li L, Wei H, Wei M et al.. An integrated centrifugal microfluidic CRISPR-based diagnostics platform for multiplexed point-of-care testing of respiratory pathogens.. Biosensors & bioelectronics. 2026

The study developed a centrifugal microfluidic diagnostic platform called iCARD to enable rapid, multiplexed point-of-care detection of respiratory pathogens while overcoming sensitivity and complexity trade-offs in existing systems. The platform integrates a polymethyl methacrylate rotating disc pre-loaded with lyophilized CRISPR/Cas13a reagents with a portable fluorescence analyzer, and employs a novel resin-based nucleic acid purification method for throat swab samples. The system can simultaneously screen six respiratory pathogens across four independent samples in a single automated run. Analytical validation confirmed single-copy sensitivity with no cross-reactivity, and clinical validation using retrospective patient samples demonstrated very high diagnostic sensitivity and perfect specificity. The authors conclude that iCARD represents a robust and accurate tool suitable for decentralized molecular screening and epidemiological surveillance.

CRISPR RNA Engineering Enables Single Nucleotide Polymorphism Discrimination in Nucleic Acid Detection.

Sun T, Yuan A, Xie W et al.. CRISPR RNA Engineering Enables Single Nucleotide Polymorphism Discrimination in Nucleic Acid Detection.. Angewandte Chemie (International ed. in English). 2026

This review examines how CRISPR-based diagnostic systems can be engineered to achieve single-nucleotide discrimination in nucleic acid detection, addressing the persistent challenge of off-target activation and insufficient specificity when distinguishing closely related sequences. The authors synthesize advances in CRISPR RNA engineering, detailing how structural features such as spacer length, intentional mismatch design, secondary-structure modulation, chemical modification, and strand-displacement gating collectively govern target recognition and cleavage fidelity. Key strategies including computational prediction, high-throughput screening, and hybrid guide architectures are assessed for their ability to improve single-nucleotide polymorphism discrimination, stabilize assay performance, and enable multiplexed detection of pathogens and disease-associated mutations. The review identifies remaining obstacles including interference from complex clinical samples, the absence of unified evaluation standards, and scalability limitations that currently impede clinical translation. The authors conclude that integrated crRNA design combined with system-level optimization and standardized benchmarking will be essential to realizing portable, ultrasensitive point-of-care diagnostics capable of supporting precision medicine and infectious disease surveillance.

SARS-CoV-2 Point-of-Care Testing Modalities: Integrating Molecular, Immunological, Biosensor, and AI Approaches.

Hetta HF, Ahmed R, Haseeb A et al.. SARS-CoV-2 Point-of-Care Testing Modalities: Integrating Molecular, Immunological, Biosensor, and AI Approaches.. Diagnostics (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

This review examined the landscape of point-of-care testing technologies developed for COVID-19 diagnosis, motivated by the pandemic's demonstration of urgent need for rapid, decentralized diagnostic tools. The authors conducted a structured literature search across major databases and evaluated molecular assays, antigen and antibody tests, biosensor platforms, and artificial intelligence-assisted diagnostic approaches. Molecular methods including rapid RT-PCR, LAMP, and CRISPR-based technologies were found to offer high sensitivity and specificity suitable for decentralized use, while antigen tests provide speed and affordability but with variable performance depending on viral load, symptom timing, and circulating variants. Antibody-based testing was deemed more appropriate for seroprevalence and immune monitoring than acute infection detection, and emerging biosensor and AI systems showed promise but largely remain at prototype or early-validation stages requiring further clinical assessment. The review concludes that no single testing modality is universally optimal, and that the complementary strengths of different approaches, combined with advances in multiplexing, digital connectivity, and variant-resilient design, will be essential for both COVID-19 management and future infectious disease preparedness.

CRISPR-Cas and Infectious Diseases: A Decade of Translational Advances in Molecular Biotechnology.

Mayuri K, Saravanan KM, Somala CS et al.. CRISPR-Cas and Infectious Diseases: A Decade of Translational Advances in Molecular Biotechnology.. Biochemical genetics. 2026

This review examines the translational progress of CRISPR-Cas systems across three major applications in infectious disease management: diagnostics, therapeutic genome editing, and vaccine development. The authors surveyed a decade of advances, focusing on diagnostic platforms that enable rapid pathogen detection, CRISPR-Cas9-based strategies targeting viral genomes and antimicrobial resistance, and the engineering of live-attenuated and personalized neoantigen vaccines. They found that CRISPR-based diagnostics have advanced furthest toward clinical evaluation, while therapeutic applications and personalized neoantigen vaccines remain at earlier, more exploratory stages, largely due to challenges in delivery, safety, and regulatory oversight. The review uniquely integrates all three application areas within a single infectious disease framework and critically assesses the maturity of each, identifying off-target effects, delivery inefficiency, and ethical concerns as key barriers to safe and equitable clinical translation. The authors conclude that although CRISPR-Cas holds transformative potential for infectious disease management, these technical and regulatory challenges must be systematically addressed before widespread clinical implementation can be realized.

PCR and molecular diagnostics: Biotechnologic integration in clinical laboratories.

Saleem MA, Riaz T, Saleem F et al.. PCR and molecular diagnostics: Biotechnologic integration in clinical laboratories.. Clinica chimica acta; international journal of clinical chemistry. 2026

This review paper examines the evolution and clinical integration of polymerase chain reaction technology and related molecular diagnostic tools in laboratory medicine. The authors survey the progression from traditional PCR to advanced platforms including quantitative, digital, reverse transcription, and multiplex variants, assessing how each contributes to nucleic acid detection, quantification, and high-throughput analysis. The paper also explores how PCR-based approaches can be combined with next-generation sequencing, CRISPR-based diagnostics, microarrays, and epigenetic profiling to provide more comprehensive characterization of disease states. Particular attention is given to clinically relevant molecular targets in oncology, such as mutations in cancer-associated genes and circulating tumor DNA, as well as applications in infectious disease diagnosis, antimicrobial resistance tracking, inherited disorder detection, and minimal residual disease monitoring. The authors conclude that while challenges related to cost, standardization, skilled personnel, and data management persist, ongoing technological advances including liquid biopsy, point-of-care platforms, and artificial intelligence-assisted multi-omics integration are steadily expanding access to precision molecular diagnostics.

Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms

Purshottam J. Assudani, Balakrishnan . P., Anny Leema A et al.. Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms. Microchimica Acta. 2026

This review examines the current landscape of biosensing technologies designed to detect priority foodborne pathogens, including Escherichia coli, Salmonella, and Listeria monocytogenes, with implications for both food safety and clinical healthcare. The authors survey electrochemical, optical, and microfluidic sensing platforms, emphasizing how advances in functional nanomaterials, aptamers, nanozymes, and surface engineering strategies have substantially improved sensor sensitivity, specificity, and performance in complex food matrices. A central theme is the integration of artificial intelligence and machine learning into these biosensing systems, enabling real-time analytics, intelligent pattern recognition, and high-throughput multiplexing that transform conventional detection tools into smart diagnostic devices. The review critically addresses persistent challenges such as matrix interference, bioreceptor instability, scalability constraints, and the absence of regulatory standardization, while proposing mitigation strategies including ratiometric sensing, microfluidic pre-treatment, explainable AI frameworks, and printable electronics. Looking ahead, the authors advocate for self-powered biosensors, modular pathogen panel hubs, blockchain-integrated data management, and standardized validation pipelines as pathways toward regulation-ready platforms for next-generation food safety monitoring.

Cell death in cancer

Marcus Conrad, Andreas Strasser, Philipp J. Jost et al.. Cell death in cancer. Cell. 2026

This review examines the diverse programs by which cancer cells die and how their evasion contributes to tumor development, progression, and treatment resistance. The authors synthesize knowledge across apoptosis and more recently characterized modalities including necroptosis, pyroptosis, and ferroptosis, framing these within a unified conceptual model of cancer biology. While apoptosis remains the dominant mechanism triggered by conventional radiation and chemotherapy, non-apoptotic programs are highlighted for their capacity to generate inflammatory signals that shape interactions between tumor cells, stromal tissue, and the immune system, with consequent effects on immunotherapy efficacy. Ferroptosis receives particular attention as a distinct, iron-dependent modality driven by lipid peroxidation and perturbations in redox and iron metabolism, lacking a single canonical induction signal. The review ultimately argues that a deeper mechanistic understanding of these cell death programs, and of how tumors resist them, is essential for developing strategies to restore cancer cell death therapeutically.

Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications

Deepak S. Chauhan, Rajendra Prasad, Mukesh Dhanka et al.. Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines nanoparticle-based phototherapy systems, which combine light-activated mechanisms with nanomaterials to treat a broad range of diseases with improved precision. The authors survey the core components, classification schemes, and delivery platforms—including gold nanoparticles, liposomes, and dendrimers—that enhance light absorption, reactive oxygen species generation, and localized heating in photodynamic and photothermal therapy. At the molecular level, the paper dissects how light-triggered interventions induce oxidative stress, activate apoptotic and autophagic pathways, and modulate immune responses including cytokine profiles and T-cell activity, with relevance not only to cancer but also to cardiovascular, neurodegenerative, metabolic, and autoimmune conditions. The review further evaluates regulatory-approved agents and recent clinical trial findings, while identifying key barriers to clinical translation such as nanoparticle optimization, effective light delivery, and regulatory challenges. The authors conclude that integrating molecular understanding with clinical advances positions nanoparticle-based phototherapy as a transformative noninvasive strategy for a wide spectrum of diseases.

New approach methodologies for drug discovery

Wenqiang Liu, Paul Pang, Catherine A. Wu et al.. New approach methodologies for drug discovery. Cell. 2026

This review examines new approach methodologies (NAMs) as alternatives to traditional animal-based drug discovery, which has long been hampered by high failure rates. The authors survey recent advances in stem cell-based, organoid-based, and computational approaches that now cover the full drug-discovery continuum, from disease modeling and drug design through efficacy testing. The review highlights progress in identifying therapeutic candidates, developing novel experimental models and technologies, and even exploring NAMs themselves as potential treatments in preclinical and clinical settings. The authors also assess the biological, technical, and regulatory obstacles that must be overcome for these methods to achieve robust translational adoption. They conclude by outlining future human-centric drug-discovery pipelines and the societal considerations necessary for the responsible integration of NAMs into mainstream pharmaceutical development.

Comprehensive Review on Candidemia: Epidemiology, Diagnosis, Treatment, and Future Directions

Haojie Dong, Minghui Song, Jingfang Sun et al.. Comprehensive Review on Candidemia: Epidemiology, Diagnosis, Treatment, and Future Directions. iFungi. 2026

This review paper examined candidemia, a serious bloodstream infection caused by Candida species that ranks among the most significant hospital-acquired infections due to its high mortality and healthcare burden. The authors conducted a comprehensive literature synthesis covering the epidemiology and species distribution of candidemia, the shortcomings of conventional diagnostic methods, and the challenges of optimizing antifungal treatment in the context of emerging resistance. The review evaluated both established and emerging diagnostic technologies, as well as therapeutic strategies ranging from empiric and targeted antifungal regimens to combination therapies and the management of mixed infections. The authors also assessed the pipeline of novel antifungal agents targeting new fungal pathways and explored future directions including the application of artificial intelligence to diagnostics and the development of vaccine-based prophylaxis. Overall, the review argues that advances in rapid diagnostics, susceptibility-guided therapy, and innovative treatment approaches are essential to improving patient outcomes and addressing the evolving challenges of candidemia management.

The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development

Emad M. Abdallah, Abdulrahman Mohammed Alhudhaibi, Mahmoud M. Dahab et al.. The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development. Frontiers in Pharmacology. 2026

This narrative review examines antimicrobial resistance as a major global public health threat, focusing on the bacterial pathogens designated as priorities by the World Health Organization. The authors synthesized current knowledge on the molecular mechanisms by which these pathogens resist antibiotics, encompassing reduced membrane permeability, efflux pumps, enzymatic inactivation, target modification, biofilm formation, and horizontal gene transfer. The review critically evaluates why conventional antibiotic therapies and traditional drug discovery pipelines have proven inadequate against multidrug-resistant organisms, while assessing emerging approaches such as artificial intelligence-assisted drug discovery, phage therapy, antimicrobial peptides, CRISPR-based systems, and natural product-derived compounds in terms of their pharmacological feasibility and translational barriers. The authors also address practical challenges including toxicity, dosing, resistance emergence, and regulatory obstacles that complicate real-world implementation of these strategies. The review concludes that combating WHO-priority pathogens requires integrated, multidisciplinary efforts spanning molecular biology, pharmacology, clinical translation, and public health, including antimicrobial stewardship and a One Health framework that encompasses human, animal, and environmental sectors.

Blazar Boosted Dark Matter in IceCube

Alberto M. Gago, Jaime Hoefken Zink, Joel Jones-Pérez et al.. Blazar Boosted Dark Matter in IceCube. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether the IceCube neutrino detector can probe dark matter particles that have been accelerated to relativistic speeds by proton jets in blazars, a process known as blazar boosting. The authors computed the diffuse dark matter flux from a large sample of blazars, using proton spectra inferred from multiwavelength observations and incorporating dark matter spike profiles around the supermassive black holes at their centers, while accounting for propagation and attenuation effects. The dark matter-nucleon scattering cross section was carefully modeled across multiple interaction regimes—elastic scattering, resonant single-pion production, and deep inelastic scattering—to smoothly bridge the transition between low- and high-energy interaction channels. Applying this framework to IceCube data, the authors derived constraints on the dark matter parameter space and found that this approach can exceed the sensitivity of conventional direct-detection experiments for dark matter masses below roughly one GeV. They also found that the signal is dominated by deep inelastic scattering, making it more sensitive to heavier mediators, while resonant pion production modestly suppresses event rates near the detector threshold.

Cosmic Ray Diffusion and the Origin of Very High Energy Gamma-Ray Emission in Young Massive Stellar Clusters

Lucas Barreto-Mota, Elisabete M. de Gouveia Dal Pino, Gaetano Di Marco et al.. Cosmic Ray Diffusion and the Origin of Very High Energy Gamma-Ray Emission in Young Massive Stellar Clusters. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how cosmic rays accelerated in young massive stellar clusters propagate through their environments and produce the extended very high energy gamma-ray halos detected by observatories such as LHAASO. The authors first develop a theoretical framework showing that magnetic mirror diffusion combined with pitch-angle scattering in turbulent flows suppresses cosmic-ray transport, yielding an energy-dependent diffusion coefficient scaling as the cube root of energy. They then couple three-dimensional magnetohydrodynamic simulations of a young massive stellar cluster with Monte Carlo cosmic-ray propagation calculations, using the cluster W43 as a benchmark case and testing both a central point source and a spherical shell injection geometry. Their results demonstrate that this suppressed diffusion regime, combined with a standard injection spectrum, successfully reproduces the gamma-ray spectra observed by Fermi and LHAASO, whereas conventional stronger energy-dependent diffusion requires an unusually hard injection spectrum to match the data. The study concludes that the observed gamma-ray emission is governed primarily by the diffusion regime rather than the specific location where cosmic rays are injected, and that the relative contributions of leptonic and hadronic emission processes depend sensitively on which diffusion regime operates.

CPI-Bench: A Comprehensive,Practical and Intelligent Benchmark for Real-World Image Editing

Qinye Zhou, Jun Zheng, Yongchao Du et al.. CPI-Bench: A Comprehensive,Practical and Intelligent Benchmark for Real-World Image Editing. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses limitations in existing image editing benchmarks, which are restricted to simple single-image tasks and struggle to differentiate model performance across diverse and complex scenarios. To overcome these shortcomings, the authors developed CPI-Bench, a benchmark comprising three subsets targeting general editing coverage (including multi-image tasks), practical high-frequency user scenarios, and reasoning-intensive editing tasks. The benchmark was used to evaluate mainstream image editing models, revealing meaningful performance distinctions that prior benchmarks could not capture. Ranking analysis showed that CPI-Bench achieves stronger alignment with a human-preference leaderboard than existing benchmarks, suggesting it serves as a reliable proxy for real-world user experience and evaluation.

GPU implementation of mixed quantum-classical Liouville molecular dynamics without momentum jump

Koji Ando. GPU implementation of mixed quantum-classical Liouville molecular dynamics without momentum jump. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper describes a GPU-based implementation of mixed quantum-classical Liouville molecular dynamics using a momentum-jump-free theoretical framework. To optimize performance on GPU hardware, the authors eliminated trajectory spawning — a sampling enhancement technique previously used in CPU implementations — because it introduces thread divergence and dynamic memory allocation overhead that are particularly costly on GPUs. This redesign achieved roughly an order-of-magnitude speedup relative to the CPU implementation with spawning, while also delivering linear scaling with the number of sampling trajectories.

MagnifiQ: Patch-aware Text Guided Progressive Upscaling for High-Resolution Image Restoration

Mahesh Reddy, Yashesh Savani, Antoine Mercier et al.. MagnifiQ: Patch-aware Text Guided Progressive Upscaling for High-Resolution Image Restoration. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of restoring degraded images at very high resolutions, where maintaining global structural consistency while recovering fine local details is particularly difficult. The authors introduce MagnifiQ, a framework that progressively upscales and restores images through multiple intermediate resolution stages rather than attempting direct reconstruction at the target resolution. The approach adapts a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model by replacing self-attention layers with convolutional operations to achieve linear rather than quadratic computational scaling with resolution, and incorporates patch-specific text prompts to provide spatially localized semantic guidance that controls content drift during restoration. Experiments on both synthetic and real-world degraded images demonstrate that MagnifiQ outperforms existing diffusion-based restoration methods in perceptual quality and human preference, producing sharper textures and more globally coherent results at high resolution.

Decoding the Past: An Uncertainty-Aware Deep Learning Framework for Sex Attribution in Prehistoric Hand Stencils

Karel Becerra, Boris Mederos, Dean Snow et al.. Decoding the Past: An Uncertainty-Aware Deep Learning Framework for Sex Attribution in Prehistoric Hand Stencils. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the longstanding challenge of determining the biological sex of individuals who created Upper Paleolithic hand stencils in prehistoric rock art, a problem complicated by the lack of ground truth, population variation, and image degradation over millennia. The researchers developed an uncertainty-aware deep learning framework that explicitly models and propagates uncertainty throughout the analytical pipeline, combining multiple image processing strategies, contour extraction techniques, and silhouette augmentation to generate numerous plausible realizations of each stencil's boundary. Two ensembles of deep neural networks with different architectures were trained on a large dataset of contemporary hand samples and then applied to the prehistoric stencils, with predictions further validated through unsupervised dimensionality reduction and explainable AI methods to ensure anatomical consistency. On contemporary data the ensembles achieved strong classification accuracy, particularly for older age groups, and when applied to prehistoric stencils the framework produced both sex attributions and quantified confidence measures that distinguish morphologically clear cases from ambiguous ones. The authors argue that by treating uncertainty as a measurable and informative component rather than an obstacle, their approach enables more robust and reproducible archaeological inference about the makers of ancient hand stencils.

Noninvasive SARS-CoV-2 detection using a low-cost electronic nose.

Fialkovitz G, Sousa PL, Hidifira AM et al.. Noninvasive SARS-CoV-2 detection using a low-cost electronic nose.. The Brazilian journal of infectious diseases : an official publication of the Brazilian Society of Infectious Diseases. 2026

This study investigated whether a low-cost, noninvasive electronic nose could accurately detect SARS-CoV-2 infection as an alternative to conventional diagnostic methods. The device used an array of commercially available metal oxide gas sensors to analyze volatile organic compounds in both saliva and exhaled breath samples, with multiple machine learning algorithms applied to classify infected versus non-infected individuals. Detection accuracy varied across algorithms for both sample types, with the best performance achieved using the K-Nearest Neighbors algorithm, which yielded area under the curve values approaching or exceeding the threshold considered clinically useful. The findings demonstrate proof-of-concept feasibility for this approach in a real-world hospital setting, suggesting that breath and saliva-based electronic nose testing could offer a rapid, comfortable, and affordable point-of-care screening option. The authors conclude that further development of this technology holds promise for improving early-stage SARS-CoV-2 detection.

Multiniche mycobiome profiling identifies distinctive fungal dysbiosis in common variable immunodeficiency.

Cabanero-Navalon MD, Carda-Diéguez M, Mira A et al.. Multiniche mycobiome profiling identifies distinctive fungal dysbiosis in common variable immunodeficiency.. Frontiers in immunology. 2026

This study investigated the fungal microbiome (mycobiome) across multiple body compartments in patients with common variable immunodeficiency (CVID), a condition previously linked to bacterial dysbiosis but poorly characterized in terms of fungal communities. Using ITS1 amplicon sequencing of saliva, sputum, and stool samples from adults with CVID and healthy controls, the researchers analyzed fungal diversity, composition, and differential abundance, and applied machine learning to identify potential biomarkers. They found that mycobiome composition differed significantly between CVID patients and controls across all three niches, with reduced fungal richness and evenness particularly in respiratory and oral samples. A consistent enrichment of opportunistic yeasts, especially *Candida albicans*, was observed in CVID patients regardless of whether they had immune dysregulation or infectious-only manifestations, accompanied by depletion of commensal and environmental taxa. Fungal community profiles showed strong discriminatory power for distinguishing CVID from healthy controls, suggesting that mycobiome signatures hold promise as non-invasive biomarkers and that *C. albicans* expansion may play a pathobiont role in this immunodeficiency.

Salivary exosomal &lt;i&gt;Mycobacterium tuberculosis&lt;/i&gt; DNA enables sensitive detection of paucibacillary tuberculosis: a molecular diagnosis study.

Ma J, Zheng X, He Y et al.. Salivary exosomal &lt;i&gt;Mycobacterium tuberculosis&lt;/i&gt; DNA enables sensitive detection of paucibacillary tuberculosis: a molecular diagnosis study.. Frontiers in cellular and infection microbiology. 2026

This study investigated whether tuberculosis could be diagnosed using *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* DNA extracted from exosomes in saliva, offering a non-sputum-based alternative for detecting paucibacillary disease. The researchers developed a method called ExoNA-based saliva tuberculosis diagnostic, which integrates salivary exosome enrichment, nucleic acid extraction, and dual-target quantitative PCR melting curve analysis, reducing turnaround time substantially compared to conventional approaches. In a pilot clinical study enrolling adults with suspected pulmonary tuberculosis, the majority of whom had negative smear microscopy, the assay demonstrated moderate sensitivity and specificity against both standard and extended microbiological reference standards. Salivary exosomal DNA was found to be the predominant detectable form of *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* DNA in saliva, and diagnostic performance was maintained in the smear-negative subgroup. The authors conclude that this non-invasive, rapid assay holds promise for tuberculosis diagnosis but requires multicenter validation in more diverse populations before clinical implementation.

Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration

Thirumalaisamy Suryaprabha, Chunghyeon Choi, Yanfang Wu et al.. Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration. npj Flexible Electronics. 2026

This review examines smart wearable and implantable biosensors designed for continuous, real-time monitoring of biophysical and biochemical signals in the context of personalized and preventive healthcare. The paper surveys advances in flexible, stretchable, and biocompatible materials that allow long-term wear and seamless integration with the body, alongside multimodal sensing approaches that improve measurement robustness. It also explores how artificial intelligence contributes to signal processing and the generation of predictive health insights. The review identifies persistent challenges including motion artifacts, energy autonomy, data privacy, and the clinical interpretability of sensor outputs, framing these as key obstacles to broader adoption.

Extracellular Vesicles: A Comprehensive Review of Their Origins, Functions, and Therapeutic Potential

Madison Schank, Juan Zhao, L Wang et al.. Extracellular Vesicles: A Comprehensive Review of Their Origins, Functions, and Therapeutic Potential. Biomedicines. 2026

This review examines extracellular vesicles, with particular focus on the exosome subtype, covering their biological origins, functions, and emerging clinical applications. The authors synthesize current knowledge on how these membrane-bound particles are produced through the endosomal pathway, how they mediate intercellular communication by transporting diverse molecular cargoes, and how they can be isolated from biological fluids and cell cultures using methods such as ultracentrifugation, size-exclusion chromatography, and immunoaffinity capture. The review also surveys characterization techniques including nanoparticle tracking analysis, electron microscopy, and flow cytometry, which assess vesicle size, morphology, and surface markers. The authors highlight the therapeutic promise of extracellular vesicles as diagnostic biomarkers, drug delivery vehicles, and tools in regenerative medicine, while acknowledging persistent challenges in standardizing production, purification, and drug loading. They conclude that resolving these technical hurdles through robust, scalable protocols will be essential before extracellular vesicles can be successfully translated into routine clinical use.

An integrated electrochemical platform for low-volume biosensing

Fatma Kurul, Damla Aydoğan, Dogukan Topcu et al.. An integrated electrochemical platform for low-volume biosensing. npj Biosensing. 2026

This paper describes the development of a fully integrated electrochemical sensing platform designed to overcome limitations of conventional screen-printed electrode workflows in low-volume biosensing applications. The system combines a custom three-dimensional-printed flow cell, a microfluidic pumping module, and a graphical user interface that synchronizes instrument control, data acquisition, and on-board analysis. Using double-stranded DNA as a model analyte, the platform was evaluated through chronoamperometry under continuous flow, demonstrating a linear calibration range and comparable signal quality to manual pipette-based delivery while improving reproducibility and reducing baseline drift and hands-on time. The platform also supported limited electrode reuse through an electrochemical regeneration step, with signal quality diminishing progressively over successive cycles. The authors conclude that the system provides a compact, programmable chassis suitable for low-volume assays with potential adaptation to point-of-care and field-deployable contexts.

Biomaterials in personalized drug delivery: innovations, challenges, and future directions

Adna Hrapović, Nadia Islam, Asmaa Al Bourghli et al.. Biomaterials in personalized drug delivery: innovations, challenges, and future directions. PeerJ. 2026

This review examines the role of biomaterials in drug delivery, focusing on how advances in polymers, metals, ceramics, composites, and nanostructured carriers have transformed therapeutic design toward personalized medicine. The authors surveyed how tunable physicochemical properties—including degradation kinetics, surface chemistry, and mechanical stability—enable controlled drug release, protection of sensitive therapeutics, and targeted accumulation at disease sites through diffusion-controlled, degradation-mediated, or stimulus-responsive mechanisms. Nanostructured platforms such as liposomes, micelles, and dendrimers were highlighted for their capacity to improve drug solubility, cellular uptake, and site-specific delivery through surface functionalization and ligand-mediated targeting. The review identifies persistent barriers to clinical translation, including immune–biomaterial interactions, batch-to-batch manufacturing variability, unpredictable long-term biodegradation, and regulatory scalability constraints. The authors conclude that future progress requires precision fabrication methods, manufacturing-practice-compatible production, and biologically informed design strategies that accommodate patient-specific variability to realize the full potential of personalized drug delivery.

Analytical Performances of Polymer-Based Biosensors for Real Samples Application

Marcello Mascini, Sara Palmieri, Fabiola Eugelio et al.. Analytical Performances of Polymer-Based Biosensors for Real Samples Application. Biosensors. 2026

This review examined how polymer-based biosensors perform analytically when applied to complex real-world samples across food, environmental, and clinical contexts. The authors conducted a meta-trend analysis of nearly a hundred original research articles spanning a decade, evaluating four distinct polymer classes—conductive polymers, redox-mediator polymers, hydrogels, and molecularly imprinted polymers—against criteria including matrix interference, precision, stability, and validation completeness. Electrochemical detection was found to dominate the field, with each polymer class offering specific advantages, such as hydrogels preserving enzyme kinetics in protein-rich matrices and molecularly imprinted polymers enabling trace-level detection in harsh environments. However, the review identified serious validation gaps, as the vast majority of studies omitted limits of quantification and substantial fractions lacked reproducibility or precision reporting. The authors conclude that maintaining calibration integrity across different hostile sample matrices remains the central obstacle to commercial deployment, while emerging architectures such as nanocapsulation and microneedle-integrated systems present promising avenues to improve fouling resistance and operational stability.

Artificial Intelligence Applications in Medical Devices for Personalized Health Care Solutions: Systematic Review

Hemanth Ponnambalath Mohanadas, A Manikandan, Ahmad Fauzi Ismail et al.. Artificial Intelligence Applications in Medical Devices for Personalized Health Care Solutions: Systematic Review. Journal of Medical Internet Research. 2026

This systematic review examined the scope and impact of artificial intelligence integration in medical devices, with a focus on how AI enables personalized, precision health care. The authors conducted a comprehensive review of peer-reviewed articles, industry analyses, and regulatory advisories published over a seven-year period, analyzing current applications, implementation barriers, and strategies to overcome them. The review found substantial advances in AI-driven diagnostics, treatment personalization, wearable health monitoring, and smart prosthetics, with some diagnostic tools demonstrating high classification accuracy across disease categories. Key challenges identified include data security vulnerabilities, algorithmic bias, patient privacy concerns, and difficulties integrating AI systems into existing health care infrastructures, though strategies such as improved data validation, updated regulatory frameworks, and ethical guidelines were found to help mitigate these issues. The authors conclude that while AI-driven personalization holds transformative potential for patient-centered medicine, realizing this potential requires coordinated technical, ethical, and regulatory progress, with emerging technologies like quantum computing and blockchain-based data management identified as promising future directions.

CPI-Bench: A Comprehensive,Practical and Intelligent Benchmark for Real-World Image Editing

Qinye Zhou, Jun Zheng, Yongchao Du et al.. CPI-Bench: A Comprehensive,Practical and Intelligent Benchmark for Real-World Image Editing. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the limitations of existing image editing benchmarks, which are confined to simple single-image tasks with narrow coverage and poor ability to distinguish between models. The authors propose CPI-Bench, a new benchmark comprising three subsets designed to evaluate general editing across diverse tasks including multi-image editing, practical high-frequency real-user application scenarios, and demanding reasoning-based editing capabilities. The benchmark was used to evaluate mainstream image editing models, revealing meaningful performance differentiation that prior benchmarks failed to capture. The results provide quantification of gaps across general, practical, and reasoning-based editing dimensions, offering guidance for future model optimization. Notably, the benchmark's rankings showed the strongest alignment with a human preference leaderboard among evaluated benchmarks, suggesting it faithfully reflects real-world user experience and serves as a robust proxy for human perceptual judgment.

GPU implementation of mixed quantum-classical Liouville molecular dynamics without momentum jump

Koji Ando. GPU implementation of mixed quantum-classical Liouville molecular dynamics without momentum jump. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper describes a GPU-based implementation of mixed quantum-classical Liouville molecular dynamics using a momentum-jump-free theoretical framework. To optimize performance on the GPU architecture, the authors eliminated trajectory spawning, which had been used on CPU implementations to enhance sampling but would introduce costly thread divergence and dynamic memory allocation on parallel hardware. The resulting implementation achieved roughly an order-of-magnitude speedup compared to the CPU approach with spawning, while also exhibiting linear scaling with the number of sampling trajectories.

MagnifiQ: Patch-aware Text Guided Progressive Upscaling for High-Resolution Image Restoration

Mahesh Reddy, Yashesh Savani, Antoine Mercier et al.. MagnifiQ: Patch-aware Text Guided Progressive Upscaling for High-Resolution Image Restoration. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of restoring high-resolution images, particularly at 4K resolution, where diffusion-based methods suffer from computational expense and texture inconsistencies. The authors developed MagnifiQ, a framework that progressively upscales degraded images through multiple intermediate resolution stages rather than attempting direct restoration at the target resolution. Their approach adapts a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model by replacing self-attention layers with convolutional operations that scale linearly with resolution, and incorporates patch-specific text prompts to provide spatially localized semantic guidance during restoration. Testing on both synthetic and real-world degraded images demonstrated that MagnifiQ outperforms prior diffusion-based restoration methods in perceptual quality and human preference, producing sharper textures and more globally coherent results at 4K resolution.

Decoding the Past: An Uncertainty-Aware Deep Learning Framework for Sex Attribution in Prehistoric Hand Stencils

Karel Becerra, Boris Mederos, Dean Snow et al.. Decoding the Past: An Uncertainty-Aware Deep Learning Framework for Sex Attribution in Prehistoric Hand Stencils. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study addresses the long-standing challenge of determining the biological sex of individuals who created Upper Paleolithic hand stencils in ancient rock art, a problem complicated by the lack of ground truth, population variation, and image degradation over time. The researchers developed an uncertainty-aware deep learning framework that models and propagates uncertainty throughout the entire analytical pipeline, combining dual image processing, contour extraction, silhouette augmentation, and ensembles of two distinct deep neural network architectures trained on thousands of contemporary hand samples. Each prehistoric stencil was rendered into multiple plausible silhouette realizations to capture boundary ambiguity, and predictions were validated through a triangulated scheme incorporating unsupervised manifold mapping and explainable AI spatial attribution methods to ensure anatomical consistency. On contemporary hand data the ensemble models achieved strong classification accuracy, particularly in older age groups, and when applied to prehistoric stencils the framework produced sex attributions alongside confidence measures that distinguish morphologically stable cases from ambiguous ones. The authors conclude that uncertainty itself can be treated as a measurable and informative component of archaeological inference, enabling more robust and reproducible analysis of ancient rock art.

Joint Estimation of Sparse Multilayer Networks via Graph Limits

Youngseok Song, Sofia C. Olhede. Joint Estimation of Sparse Multilayer Networks via Graph Limits. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of jointly modeling multilayer network data, where multiple types of interactions occur among the same set of individuals. The authors represent multiplex networks using a nonparametric framework based on graph limits called scaled graphons, and develop a joint estimator called the multi-network histogram using blockmodel approximations that accounts for each layer's varying sparsity through shared latent variables. They derive theoretical guarantees for this estimator, including an upper bound on the weighted mean integrated squared error and the optimal bandwidth that minimizes it. A key finding is that jointly modeling layers reduces estimation error and allows for a smaller optimal bandwidth compared to single-layer approaches, enabling finer-resolution estimation even in sparser network layers. The method's practical value is demonstrated through simulations and an application to socioeconomic multiplex networks from an Indian village.

Finding Vulnerabilities via LLM-Augmented Semantics-Aware Type-Checking

Ruizhe Wang, Meng Xu, N. Asokan. Finding Vulnerabilities via LLM-Augmented Semantics-Aware Type-Checking. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of detecting software vulnerabilities through static analysis, arguing that traditional rule-based approaches rely too heavily on syntactic patterns while neglecting the semantic meaning embedded in code. The authors propose SETYPE, a semantics-aware type system in which types are derived from the natural language meanings of variable and function names, with both type inference and type checking performed by large language models. A failed type check in this framework signals a potential vulnerability, enabling detection without manually encoded security rules. The approach was prototyped for Python web applications and evaluated on real-world software, achieving high precision and accuracy. The system successfully identified numerous previously unknown zero-day vulnerabilities, the majority of which were subsequently confirmed by developers.

Trends of nucleic acid - based point-of-care diagnostics for infectious diseases.

Hatem H, Mysara M, Ramadan R. Trends of nucleic acid - based point-of-care diagnostics for infectious diseases.. Journal of biological engineering. 2026

This review paper examines the current landscape of nucleic acid-based point-of-care diagnostics for infectious diseases, motivated by the global need for rapid, decentralized testing outside conventional laboratory settings. The authors surveyed a broad range of platforms—including lateral flow assays, biochips, and biosensors—organized around the three core operational steps of nucleic acid extraction, amplification, and detection. For extraction, they evaluated approaches such as magnetic bead-based, paper-based, and integrated microfluidic methods with an emphasis on simplicity and equipment-free operation. Amplification strategies reviewed include both thermocycling methods like PCR and isothermal alternatives such as LAMP, RPA, and NASBA, all adapted for portable and low-power use. On the detection side, the review spans colorimetric and fluorescent assays through to advanced biosensor platforms exploiting CRISPR/Cas systems and toehold switch principles, which offer highly sensitive and programmable nucleic acid recognition.

Bridging the diagnostic gap: the role of culture methods in the era of syndromic panels for central nervous system infections.

Sberna G, D'Arezzo S, Valli MB et al.. Bridging the diagnostic gap: the role of culture methods in the era of syndromic panels for central nervous system infections.. Frontiers in cellular and infection microbiology. 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance of a syndromic molecular panel for central nervous system infections by comparing it against conventional cerebrospinal fluid culture at a national reference center over three years. Using a large dataset of paired molecular and culture results, the researchers calculated measures of diagnostic concordance, sensitivity, specificity, and predictive values across the full sample set and within the subset of pathogens specifically targeted by the panel. The syndromic panel demonstrated high specificity and a strong negative predictive value when restricted to its included bacterial targets, but its overall sensitivity was substantially lower, reflecting the limitations of its fixed pathogen coverage. Critically, conventional culture identified clinically important organisms not included in the panel, such as certain staphylococcal and streptococcal species as well as rare environmental pathogens, underscoring that the syndromic panel cannot serve as a standalone diagnostic tool. The authors conclude that optimal management of central nervous system infections requires systematic integration of both approaches, and advocate for next-generation multiplex platforms with broader target coverage guided by real-world epidemiological data.

Performance of the Xpert Xpress Strep A for detection of &lt;i&gt;Streptococcus pyogenes&lt;/i&gt; from pediatric pleural fluids.

Amaral G, Gadkar VJ, Almodahka A et al.. Performance of the Xpert Xpress Strep A for detection of &lt;i&gt;Streptococcus pyogenes&lt;/i&gt; from pediatric pleural fluids.. Microbiology spectrum. 2026

This study evaluated a rapid molecular diagnostic tool for detecting Group A *Streptococcus* (*Streptococcus pyogenes*) from pediatric pleural fluids, a clinical setting where accurate diagnosis is critical for patient management and public health contact tracing. The researchers compared the performance of the Xpert Xpress Strep A, a quantitative real-time PCR assay originally designed for throat swabs, against bacterial culture and a laboratory-developed PCR assay targeting a different gene, using a composite reference standard. Testing a convenience sample of pediatric pleural fluid specimens that included both positive and negative cases, the Xpert assay achieved perfect agreement with the composite reference standard and the laboratory-developed PCR, detecting all positive specimens, while culture alone identified only a minority of those same positive cases. The findings demonstrate that the Xpert assay is substantially more sensitive than culture for recovering *S. pyogenes* from pleural fluid, likely because many patients had received antibiotics prior to sample collection. The authors conclude that Xpert offers a rapid and highly accurate alternative for diagnosing invasive Group A streptococcal disease from sterile site specimens in pediatric patients, with faster turnaround times supporting timely clinical and public health decision-making.

Graphene oxide as smart sustainable nanomaterial: a versatile multifunctional material with transformative potential in advanced materials science research

This review paper examines graphene oxide, a two-dimensional carbon-based nanomaterial, as a versatile and sustainable platform for addressing challenges across energy, environmental, and healthcare sectors. The authors survey the synthesis chemistry and property modification strategies of graphene oxide, highlighting how its layered architecture enables surface functionalization, tunable band gap, high surface area, and adjustable hydrophobic or hydrophilic character across structural configurations ranging from zero to three dimensions. The review covers a broad range of applications, including adsorption and photocatalytic degradation of pollutants and microplastics, carbon dioxide photoreduction, photocatalytic hydrogen production, battery technologies, ultra-sensitive molecular detection via surface-enhanced Raman scattering, and healthcare platforms. The authors emphasize that graphene oxide can function effectively as a standalone material rather than solely as a composite component, underscoring its transformative and multifunctional potential. However, the review also acknowledges persistent challenges surrounding scalability, long-term stability, reproducibility, and health concerns, and proposes possible solutions to enable sustainable real-world and industrial deployment.

The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development

Emad M. Abdallah, Abdulrahman Mohammed Alhudhaibi, Mahmoud M. Dahab et al.. The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development. Frontiers in Pharmacology. 2026

This narrative review examines the global challenge of antimicrobial resistance with a focus on bacterial pathogens designated as priorities by the World Health Organization. The authors synthesize current understanding of the molecular mechanisms by which these pathogens evade existing antibiotics, including reduced membrane permeability, efflux pump overexpression, enzymatic inactivation, target modification, biofilm formation, and horizontal gene transfer. The review critically evaluates why conventional antibiotic discovery pipelines have failed to keep pace with resistance and assesses a range of emerging therapeutic strategies—such as artificial intelligence-assisted drug discovery, phage therapy, antimicrobial peptides, CRISPR-based systems, and natural product-derived compounds—while addressing their translational, regulatory, and clinical obstacles. The authors also emphasize that delivery challenges, toxicity, dosing optimization, and the risk of resistance emergence remain significant barriers for next-generation approaches. They conclude that combating priority-resistant pathogens will require integrated, multidisciplinary efforts spanning molecular biology, pharmacology, clinical translation, antimicrobial stewardship, and a One Health framework that connects human, animal, and environmental health sectors.

Fluorescence-Guided Chitosan and Eugenol-Based Carbon Dots for Comprehensive Infection Control and In Vitro Wound-Healing Applications in Dentistry

N. Narayanan, Aruchamy Mohanprasanth, Natesan Thirumalaivasan. Fluorescence-Guided Chitosan and Eugenol-Based Carbon Dots for Comprehensive Infection Control and In Vitro Wound-Healing Applications in Dentistry. Dentistry Journal. 2026

Researchers investigated chitosan–eugenol carbon dots as a multifunctional nanomaterial platform for dental applications, motivated by the need for materials that simultaneously control infection and support tissue regeneration. The carbon dots were synthesized from biocompatible chitosan and eugenol, then characterized for their physical and chemical properties using electron microscopy and elemental analysis, while their biological performance was assessed through antibacterial assays against the caries-associated pathogen Streptococcus mutans and cytocompatibility testing with fibroblast cell cultures. The resulting nanoparticles were spherical and nanoscale in size, with elemental analysis confirming successful incorporation of heteroatoms and surface functionalization. The material demonstrated concentration-dependent antibacterial activity against Streptococcus mutans and showed strong biocompatibility, with fibroblast cells maintaining viability, normal morphology, and enhanced migratory behavior indicative of wound-healing potential. The authors conclude that combining chitosan and eugenol within a carbon dot framework yields a promising multifunctional nanoplatform capable of addressing both oral infection control and tissue repair in dental therapeutic contexts.

Detection to Disruption: A Comprehensive Review of Bacterial Biofilms and Therapeutic Advances

Pranay Amruth Maroju, Angad S. Sidhu, Amogh R. Motaganahalli et al.. Detection to Disruption: A Comprehensive Review of Bacterial Biofilms and Therapeutic Advances. Antibiotics. 2026

This review examines bacterial biofilms — structured microbial communities encased in a self-produced extracellular polymeric substance matrix — focusing on their biology, clinical significance, and the therapeutic challenges they pose. The authors synthesize current knowledge on biofilm development stages, matrix composition, and the physiological heterogeneity that emerges during maturation, alongside the multiple resistance mechanisms that make biofilms difficult to eradicate, including restricted antibiotic diffusion, enzymatic inactivation, efflux pump upregulation, persister cell formation, and horizontal gene transfer. The review maps the clinical contexts in which biofilms are implicated, spanning cystic fibrosis, chronic wounds, osteomyelitis, implant-associated infections, and breast implant illness, where persistent biofilms are hypothesized to drive local and systemic inflammatory manifestations. Conventional and emerging detection methods, including real-time monitoring approaches, are also discussed alongside treatment strategies that pair antibiotics with matrix-disrupting enzymes, quorum-sensing inhibitors, bacteriophages, metabolic reactivators, and nanotechnology-based delivery systems. The authors conclude that effective management of biofilm-associated infections requires integrated strategies combining accurate detection with multifaceted therapies, and that advances in multi-omics and systems-level modeling will be critical for developing next-generation preventive and therapeutic approaches.

Blazar Boosted Dark Matter in IceCube

Alberto M. Gago, Jaime Hoefken Zink, Joel Jones-Pérez et al.. Blazar Boosted Dark Matter in IceCube. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether the IceCube neutrino detector can probe dark matter that has been accelerated to relativistic speeds by blazar jets, a mechanism that circumvents the usual sensitivity limitations of direct-detection experiments for light dark matter. The researchers computed the diffuse flux of boosted dark matter from a large sample of blazars, using proton spectra inferred from multiwavelength observations and conservative dark matter spike profiles around central supermassive black holes, while carefully accounting for propagation and attenuation effects. The dark matter–nucleon scattering cross section was modeled to include elastic, resonant single-pion production, and deep inelastic scattering contributions, with particular attention paid to smoothly bridging the transition between elastic and deep inelastic regimes. Applying this framework to IceCube neutrino data, the authors derived constraints on model parameter space and found that this approach can surpass conventional direct-detection experiments for dark matter masses below approximately one GeV. They further found that the signal is dominated by deep inelastic scattering and is therefore most sensitive to heavier mediators, while resonance processes moderately suppress the event rate near the detection threshold.

Cosmic Ray Diffusion and the Origin of Very High Energy Gamma-Ray Emission in Young Massive Stellar Clusters

Lucas Barreto-Mota, Elisabete M. de Gouveia Dal Pino, Gaetano Di Marco et al.. Cosmic Ray Diffusion and the Origin of Very High Energy Gamma-Ray Emission in Young Massive Stellar Clusters. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the origin of very high energy gamma-ray emission from young massive stellar clusters, focusing on how cosmic rays escape their acceleration sites and propagate through the surrounding medium. The authors developed a theoretical framework showing that mirror diffusion combined with pitch-angle scattering in turbulent magnetic fields produces an energy-dependent diffusion coefficient proportional to the cube root of energy, which suppresses cosmic-ray transport compared to standard diffusion. They coupled three-dimensional magnetohydrodynamic simulations of a young massive stellar cluster with Monte Carlo cosmic-ray propagation calculations, using the cluster W43 as a benchmark and testing both a central point source and a spherical shell injection geometry. They found that this suppressed diffusion regime, combined with a standard cosmic-ray injection spectrum, successfully reproduces the gamma-ray spectra observed by Fermi and LHAASO, whereas stronger energy-dependent diffusion requires an unusually hard injection spectrum to match the data. A key conclusion is that the observed gamma-ray emission is determined primarily by the diffusive propagation regime rather than by where within the cluster the cosmic rays are injected, with the relative contributions of leptonic and hadronic emission processes also depending sensitively on the diffusion model.

GPU implementation of mixed quantum-classical Liouville molecular dynamics without momentum jump

Koji Ando. GPU implementation of mixed quantum-classical Liouville molecular dynamics without momentum jump. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper describes a GPU-based implementation of mixed quantum-classical Liouville molecular dynamics using a momentum-jump-free theoretical framework. To optimize performance on GPU hardware, the authors removed the trajectory spawning mechanism that had previously been used on CPU to enhance sampling, thereby avoiding thread divergence and dynamic memory allocation overhead. This redesign achieved a speedup of an order of magnitude relative to CPU calculations that include spawning, and the approach scales linearly with the number of sampling trajectories.

MagnifiQ: Patch-aware Text Guided Progressive Upscaling for High-Resolution Image Restoration

Mahesh Reddy, Yashesh Savani, Antoine Mercier et al.. MagnifiQ: Patch-aware Text Guided Progressive Upscaling for High-Resolution Image Restoration. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of restoring high-resolution images, particularly at 4K resolution, where diffusion-based methods suffer from computational expense and tend to produce repeated or inconsistent textures. The authors introduce MagnifiQ, a framework that progressively upscales degraded images through multiple intermediate resolution stages rather than attempting direct high-resolution restoration in a single step. A remark on the full support property

Yiran Cheng. A remark on the full support property. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper studies stability conditions on projective schemes over a field, focusing on the support property, which is a key technical condition in the theory of Bridgeland stability conditions. The authors prove that a mass-upper-bound condition called a mass-Hom bound for a numerical pre-stability condition implies the support property with respect to the full numerical Grothendieck group, rather than just a quotient of it. By combining this result with recent constructions of stability conditions on projective schemes, they establish the existence of stability conditions satisfying the full support property on every projective scheme over a field, and extend this to a uniform statement in the relative setting. Additionally, they show that the distinguished connected component of the full numerical stability manifold does not depend on the choice of polarization, yielding a canonical component intrinsic to the scheme.

Decoding the Past: An Uncertainty-Aware Deep Learning Framework for Sex Attribution in Prehistoric Hand Stencils

Karel Becerra, Boris Mederos, Dean Snow et al.. Decoding the Past: An Uncertainty-Aware Deep Learning Framework for Sex Attribution in Prehistoric Hand Stencils. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the longstanding challenge of determining the biological sex of individuals who created Upper Paleolithic hand stencils in prehistoric rock art, a problem complicated by the lack of ground truth, population variation, and image degradation over time. The researchers developed an uncertainty-aware deep learning framework that combines dual image processing, contour extraction, silhouette augmentation, and ensembles of two different neural network architectures trained on a large dataset of contemporary hand samples. The pipeline generates multiple silhouette realizations per stencil to capture boundary uncertainties, and integrates ensemble predictions with unsupervised dimensionality reduction and explainable AI techniques to validate anatomical consistency. Applied to contemporary data, the ensemble models achieved strong classification accuracy, particularly in older age groups, and when deployed on prehistoric stencils the framework produced both sex attributions and quantified confidence measures that distinguish morphologically stable from ambiguous cases. Innovative epitope-based ELISA assay for detecting Group B Streptococcus carriage in pregnant women.

Brzychczy-Włoch M, Dobrut A, Drożdż K et al.. Innovative epitope-based ELISA assay for detecting Group B Streptococcus carriage in pregnant women.. Applied microbiology and biotechnology. 2026

This study investigated whether synthetic peptide epitopes derived from *Streptococcus agalactiae* surface proteins could serve as the basis for an ELISA-based immunodiagnostic test for Group B Streptococcus carriage in pregnant women. Three epitopes from the bacterial proteins GroEL, enolase, and EF-Tu were selected and tested against serum samples from GBS-positive and GBS-negative individuals using IgG, IgM, and IgA antibody detection. All three peptides, whether used individually or in combination, significantly distinguished between GBS-positive and GBS-negative sera, with the enolase- and EF-Tu-derived epitopes performing best as individual candidates due to their higher immunoreactivity at lower concentrations. Combining epitopes in various mixtures further enhanced immunoreactivity compared to single-peptide assays. The authors conclude that this epitope-based ELISA represents a promising and potentially pioneering alternative to the current culture-based gold standard, which is slow and can yield ambiguous results.

Performance of the LIAISON PLEX gram-positive blood culture assay for identifying bacterial pathogens and resistance genes in blood cultures.

Chow S-K, Stevig C, Granato P et al.. Performance of the LIAISON PLEX gram-positive blood culture assay for identifying bacterial pathogens and resistance genes in blood cultures.. Journal of clinical microbiology. 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance of the LIAISON PLEX Gram-Positive Blood Culture Assay, a multiplexed molecular test designed to rapidly identify gram-positive bacterial pathogens and antibiotic resistance markers directly from positive blood culture bottles. The assay was assessed across prospective, pre-selected, and contrived blood culture samples in a multisite design, testing its ability to detect and differentiate thirteen gram-positive bacterial targets and four antibiotic resistance markers. The assay demonstrated a very high valid rate and achieved strong overall sensitivity and specificity across all targets, with robust inclusivity results and minimal cross-reactivity among off-panel species. Performance was maintained in the presence of potential interfering substances, in polymicrobial infection scenarios, and across blood culture bottles from multiple manufacturers. The authors conclude that the assay offers fast, accurate, and reliable detection of gram-positive organisms and their resistance genes, with potential to support antibiotic optimization and patient management in bloodstream infection care.

Validation of an in-house developed rapid assay for Group B Streptococcus detection in pregnancy.

Hu S, Xie J, Liu L et al.. Validation of an in-house developed rapid assay for Group B Streptococcus detection in pregnancy.. International journal of medical microbiology : IJMM. 2026

This study addressed the need for faster and simpler detection of Group B Streptococcus colonization in pregnant women, a key risk factor for neonatal infections and preterm stillbirth. The researchers developed and validated an in-house rapid diagnostic assay combining CRISPR/Cas12a technology with recombinase polymerase amplification, an isothermal method that does not require thermocycling equipment. Specificity was tested against other common reproductive tract pathogens, and the detection limit was established using serially diluted plasmids, with clinical performance evaluated using vaginal-rectal swabs from pregnant women compared against a commercial quantitative real-time PCR method. The assay completed detection within one hour, with results readable either visually via colloidal gold test strips or through a fluorescence reader, and it accurately distinguished Group B Streptococcus from non-target pathogens. Clinical evaluation demonstrated that the rapid assay results correlated closely with conventional PCR, supporting its potential as a high-sensitivity, high-specificity alternative for point-of-care prenatal screening.

Utility and clinical relevance of multiplex PCR panels for diagnosis of central nervous system infections.

Gürler M, Karaoğlu Ç, Yurtcu EE et al.. Utility and clinical relevance of multiplex PCR panels for diagnosis of central nervous system infections.. The Indian journal of medical research. 2026

This study evaluated the real-world diagnostic performance and clinical utility of multiplex polymerase chain reaction panels designed to detect multiple pathogens simultaneously from cerebrospinal fluid in patients with suspected meningitis or encephalitis. Researchers retrospectively reviewed five years of testing data at a tertiary care hospital, examining pathogen detection rates, co-detection frequencies, repeat testing yield, and whether positive results led to documented changes in diagnosis or treatment. Viral pathogens dominated the positive findings, with bacterial detections also present, and co-detections occurred in a notable proportion of positive cases. Despite the technology's capacity for rapid, broad pathogen identification, fewer than half of positive results were associated with a clinically meaningful action, and repeat testing after an initial negative result offered little additional diagnostic value. The authors conclude that while these panels hold genuine diagnostic potential for central nervous system infections, their clinical impact is undermined by inappropriate test ordering and insufficient clinician familiarity with panel content, calling for clearer testing criteria and better-targeted use.

Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.

Walzl G, McHugh TD, Chakaya J et al.. Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.. The Lancet. Microbe. 2027

This paper reviewed advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarker research from roughly the first half of the 2020s, synthesizing evidence to inform updated WHO policy guidance on detection of tuberculosis disease and drug resistance. The review covers a broad range of technologies and approaches, including digital chest radiography supported by computer-aided detection as a frontline screening tool, expanded deployment of rapid molecular diagnostics such as low-complexity automated and manual nucleic acid amplification tests, novel specimen types including tongue swabs and stool, and emerging near point-of-care platforms. Molecular drug-resistance testing has also advanced, with targeted next-generation sequencing now recognized for comprehensive drug-susceptibility profiling at reference laboratory level, while urine lipoarabinomannan assays improve diagnostic yield in people with HIV and advanced disease despite performance limitations. Host blood transcriptomic and other biomarker signatures show promise for triage and prognosis but no candidate has yet met WHO target product profile criteria for a point-of-care test capable of reliably distinguishing infection from disease, including subclinical tuberculosis. The authors conclude that despite meaningful progress, substantial gaps in performance, implementation, and equity remain, underscoring ongoing developmental priorities.

Development of a microfluidic isothermal amplification nucleic acid detection (mFIND) platform for simultaneous detection of respiratory pathogens.

He P, Feng Y, Zhou X et al.. Development of a microfluidic isothermal amplification nucleic acid detection (mFIND) platform for simultaneous detection of respiratory pathogens.. Biosensors & bioelectronics. 2026

This paper describes the development and validation of a centrifugal microfluidic platform designed for simultaneous point-of-care detection of multiple respiratory pathogens, including influenza A and B, SARS-CoV-2, and RSV. The system integrates lyophilized reagents into independent reaction chambers on a chip, paired with a portable analyzer that automates sample handling, isothermal amplification, and real-time fluorescence detection in a two-step workflow. The platform achieved extremely low limits of detection with no cross-reactivity against a broad panel of non-target pathogens, and clinical validation using a large set of positive samples demonstrated complete agreement with quantitative PCR. The entire detection process is completed within fifteen minutes using very small reaction volumes, making the system well suited for decentralized testing environments.

Development and evaluation of a rapid duplex qPCR assay for Treponema pallidum using the GeneSoC microfluidic platform.

Kawahata T, Banno F, Hama M. Development and evaluation of a rapid duplex qPCR assay for Treponema pallidum using the GeneSoC microfluidic platform.. Diagnostic microbiology and infectious disease. 2026

The paper studied the development and evaluation of a rapid molecular diagnostic assay for detecting Treponema pallidum, the causative agent of syphilis, with an emphasis on suitability for point-of-care testing. The researchers designed a duplex quantitative PCR assay targeting two genomic regions and implemented it on the GeneSoC microfluidic platform, testing it against DNA standards, a reference strain, and clinical specimens including lesion swabs and urine, both with and without prior DNA extraction. The assay demonstrated good linearity and amplification efficiency across the tested concentration range, with no cross-reactivity against other pathogens and qualitative agreement with a conventional comparator assay. A key finding was that detection could be completed within fifteen minutes directly from clinical specimens without DNA extraction, substantially faster than the comparator system, owing to the microfluidic platform's superior thermal cycling speed. The authors note that while extraction-free testing is feasible, DNA extraction may enhance sensitivity when target DNA is present in low quantities.

Trends of nucleic acid - based point-of-care diagnostics for infectious diseases.

Hatem H, Mysara M, Ramadan R. Trends of nucleic acid - based point-of-care diagnostics for infectious diseases.. Journal of biological engineering. 2026

This review examines the landscape of nucleic acid-based point-of-care diagnostics for infectious diseases, motivated by the need for decentralized, rapid, and accessible testing modalities. The paper systematically organizes the field around three core operational steps: nucleic acid extraction, amplification, and detection, covering a wide range of platforms including lateral flow devices, biochips, and biosensors. Extraction strategies discussed include magnetic bead-based, paper-based, and integrated microfluidic approaches, with emphasis on simplicity and equipment-free operation, while amplification methods such as PCR, NASBA, RPA, and LAMP are reviewed for their adaptation to portable and low-power conditions. Detection platforms are surveyed from conventional colorimetric and fluorescent assays to advanced biosensors incorporating CRISPR/Cas systems and toehold switch principles, which provide highly sensitive and programmable nucleic acid recognition. The review concludes that these combined technological advances are driving point-of-care diagnostics toward sensitive, selective, and economically viable infectious disease testing outside traditional laboratory settings.

A Novel Voltammetric Point-of-Care Device for Rapid and Accurate Nucleic Acid and Viral Pathogen Detection.

Munita R, Lyttleton R, Danefur E et al.. A Novel Voltammetric Point-of-Care Device for Rapid and Accurate Nucleic Acid and Viral Pathogen Detection.. ACS omega. 2026

This paper developed an electrochemical nucleic acid amplification test that combines isothermal amplification with real-time voltammetric detection for diagnosing infectious diseases at the point of care. The approach works by monitoring the proton release that accompanies nucleic acid amplification using a pH-sensing redox compound, addressing optical detection limitations common to existing isothermal methods like LAMP. The system was evaluated against RT-qPCR across multiple respiratory pathogens including SARS-CoV-2, influenza A and B viruses, and RSV A, achieving high concordance with the reference method in purified RNA from clinical samples. A proof-of-concept extraction-free workflow using equine nasal swabs correctly classified all samples for a equine herpesvirus relative to qPCR. The authors conclude that real-time voltammetric LAMP is feasible across diverse sample types and pathogens, though further validation in additional sample matrices and prospective cohorts is needed.

Isothermal nucleic acid amplification techniques: A comprehensive overview on their principle &amp; applications as alternative to PCR.

Amare Amdiyee A, Sisay Tessema T. Isothermal nucleic acid amplification techniques: A comprehensive overview on their principle &amp; applications as alternative to PCR.. Practical laboratory medicine. 2026

This review paper examined isothermal nucleic acid amplification methods as alternatives to conventional PCR-based diagnostics. The authors surveyed the principles, detection strategies, and applications of the most widely used techniques, including loop-mediated isothermal amplification, helicase-dependent amplification, rolling circle amplification, strand displacement amplification, nucleic acid sequence-based amplification, and recombinase polymerase amplification. Unlike PCR, these methods operate at a constant temperature and do not require thermal cycling equipment, making them particularly suited to resource-limited settings that demand rapid, affordable, and simple diagnostic tools. The review synthesizes information on reaction mechanisms, product detection approaches, and disease diagnostic applications into a single comprehensive resource, addressing a gap in the existing literature where no unified overview had previously covered all these aspects together. The authors also discuss the future prospects of isothermal amplification methods and their potential to broaden access to genomic testing across research and clinical contexts.

Looking ahead, the authors advocate for self-powered biosensors, modular pathogen detection panels, blockchain integration, and standardized validation pipelines as pathways toward next-generation, regulation-ready food safety monitoring platforms.
